# Federer 2.7: Invariant measures (pp. 121–141)

[Documentation](../toc.md) · [Item map](../contents.md) · [Index](../index.md) · [Symbols](../symbols.md) · [Divergences](../divergences.md) · [Bibliography](../bibliography.md)

## Overview

Source numbering and printed pages refer to
[Federer1969](../bibliography.md#federer1969) and corrections to
[Federer1987Erratum](../bibliography.md#federer1987erratum); see the
[bibliography](../bibliography.md) for source editions.

Part of the [Chapter 2 index](../toc.md). Each entry records the formalised assertions of one numbered item, their Rocq names, hypotheses, corrections, and sources. The divergences from the printed text are listed in each entry and in [divergences.md](../divergences.md).

## Numbered items

<a id="item-2-7-1"></a>

### 2.7.1 — Homogeneous spaces and covariant integrals

Federer, pp. 121–122. Every assertion of 2.7.1 is proved. The abbreviations
u o A_g = u_g and A(g,x) = gx are notation only and are not introduced. The
standing hypotheses (G a locally compact Hausdorff topological group, X a
locally compact Hausdorff space) are not built into the definitions; each
result assumes only the parts it uses, listed below.

*Topological groups and homogeneous spaces.*

- [`TopologicalGroupUP.v`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v) specifies a group by predicates on its operations
  [`mul`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v#L22), [`inv`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v#L27) and identity `e`: [`is_group_laws`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L24) (associativity, left
  identity, left inverses), [`is_topological_group`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L39) (the group laws,
  continuity of (g,h) |-> gh on the product space G x G, continuity of
  g |-> g^{-1}), [`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L56) (Federer's standing
  hypothesis), [`is_subgroup`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L73) and [`left_coset`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L88) (g S). MathComp has no
  structure of a noncommutative infinite group.
  [`TopologicalGroupLaws.v`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupLaws.v) derives the two-sided laws, cancellation,
  (gh)^{-1} = h^{-1}g^{-1}, [`left_coset_eq`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupLaws.v#L97) (g S = h S iff g^{-1}h in S, for a
  subgroup S), and shows that translations and inversion are homeomorphisms.
- [`HomeomorphismUP.v`](../../theories/HomeomorphismUP.v): [`is_open_map`](../../theories/HomeomorphismUP.v#L17), `is_homeomorphism_with_inverse f h`
  (f, h continuous and mutually inverse), `is_homeomorphism f` (some inverse
  exists) and [`open_continuous_bijection_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HomeomorphismUP.v#L89).
- [`HomogeneousSpaceUP.v`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v): `orbit_map A x` is A^x, `isotropy_subgroup A x`
  is G^x, and `is_homogeneous_space mul A` records exactly the four printed
  conditions: A continuous on G x X, A(gh,x) = A(g,A(h,x)), each A^x open, and
  im A^x = X. The action is curried, so `A g` is A_g. A(e,x) = x is not
  assumed.
- [`HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v), for [`is_group_laws`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L24) and [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33):
  [`homogeneous_unit_identity`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L45) (A_e = 1_X),
  [`homogeneous_translation_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L78) (A_g is a homeomorphism with inverse
  A_{g^{-1}}), [`isotropy_subgroup_subgroup`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L110), and [`isotropy_subgroup_closed`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L122)
  (G^x is closed when X is Hausdorff).

*The coset spaces G/S.*

- [`CosetSpaceUP.v`](../../theories/CosetSpaceUP.v): `is_left_coset_space mul inv S pi` specifies G/S by its
  projection: pi is onto, pi g = pi h iff g^{-1}h in S, and U is open iff
  pi^{-1}(U) is open.
- [`CosetSpaceLaws.v`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v): [`coset_projection_fibre`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L40) (the fibre of pi through g is
  the coset g S), [`coset_space_universal_property`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L93) (a continuous map constant
  on cosets is f' o pi for exactly one map f', which is continuous), and, for a
  topological group, [`coset_projection_open`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L132), [`coset_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L145) (S
  closed) and [`coset_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L172) (S closed, G locally compact).
- Existence: `CosetSpaceConstruction` (construction module; MathComp
  generic quotient by g S = h S with `quotient_topology`), sealed in
  `CosetSpaceSealed` (module `LeftCosetSpace`); the interface
  [`CosetSpace.v`](../../theories/CosetSpace.v) exposes `coset_space mul S`, `coset_projection mul S` and
  [`coset_space_specification`](../../theories/CosetSpace.v#L26) for every subgroup S of a group.
- [`HomogeneousSpaceCosets.v`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceCosets.v): [`isotropy_quotient_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceCosets.v#L31): for every
  space Q with projection pi specified as G/G^x, there is exactly one map
  phi : Q -> X with phi(pi g) = A(g,x), and phi is a homeomorphism.
  [`isotropy_coset_space_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceCosets.v#L63) states this for the sealed G/G^x. Only
  the group laws and [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33) are assumed.
- [`CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v): `is_coset_action mul pi A` is the law
  A(g, pi h) = pi(gh); [`coset_action_exists`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L36) and [`coset_action_unique`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L46).
  For a topological group, [`coset_action_homogeneous`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L100) and
  [`closed_subgroup_coset_homogeneous`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L126): for a closed subgroup S of a locally
  compact group, G/S is Hausdorff, locally compact and homogeneous through A,
  and `isotropy_subgroup A (pi e) = S` (Federer's G^S = S).
  [`closed_subgroup_isotropy`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L145): every closed subgroup is G^x for some locally
  compact Hausdorff homogeneous space X of G.

*Covariant integrals and measures.* K(X) is
[`compactly_supported_continuous_functions`](../../theories/CompactSupportFunctionUP.v#L21) of 2.5.13.

- [`CovariantIntegralUP.v`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v): `is_covariant_on L A chi mu` (mu(u o A_g) =
  chi(g) mu(u) for u in L), `is_covariant_integral A chi mu` (a Daniell integral
  on K(X) satisfying it on K(X)), [`is_invariant_integral`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v#L52) (chi = 1),
  `is_covariant_measure A chi phi` (phi is the K(X) regular measure
  representing some monotone Daniell integral on K(X) that is chi covariant),
  [`is_invariant_measure`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v#L73).
- [`CovariantIntegralLaws.v`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v), for [`is_group_laws`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L24) and
  [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33): A_g is proper and u o A_g is in K(X) for u in K(X).
  [`covariant_pushforward`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L72) and [`covariant_integral_pushforward`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L91): chi covariance
  is equivalent to A_g#(mu) = chi(g) mu on K(X), with A_g# the
  [`daniell_pushforward`](../../theories/DaniellSpacePushforward.v#L55) of 2.5.19. For mu(u) <> 0, the lemmas
  [`covariant_character_unit`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L118), [`covariant_character_multiplicative`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L130),
  [`covariant_character_nonzero`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L144) and [`covariant_character_inverse`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L154).
  [`covariant_variation_integral`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L172): if mu is chi covariant and P, Q satisfy the
  supremum laws of mu+ and mu- (2.5.5), then P + Q is |chi| covariant on K(X)+.
  [`covariant_measure_exists`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L226): the K(X) regular measure associated with a
  monotone chi covariant integral exists, is chi covariant, and is unique.
- [`CovariantCharacterContinuity.v`](../../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v), for a topological group G that is
  locally compact and a locally compact Hausdorff space X:
  [`covariant_character_continuous`](../../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v#L58) and [`covariant_character_homomorphism`](../../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v#L116)
  (if mu(u) <> 0 for some u in K(X), then chi(gh) = chi(g)chi(h), chi(e) = 1,
  chi(g) <> 0, and chi is continuous for the real-line topology).

Five regressions: a one-point homogeneous space with isotropy subgroup G; a
constant action on the discrete two-point space that is continuous,
compatible and open but has A(e,false) <> false, and so is not homogeneous;
the zero integral is chi covariant for chi = 0, so the hypothesis mu <> 0 is
needed; G/G is a single point; G -> G/{e} is injective.

Sources: Federer 2.7.1, pp. 121–122, with 2.5.5–2.5.6, pp. 96–98, 2.5.13, p.
106, and 2.5.19, p. 113. The erratum has no entry for pp. 121–122.

**Divergences.**

- *Topological group (p. 121).* Federer names topological groups without
  axioms. The predicates take the one-sided group axioms and continuity of the
  multiplication on G x G and of the inversion; the two-sided laws are
  derived. This is an interpretation, since MathComp provides no such
  structure.
- *Homogeneous space (p. 121).* The action is curried (`A g x` for A(g,x)),
  with continuity of the uncurried map on the product space. The standing
  hypotheses are separate from [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33). The identity A_e = 1_X,
  the homeomorphisms A_g and the homeomorphism G/G^x ~ X are proved without
  local compactness or the Hausdorff property. The closedness of G^x uses only
  that X is Hausdorff. This is a generalisation.
- *Identity A_e = 1_X (p. 121).* Federer writes "It follows" and gives no
  proof. Choose g with A(g,x) = x, which exists since im A^x = X; then A(e,x)
  = A(eg,x) = A(g,x) = x. The regression [`constant_action_not_unital_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L5763)
  shows that im A^x = X is needed.
- *G/S (p. 122).* Federer uses the coset space without describing it. It is
  specified by its projection; its points correspond to cosets through
  [`coset_projection_fibre`](../../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L40). Existence uses the MathComp generic quotient and
  `quotient_topology`; this is an external construction, sealed, and Federer
  gives no construction to follow. The universal property, openness of pi, and
  the Hausdorff property and local compactness of G/S are supporting results
  that Federer uses tacitly.
- *G/G^x ~ X (p. 122).* Uniqueness of the induced map is added. The statement
  holds for every space with the specification of G/G^x.
- *Closed subgroups (p. 122).* The action is specified by the printed formula
  A(g, hS) = ghS as a predicate ([`is_coset_action`](../../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L24)), with existence and
  uniqueness proved. Federer's point S of G/S is pi(e). The Hausdorff property
  of G is not needed; local compactness of G is used for that of G/S.
- *Character (p. 122).* The hypothesis mu <> 0 means mu(u) <> 0 for some u in
  K(X). The phrase "into R ~ {0}" is stated as chi(g) <> 0 together with
  continuity into R for the topology of `real_line_metric R`. This is
  equivalent to continuity into the subspace R ~ {0}. Federer gives no proof
  of continuity. Our proof chooses a compact neighbourhood N of g_0, bounds
  the supports of the u o A_g with g in N by the compact set {A(h^{-1},y) : h
  in N, y in spt u} (continuity of the inversion is used here), and combines
  the fixed-support bound of 2.5.13 ([`compact_daniell_fixed_support_bound`](../../theories/CompactDaniellUniformBound.v#L58))
  with local uniform continuity of (x,g) |-> u(A(g,x))
  ([`compact_sections_locally_uniform`](../../theories/CompactSectionUniform.v#L19)). The algebraic laws use only the group
  laws and the action.
- *Push-forward A_g# (p. 122).* Elements of K(X)* are functionals on K(X). The
  equality A_g#(mu) = chi(g) mu is therefore stated on K(X); the values of the
  total functionals outside K(X) are irrelevant. Properness of A_g, needed by
  2.5.19, is proved ([`homogeneous_translation_proper`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L42)).
- *Variation (p. 122).* The variations mu+ and mu- are functions on K(X)+
  (2.5.5), so "mu+ + mu- is |chi| covariant" is the identity (mu+ + mu-)(u o
  A_g) = |chi(g)| (mu+ + mu-)(u) for u in K(X)+. The proof follows Federer's
  reference to 2.5.6, using its formula mu+(f) + mu-(f) = sup{|mu(h)| : h in
  K(X), |h| <= f}.
- *Covariant measures (p. 122).* "The K(X) regular measure associated with" mu
  is formalised as the K(X) regular measure representing mu. By 2.5.3 it
  exists and is unique ([`covariant_measure_exists`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L226)).

<a id="item-2-7-2"></a>

### 2.7.2 — Left and right multiplication; Haar integrals

Federer, pp. 122–123. Every assertion of 2.7.2 is proved. The actions are
curried as in 2.7.1, so the action applied to g is A_g.

- [`HaarIntegralUP.v`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v) contains the definitions. `left_multiplication_action mul`
  is A(g,x) = gx and `right_multiplication_action mul inv` is A(g,x) = xg^{-1}.
  [`is_left_covariant_integral`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L90), [`is_left_invariant_integral`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L95),
  [`is_left_covariant_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L100) and [`is_left_invariant_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L105), together with
  their `right` counterparts, are the notions of 2.7.1 for these actions.
  `is_nonzero_monotone_invariant_integral A mu` is an invariant integral for an
  arbitrary action A which is a monotone Daniell integral on K(X) with
  mu(u) <> 0 for some u in K(X). The phrase recurs in 2.7.4.
  `is_nonzero_monotone_invariant_measure A phi` says that phi is the K(X)
  regular measure associated with such an integral. [`is_left_haar_integral`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L111),
  [`is_right_haar_integral`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L145), [`is_left_haar_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L118) and [`is_right_haar_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L152)
  are these notions for the two actions.
- [`GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v`](../../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v): [`left_multiplication_homogeneous`](../../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v#L46) and
  [`right_multiplication_homogeneous`](../../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v#L93) show that G is a homogeneous space of
  itself, in the sense of [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33), through either action. The
  hypothesis is only `is_topological_group mul inv e`. The lemmas
  [`left_multiplication_translation`](../../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v#L30) and [`right_multiplication_translation`](../../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v#L36)
  record that A_g is left multiplication by g, respectively right
  multiplication by g^{-1}.
- [`EquivariantPushforward.v`](../../theories/EquivariantPushforward.v) treats actions A on X and B on Y and a
  homeomorphism p: X -> Y with inverse q such that p(A_g x) = B_g(p x).
  [`equivariant_covariant_integral`](../../theories/EquivariantPushforward.v#L161) shows that mu is a chi covariant integral
  for A if and only if p#(mu) = `daniell_pushforward p mu` is one for B.
  [`equivariant_nonzero_invariant_integral`](../../theories/EquivariantPushforward.v#L176) is the same statement for nonzero
  monotone invariant integrals. The supporting lemma
  [`homeomorphism_with_inverse_proper`](../../theories/EquivariantPushforward.v#L29) shows that homeomorphisms are proper. No
  group structure is used.
- [`HaarInversion.v`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v), for a topological group, with p = inv.
  [`inversion_commutative_diagram`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L39) is the printed diagram
  p o (left multiplication by g) = (right multiplication by g^{-1}) o p.
  [`left_covariant_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L65) is Federer's statement: mu is a left chi covariant
  integral if and only if p#(mu) is a right chi covariant integral.
  [`right_covariant_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L74) is the statement with the two actions
  exchanged. [`left_invariant_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L82) is the case chi = 1, and
  [`left_haar_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L90) and [`right_haar_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L98) are the statements for Haar
  integrals.
- [`HaarIntegralLaws.v`](../../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v), for an arbitrary action.
  [`nonzero_invariant_measure_invariant`](../../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L37) shows that a Haar measure is an
  invariant measure. [`nonzero_invariant_measure_nonzero`](../../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L50) shows that it is not
  the zero measure. [`nonzero_invariant_measure_exists`](../../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L66) shows that every Haar
  integral has exactly one associated K(X) regular measure.

Two regressions. The zero functional is a left invariant integral but not a
left Haar integral. On the one-point group, evaluation at the point is both a
left and a right Haar integral.

Sources: Federer 2.7.2, pp. 122–123, with 2.5.19, p. 113, and 2.7.1, pp.
121–122. The erratum has no entry for pp. 122–123.

**Divergences.**

- *The two actions are homogeneous (p. 122).* Federer calls the two actions
  "special cases" of 2.7.1 without proof. Both are proved to make G a
  homogeneous space for every topological group, without local compactness
  or the Hausdorff property. This is a supporting result and a
  generalisation.
- *Nonzero integrals (p. 122).* "Nonzero" means that mu(u) <> 0 for some u in
  K(G), as in 2.7.1.
- *Haar measures (p. 122).* "A nonzero monotone left invariant ... (measure)"
  is read as the K(G) regular measure associated with a left Haar integral,
  following the definition of invariant measures in 2.7.1. This is an
  interpretation. The literal reading, an invariant measure that is not the
  zero measure, is implied by [`nonzero_invariant_measure_invariant`](../../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L37) and
  [`nonzero_invariant_measure_nonzero`](../../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L50). The converse is not claimed. On a space
  that is not a countable union of compact sets, the K(X) regular measure
  associated with the zero integral need not vanish. An example is the
  measure on an uncountable discrete group that is 0 on countable sets and
  infinite on the others. This observation is not formalised.
- *Relation by p (p. 123).* Federer writes "We see" and gives no proof. The
  proof is the general transport lemma for equivariant homeomorphisms,
  applied to p = inv. The converse direction uses q#(p#(mu)) = mu. Properness
  of p, which 2.5.19 requires, is proved. The case with the actions
  exchanged and the versions for invariant and Haar integrals are added; they
  follow in the same way. Only the group laws and the continuity of the
  inversion are used.
- *Diagram (p. 123).* The diagram is stated as an equality of functions G -> G.

<a id="item-2-7-3"></a>

### 2.7.3 — Uniform actions

Federer, p. 123. Both assertions of 2.7.3 are proved. The second requires X to
be nonempty, as recorded below.

- [`UniformActionUP.v`](../../theories/UniformActionUP.v) contains the definitions.
  `is_uniformity E` gives the entourage laws for a family E of subsets of
  X x X: E is a filter, each member contains the diagonal, E is closed under
  inversion of relations, and each member contains D o D for some member D.
  These are the fields of MathComp's factory `Nbhs_isUniform`.
  `uniformity_induces_topology E` says that nbhs x = `nbhs_` E x for every x.
  `is_uniformity_basis E B` says that B is a subfamily of E and every member
  of E contains a member of B. `is_invariant_entourage_family A B` is the
  printed invariance: (x,y) in beta if and only if (gx,gy) in beta.
  `acts_uniformly A` says that some uniformity induces the topology of X and
  has an invariant basis. `uniform_action_condition A` is the printed
  condition on neighbourhoods V and W. `orbit_entourage A V` is the union of
  the sets A_g(V) x A_g(V) over g in G, and `orbit_entourage_family A` is the
  family of these sets for all nonempty open V. [`uniform_type_uniformity`](../../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L104)
  shows that the entourages of every MathComp `uniformType` satisfy the
  predicates.
- [`UniformActionLaws.v`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v). [`acts_uniformly_condition`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L48) proves the necessity of
  the condition for an arbitrary map A, with no hypothesis on it.
  [`acts_uniformly_iff`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L305) proves Federer's equivalence, assuming
  `is_group_laws mul inv e` and `is_homogeneous_space mul A`.
  [`uniform_action_orbit_basis`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L262) adds the hypothesis that X is nonempty. Under
  the condition, the sets containing some `orbit_entourage A V` then form a
  uniformity, namely `filter_from [set V | open V /\ V !=set0]
  (orbit_entourage A)`. This uniformity induces the topology of X, and
  `orbit_entourage_family A` is a G invariant basis of it. The supporting
  lemmas include [`orbit_entourage_translate`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L150) (beta_{A_h(V)} = beta_V),
  [`orbit_entourage_split`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L220) and [`orbit_entourage_section_open`](../../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L193).

Two regressions. On the empty space every group acts homogeneously and the
condition holds, but the family of the sets beta_V is empty and is not the
basis of any uniformity. The trivial action on the discrete space
{true, false} satisfies the condition but is not transitive. There the family
is again not the basis of any uniformity: the member for V = {true} does not
contain (false, false).

Sources: Federer 2.7.3, p. 123, with 2.7.1, pp. 121–122. The erratum has no
entry for p. 123.

**Divergences.**

- *Uniformity (p. 123).* MathComp's `uniformType` is a structure carried by a
  type. "The topology of X is induced by some uniformity" therefore cannot
  quantify over it directly. A uniformity is instead the predicate
  [`is_uniformity`](../../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L33) on a family of subsets of X x X, together with
  [`uniformity_induces_topology`](../../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L54). This is an interpretation. The fields are
  those of MathComp's `Nbhs_isUniform`, and [`uniform_type_uniformity`](../../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L104) proves
  that MathComp uniform spaces satisfy them. The converse construction of a
  MathComp structure from the predicate is not formalised. A basis is a
  subfamily such that each entourage contains one of its members.
- *Proof (p. 123).* Federer writes "One easily verifies" and gives no proof.
  The proof of necessity takes the section W = {y : (x,y) in beta} for a member
  beta of the basis with beta o beta^{-1} o beta inside a given entourage. The
  proof of sufficiency uses transitivity for the diagonal and
  beta_{A_h(V)} = beta_V for the filter base property. The condition gives
  beta_W o beta_W subset beta_V and the coincidence of the topologies.
- *Hypotheses (p. 123).* Necessity holds for every map A. Sufficiency uses the
  group laws and the definition of a homogeneous space of 2.7.1. Neither local
  compactness nor the Hausdorff property of X or G is used; this is a
  generalisation. Transitivity is needed for the specified basis, as the
  regression with the trivial action shows.
- *Empty X (p. 123).* For empty X the family of the sets beta_V is empty. It
  cannot be the basis of a uniformity, because a uniformity contains X x X.
  The second assertion therefore assumes that X is nonempty; this is a
  corrected statement, and the regression shows that the hypothesis is
  needed. The equivalence itself holds for empty X: there, the family of all
  subsets of X x X serves as both the uniformity and its basis.
- *The uniformity in the second assertion (p. 123).* Federer specifies only
  B. The uniformity is made explicit as the filter generated by B.

<a id="item-2-7-4"></a>

### 2.7.4 — Haar ratios; existence of a nonzero monotone invariant integral

Federer, pp. 123–125. The theorem and every property of Haar ratios listed in
its proof are proved. The theorem assumes in addition that X is nonempty, as
recorded below. The proof follows Federer, with the corrections of his erratum
for pp. 124–125.

- [`HaarRatioUP.v`](../../theories/HaarRatioUP.v) contains the definitions. `group_sum xi` is the finitely
  supported sum over G. `haar_cover A u v` is C(u,v): the maps xi: G -> R with
  xi >= 0, {g: xi(g) > 0} finite and u <= sum_{g in G} xi(g) (v o A_g).
  `is_haar_ratio A u v r` states that r is the infimum (u:v) of the sums
  sum_G xi with xi in C(u,v), by the two infimum laws.
  `is_haar_ratio_function A rho` states that rho u v is the Haar ratio
  whenever u in K(X)+ and 0 <> v in K(X)+.
- [`HaarRatioLaws.v`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v). A member of C(u,v) corresponds to a finite family of
  pairs (g, c): [`haar_family_cover`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L53), [`haar_family_total`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L59),
  [`haar_cover_family`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L114), [`haar_family_cover_function`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L134) and [`is_haar_ratioP`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L155). For
  a homogeneous space X of G the listed properties are proved for every Haar
  ratio function rho: [`haar_cover_nonempty`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L426) (C(u,v) <> empty),
  [`haar_ratio_function_exists`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L455), [`haar_ratio_sup_bound`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L519)
  (sup im u/sup im v <= (u:v)), [`haar_ratio_translate`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L506)
  ((u o A_h:v) = (u:v)), [`haar_ratio_scale`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L525) ((cu:v) = c(u:v) for 0 < c),
  [`haar_ratio_add`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L539), [`haar_ratio_monotone`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L553), [`haar_ratio_mul`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L580)
  ((u:w) <= (u:v)(v:w)) and [`haar_ratio_quotient_bounds`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L611)
  (1/(w:u) <= (u:v)/(w:v) <= (u:w)). The supporting results include
  [`haar_ratio_zero`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L562), [`haar_ratio_nonnegative`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L572), [`haar_ratio_positive`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L592),
  [`haar_ratio_quotient_upper`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L628), and the forms `is_haar_ratio_*` for a single
  infimum.
- [`UniformityCompactSupport.v`](../../theories/UniformityCompactSupport.v), for a uniformity E inducing the topology of X
  (2.7.3). [`compactly_supported_uniformly_continuous`](../../theories/UniformityCompactSupport.v#L56) shows that every member
  of K(X) is uniformly continuous. [`entourage_small_support_function`](../../theories/UniformityCompactSupport.v#L104) gives,
  for X locally compact Hausdorff, an entourage beta and a point a, some v in
  K(X)+ with v(a) = 1 and (spt v) x (spt v) subset beta.
- [`InvariantIntegralExistence.v`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v). For a fixed nonzero w in K(X)+:
  `haar_approximation v` is p_v, [`haar_product_space`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L197) is P, compact by
  [`haar_product_space_compact`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L250), and `haar_closed_set beta` is S(beta).
  [`haar_closed_set_nested`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L281) is the printed statement
  S(beta_1) cap S(beta_2) supset S(beta_3) <> empty, and [`haar_limit_exists`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L319)
  gives lambda in P cap cap_{beta in B} S(beta). The properties of lambda on
  K(X)+ are [`haar_limit_nonneg`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L377), [`haar_limit_unit`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L383) (lambda(w) = 1),
  [`haar_limit_translate`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L394), [`haar_limit_scale`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L405), [`haar_limit_zero`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L417),
  [`haar_limit_subadditive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L429), [`haar_limit_superadditive_estimate`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L457) (the printed
  estimate with the factor 1+2 epsilon) and [`haar_limit_superadditive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L564). The
  theorem [`uniform_action_invariant_integral_exists`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L601) assumes
  `is_group_laws mul inv e`, `is_homogeneous_space mul A`,
  `hausdorff_space X`, `locally_compact [set: X]`, `exists x : X, True` and
  `acts_uniformly A`; it concludes that some mu satisfies
  `is_nonzero_monotone_invariant_integral A mu`. The integral is
  [`positive_difference_functional`](../../theories/PositiveDifferenceFunctional.v#L41) of lambda.

Two regressions. For 0 <> v in K(X)+, (v:v) = 1. On the empty space every map
is a uniform action and a homogeneous space, but no nonzero integral exists.

Sources: Federer 2.7.4, pp. 123–125, with 2.5.13–2.5.14, pp. 106–108, and
2.7.1–2.7.3, pp. 121–123. Federer's erratum p. 2 corrects book p. 124 line 30
and book p. 125 lines 7–8; both corrections are applied.

**Divergences.**

- *Nonempty X (p. 123).* For empty X, K(X) = {0} and every Daniell integral
  vanishes on it, although all the other hypotheses hold. The theorem
  therefore assumes that X is nonempty. This is an added hypothesis, and the
  regression shows that it is necessary.
- *Hypotheses on G and X (p. 123).* The proof uses the group laws of G and the
  definition of a homogeneous space; local compactness and the Hausdorff
  property of G are not used, and the Haar ratio laws do not use them for X.
  This is a generalisation.
- *Sums over G (p. 123).* The sums over G are the finitely supported sums of
  MathComp. The proofs work with finite families (g_i, c_i), which determine
  the members of C(u,v) and give the same sums; the infimum is specified by
  its laws. This is a different proof route for the printed facts, which
  Federer calls obvious.
- *(u:w) <= (u:v).(v:w) (p. 124).* Federer assumes u, v, w nonzero. The proof
  uses only that v and w are nonzero, and the same holds for the right
  inequality (u:v)/(w:v) <= (u:w) ([`haar_ratio_quotient_upper`](../../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L628)). This is a
  generalisation.
- *The product P (p. 124).* Federer indexes the product by K(X)+. The
  formalisation indexes it by all functions X -> R, with the factor {0} and
  p_v(u) = 0 for u outside K(X)+. This is an interpretation; the additional
  coordinates are constant.
- *The point lambda (p. 124).* The printed nested statement is proved
  ([`haar_closed_set_nested`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L281)), including the nonemptiness of S(beta), which the
  book does not argue. Since compactness in MathComp is defined through
  filters, lambda is obtained as a cluster point in P of the proper filter
  generated by the sets {p_v: (spt v) x (spt v) subset beta}. This is a
  different proof route for the same step.
- *Properties of lambda (p. 124).* Federer writes that they "follow from like
  properties of the approximating functions p_v". Each property is a closed
  condition in the product topology which holds at the p_v
  ([`haar_limit_closed`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L346)). This makes the printed argument explicit.
- *The estimate (pp. 124–125).* The printed "For any v in S(beta)" and
  "whenever v in S(beta). Since lambda in Closure S(beta)" are replaced
  according to Federer's erratum p. 2 by "Whenever 0 <> v in K(X)+,
  (spt v) x (spt v) subset beta, a in spt v" and "Since lambda in S(beta)".
  This is a corrected statement from Federer's own erratum.
- *The functions r_j (p. 124).* The proof defines r_j = u_j/s, with x/0 = 0,
  and shows r_j in K(X)+ and r_j s = u_j ([`compactly_supported_quotient`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L153));
  spt r_j = spt u_j is not needed. The uniform continuity of r_1, r_2, used
  without proof, is proved for every member of K(X). This makes the printed
  argument explicit.
- *Limit and extension (pp. 124–125).* Federer stops at
  lambda(u_1)+lambda(u_2) <= (1+2 epsilon)[lambda(u_1+u_2)+epsilon lambda(f)]
  and calls lambda an integral on K(X)+. The limit epsilon -> 0 is made
  explicit, and lambda is extended to K(X) by lambda(f+) - lambda(f-), which is
  a monotone Daniell integral by 2.5.13 ([`compact_positive_difference_daniell`](../../theories/CompactPositiveConeExtension.v#L25)).
  This makes the printed argument explicit.

<a id="item-2-7-5"></a>

### 2.7.5 — Existence of left and right Haar integrals

Federer, p. 125. The corollary is proved for a locally compact Hausdorff
topological group, with the printed bases.

- [`GroupUniformity.v`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v). Consider a map d: G x G -> G with d(x,x) = e,
  d(x,z) = d(x,y) d(y,z) and d(y,x) = d(x,y)^{-1}, such that y |-> d(x,y) is
  continuous and has a continuous left inverse z |-> k(x,z) with k(x,e) = x.
  Then `division_entourage V` is {(x,y): d(x,y) in V},
  [`division_entourage_family`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L54) is the family of these sets for all
  neighbourhoods V of e, and [`division_uniformity`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L60) is the filter they
  generate. [`division_uniformity_laws`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L66), [`division_uniformity_topology`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L93),
  [`division_uniformity_basis`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L107) and [`division_entourage_invariant`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L117) show that
  this is a uniformity inducing the topology, with a basis invariant under
  every action preserving d; [`division_acts_uniformly`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L124) combines them.
  [`left_division_entourage_basis`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L150) is the statement for the sets
  {(x,y): x^{-1} y in V} and the action 2.7.2(1), and
  [`right_division_entourage_basis`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L177) the statement for the sets
  {(x,y): x y^{-1} in V} and the action 2.7.2(2).
  [`left_multiplication_acts_uniformly`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L214) and
  [`right_multiplication_acts_uniformly`](../../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L223) conclude [`acts_uniformly`](../../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L75). The
  hypothesis is only `is_topological_group mul inv e`.
- [`HaarIntegralExistence.v`](../../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v), for `is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`:
  [`left_haar_integral_exists`](../../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L33), [`right_haar_integral_exists`](../../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L45) and the corollary
  [`haar_integrals_exist`](../../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L58), by 2.7.4 for the two actions.

Sources: Federer 2.7.5, p. 125, with 2.7.2–2.7.4, pp. 122–125. The erratum has
no entry for 2.7.5.

The regressions for 2.7.4, (v:v) = 1 and the empty space without nonzero invariant integrals, are added with this item.

**Divergences.**

- *Uniformity of the actions (p. 125).* Federer writes "one sees that the
  action ... is uniform". The proof verifies the entourage laws, the
  coincidence of the topologies and the invariance of the printed bases. Both
  cases are derived from one statement about the map d. This is a different
  proof organisation; the right case is treated with the printed sets, as in
  the book, and not through the inversion of 2.7.2.
- *The uniformity (p. 125).* Federer specifies only the basis B. The
  uniformity is made explicit as the filter generated by B, as in 2.7.3.
- *Hypotheses (p. 125).* The uniformity of the two actions uses only the group
  laws and the continuity of the multiplication and of the inversion. The
  corollary itself uses the standing hypotheses of 2.7.1 on G, and the
  nonemptiness of G, which holds since e in G.

<a id="item-2-7-6"></a>

### 2.7.6 — The density of a left Haar integral with respect to a right one

Federer, p. 125. The lemma is proved as printed, together with the claims of
its proof: the computation, the continuity and positivity of k, the
uniqueness of k under (2), its independence of the choice of v, and (1) for
all v. G is a locally compact Hausdorff topological group
(`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`). The left Haar integral
lambda and the right Haar integral mu are hypotheses; the existence theorem
2.7.5 is not used. K(G) is [`compactly_supported_continuous_functions`](../../theories/CompactSupportFunctionUP.v#L21),
lambda_x v(y x^{-1}) is lambda applied to x |-> v(y x^{-1}), k u is the
pointwise product, and continuity of k refers to the standard topology of R
(`real_line_metric R`).

- [`InvariantIntegralPositivity.v`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v), for a homogeneous space X of G and a
  nonzero monotone invariant integral mu on K(X). The theorem
  [`nonzero_invariant_integral_positive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L127) gives mu(u) > 0 for every nonzero u in
  K(X)+. Its proof rests on [`invariant_integral_domination`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L71): if u in K(X)+ and
  u(a) > 0, then |mu(w)| <= C mu(u) for each w in K(X) and some C >= 0, by a
  finite cover of spt w by sets {x : u(A(g,x)) > u(a)/2}. If X is moreover a
  locally compact Hausdorff space, [`nonzero_invariant_integral_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L152)
  shows that a continuous function f on X is determined by the values mu(f u),
  u in K(X). The corollaries [`left_haar_integral_positive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L208),
  [`right_haar_integral_positive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L218), [`left_haar_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L232) and
  [`right_haar_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L245) are these statements for Haar integrals.
- [`HaarIntegralDensity.v`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v). [`left_shear_compact`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L98) and [`right_shear_compact`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L108) show
  that (x,y) |-> u(y x) v(y) and (x,y) |-> u(y) v(y x^{-1}) belong to
  K(G x G). They compose (z,y) |-> u(z) v(y) with the homeomorphisms
  [`left_shear_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L62) and [`right_shear_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L82) of G x G.
  [`haar_fubini_identity`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L176) is Federer's computation:
  lambda(u) mu(v) = mu_y[u(y) lambda_x v(y x^{-1})] whenever u, v in K(G).
  [`haar_section_integral_continuous`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L211) proves that y |-> lambda_x v(y x^{-1}) is
  continuous for v in K(G). [`haar_section_quotient_density`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L236) shows that for
  v in K(G) with mu(v) <> 0 the function k(y) = lambda_x v(y x^{-1}) / mu(v) is
  continuous and satisfies (2). [`haar_integral_density_unique`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L265) states that k is
  uniquely characterised by (2) among continuous functions, and
  [`haar_integral_density_choice_independent`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L279) that k does not depend on the
  choice of v. [`haar_density_section_identity`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L296) shows that every continuous k
  with (2) satisfies (1) for all v in K(G). The lemma itself is
  [`haar_integral_density`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L322): there exists k with k continuous, k(y) > 0 for all
  y, (1) and (2).

Two regressions. On the one-point group, evaluation at the point is a left
and a right Haar integral, and the only continuous k with (2) is the constant
1.

Sources: Federer 2.7.6, p. 125, with 2.5.13–2.5.14, pp. 106–108, 2.6.4, p.
119, 2.6.6, p. 120, and 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–123. The erratum has no entry for
p. 125.

The regressions on the one-point group are added with 2.7.7.

**Divergences.**

- *Fubini's theorem (p. 125).* Federer cites "Fubini's theorem". The two
  interchanges of the order of integration are made by
  [`compact_daniell_iterations_commute`](../../theories/CompactDaniellIterationCommutation.v#L44). This is the order independence of
  iterated Daniell integrals on K(G x G), which follows from the uniqueness
  of the product Daniell integral of 2.6.4. No product measure is used. This
  is a different proof route. The membership in K(G x G) of the two
  integrands, which Federer does not mention, is proved.
- *Positivity of Haar integrals (p. 125).* Federer uses without statement
  that a Haar integral is positive on nonzero members of K(G)+, both for
  "positive" and for "k is uniquely characterized by (2)". This is proved for
  nonzero monotone invariant integrals on any homogeneous space, as a
  supporting result. The uniqueness of a continuous multiplier also uses a
  cutoff function of 2.5.13–2.5.14.
- *Continuity of k (p. 125).* Federer asserts that (1) defines a continuous
  function. The proof multiplies by a cutoff function w equal to 1 on a
  compact neighbourhood of a given point. Continuity then follows from the
  continuity of y |-> lambda_x[w(y) v(y x^{-1})] given by the argument of
  2.6.4.
- *Choice of v (p. 125).* The "particular nonzero v in K(G)+" is a cutoff
  function v_0 with v_0(e) = 1. Then mu(v_0) > 0 and
  lambda_x v_0(y x^{-1}) > 0 for every y, which gives k > 0.
  [`haar_section_quotient_density`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L236) and the independence statement are proved
  for all v in K(G) with mu(v) <> 0. By positivity, these include the nonzero
  members of K(G)+. This is a generalisation.
- *(1) for all v (p. 125).* Federer infers (1) from the independence of the
  choice of v. Read literally, this gives (1) for the nonzero v in K(G)+, and
  the other v would follow by linearity. Here, for each v in K(G), the
  continuous functions y |-> lambda_x v(y x^{-1}) and y |-> mu(v) k(y) are
  shown to have equal integrals mu(. u) for all u in K(G). Hence they
  coincide. This is a different proof route.
- *Uniqueness (p. 125).* "Uniquely characterized by (2)" is stated among
  continuous functions, because mu is defined on K(G) only. The proof uses
  only that mu is a right Haar integral; lambda may be any functional. This
  is an interpretation and a generalisation.

<a id="item-2-7-7"></a>

### 2.7.7 — Uniqueness of Haar integrals

Federer, pp. 125–126. The theorem is proved in both versions, for a locally
compact Hausdorff topological group G. [`left_haar_integral_unique`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUniqueness.v#L54) states that
for left Haar integrals lambda_1, lambda_2 there is c > 0 with
lambda_1(u) = c lambda_2(u) whenever u in K(G). [`right_haar_integral_unique`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUniqueness.v#L97)
is the same statement for right Haar integrals mu_1, mu_2. Both are in
[`HaarIntegralUniqueness.v`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUniqueness.v).

The left version follows the printed proof. The densities k_1, k_2 of 2.7.6
([`haar_integral_density`](../../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L322)) give f = k_1/k_2, positive and continuous, with
lambda_1(u) = lambda_2(f u). The left invariance of lambda_1 and lambda_2
gives lambda_2(f u) = lambda_2((f o A_g^{-1}) u). Then
[`left_haar_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L232) gives f = f o A_g^{-1}, hence
f(x) = f(x^{-1} x) = f(e). The constant is c = f(e).

Two regressions. On the one-point group, every left Haar integral is a
positive multiple of evaluation at the point. The zero functional is a left
invariant integral but no positive multiple of that Haar integral.

Sources: Federer 2.7.7, pp. 125–126, with 2.7.2, pp. 122–123, and 2.7.6, p.
125. The erratum has no entry for 2.7.7; its entries for p. 125 concern
2.7.4.

**Divergences.**

- *The right Haar integral (p. 126).* Federer's proof assumes a right Haar
  integral mu, whose existence is 2.7.5. Here mu = p#(lambda_1) with
  p(x) = x^{-1}, which is a right Haar integral by 2.7.2
  ([`left_haar_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L90)). The theorem therefore does not depend on 2.7.5.
  The rest of the proof follows Federer.
- *"Hence f = f o A_g^{-1}" (p. 126).* The values lambda_2(f u), u in K(G),
  determine the continuous function f.
  It rests on the positivity of Haar integrals and is proved as
  [`left_haar_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L232).
- *Right Haar integrals (p. 125).* Federer states the right version in
  parentheses. It is deduced from the left version applied to p#(mu_1) and
  p#(mu_2), which are left Haar integrals by 2.7.2
  ([`right_haar_inversion`](../../theories/HaarInversion.v#L98)), since p#(p#(mu)) = mu. This is a different proof
  route.

<a id="item-2-7-8"></a>

### 2.7.8 — The modular function

Federer, p. 126. The theorem is proved with all its claims: the existence of
Delta_G, the properties (1)–(4) for all left and right Haar integrals, and the
four uniqueness assertions. G is a locally compact Hausdorff topological group
(`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`). K(G) is
[`compactly_supported_continuous_functions`](../../theories/CompactSupportFunctionUP.v#L21), lambda_x[u(x^{-1}) D(x^{-1})] is
lambda applied to x |-> u(x^{-1}) D(x^{-1}), and continuity refers to the
standard topology of R (`real_line_metric R`).

- [`ModularFunctionUP.v`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v) specifies the four properties of a function
  D: G -> R by the predicates [`left_haar_right_covariant`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L37) (1),
  [`right_haar_left_covariant`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L43) (2), [`left_haar_inversion_identity`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L50) (3) and
  [`right_haar_inversion_identity`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L58) (4). The predicate [`is_modular_function`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L66)
  requires continuity, positivity and (1)–(4). Later results use only this
  predicate.
- [`ModularFunction.v`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v) follows the printed proof. For a left Haar integral
  lambda, mu = p#(lambda) and the function k of 2.7.6 for lambda and mu, the
  lemmas [`haar_density_left_translation`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L130) (k(y) mu(v) = mu_x v(y x)),
  [`haar_density_right_haar_left_covariant`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L135),
  [`haar_density_left_haar_right_covariant`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L144), [`haar_density_left_inversion`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L154) and
  [`haar_density_right_inversion`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L163) are the displays of the proof.
  [`haar_density_modular_function`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L177) shows that k is a modular function; the
  passage to arbitrary Haar integrals uses 2.7.7. [`modular_function_of_left_haar`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L206)
  packages this for a given lambda.
- [`modular_function_exists`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L225): there exists D with `is_modular_function mul inv D`.
  The left Haar integral is supplied by 2.7.5 ([`left_haar_integral_exists`](../../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L33)).
- Uniqueness: [`modular_function_unique_left_haar`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L238) (every D: G -> R with (1)
  equals Delta_G), [`modular_function_unique_right_haar`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L255) (the same for (2)),
  [`modular_function_unique_left_inversion`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L276) and
  [`modular_function_unique_right_inversion`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L307) (every continuous D with (3),
  respectively (4), equals Delta_G), and [`modular_function_unique`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L325) (two modular
  functions coincide).
- [`modular_function_homomorphism`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L337): Delta_G(g h) = Delta_G(g) Delta_G(h) and
  Delta_G(e) = 1, by 2.7.8(1) and 2.7.1, p. 122.

Three regressions. On a commutative group every modular function is the
constant 1, and then (3) gives lambda(u o p) = lambda(u) for every left Haar
integral lambda. With chi = Delta_G, 2.7.9(3) says that a Daniell integral is
left Delta_G covariant if and only if it is right invariant, which recovers
2.7.8(2) for right Haar integrals.

Sources: Federer 2.7.8, p. 126, with 2.7.2, pp. 122–123, 2.7.5, p. 125, and
2.7.6–2.7.7, pp. 125–126. The erratum has no entry for p. 126.

**Divergences.**

- *The modular function as a predicate (p. 126).* Federer names "the"
  function Delta_G. Here a modular function is any function satisfying
  [`is_modular_function`](../../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L66); existence and the uniqueness theorems show that there
  is exactly one. This is an interpretation following the rule that objects
  are specified by their properties.
- *Uniqueness for (3) and (4) (p. 126).* Federer gives no proof of
  "uniquely characterized by each of these properties". The competing
  function D is assumed continuous in the uniqueness statements for (3) and
  (4): lambda and mu are defined on K(G) only, and continuity of D ensures
  that the integrands of (3) and (4) belong to K(G). Positivity of D is not
  assumed. The proof substitutes u = w o p. The values lambda(f w), w in K(G),
  determine the continuous function f
  ([`left_haar_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L232), [`right_haar_multiplier_unique`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L245)).
- *Uniqueness for (1) and (2) (p. 126).* No condition is imposed on D, a
  slight generalisation of the class "continuous positive real valued
  functions". The proof compares D(g) lambda(u) with Delta_G(g) lambda(u) for
  a u in K(G) with lambda(u) <> 0.
- *Homomorphism property.* [`modular_function_homomorphism`](../../theories/ModularFunction.v#L337) is not stated in
  2.7.8. It follows from 2.7.8(1) and 2.7.1, p. 122, and is used implicitly by
  Federer in 2.7.9(3).
- The existence proof follows Federer.

<a id="item-2-7-9"></a>

### 2.7.9 — Covariant Daniell integrals on K(G)

Federer, pp. 126–127. All three equivalences are proved for a Daniell
integral nu on K(G) and a continuous homomorphism chi of G into R ~ {0}, G a
locally compact Hausdorff topological group. The homomorphism chi is given by
the hypotheses chi(g h) = chi(g) chi(h), chi(g) <> 0 and continuity;
u chi^{-1} is x |-> u(x) chi(x)^{-1}. Delta_G is any function with
`is_modular_function mul inv Delta`.

- [`DaniellContinuousMultiplier.v`](../../theories/DaniellContinuousMultiplier.v): [`continuous_multiplier_daniell`](../../theories/DaniellContinuousMultiplier.v#L100) shows that
  u |-> nu(u f) is a Daniell integral on K(X) for every continuous f on a
  locally compact Hausdorff space X. The bound of 2.5.13 on functions
  vanishing outside spt u gives the upper bound on positive order intervals,
  and the first assertion of 2.5.13 concludes.
- [`InvariantDaniellVariation.v`](../../theories/InvariantDaniellVariation.v), for an invariant integral sigma on K(X), X a
  locally compact Hausdorff homogeneous space of G.
  [`invariant_integral_variations_invariant`](../../theories/InvariantDaniellVariation.v#L123): sigma+ and sigma- are invariant
  on K(X)+. [`invariant_integral_variation_extensions`](../../theories/InvariantDaniellVariation.v#L148): they extend to monotone
  invariant Daniell integrals P, Q on K(X) with sigma = P
- Q on K(X).
  [`invariant_integral_variation_decomposition`](../../theories/InvariantDaniellVariation.v#L178) is the same for the variations
  of 2.5.5.
- [`CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v). [`left_invariant_variations_haar_multiples`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L118)
  is Federer's "sigma+ and sigma- are nonnegative multiples of a left Haar
  integral"; [`left_invariant_integral_multiple`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L142) gives sigma = r lambda on K(G);
  [`left_invariant_integral_right_covariant`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L160) shows that every left invariant
  integral is right D covariant whenever every left Haar integral is.
  [`covariant_weight`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L234) is sigma(u) = nu(u chi), and
  [`left_covariant_weight_invariant`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L256) is the computation "nu is left chi
  covariant if and only if sigma is left invariant".
- (1) [`left_covariant_haar_characterisation`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L322): nu is left chi covariant if and
  only if there exist a left Haar integral lambda and r in R with
  nu(u) = r lambda(u chi^{-1}) whenever u in K(G). The two directions for a
  given lambda are [`left_covariant_haar_multiple`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L286) and
  [`haar_multiple_left_covariant`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L302).
- (2) [`right_covariant_haar_characterisation`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L405): nu is right chi covariant if and
  only if there exist a right Haar integral mu and r in R with
  nu(u) = r mu(u chi) whenever u in K(G).
- (3) [`left_covariant_modular_characterisation`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L436): nu is left chi covariant if
  and only if nu is right Delta_G chi^{-1} covariant; the direct implication
  is [`left_covariant_right_modular`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L339).

Sources: Federer 2.7.9, pp. 126–127, with 2.5.5–2.5.6, pp. 96–98,
2.5.13–2.5.14, pp. 106–108, 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–123, 2.7.5, p. 125, 2.7.7,
pp. 125–126, and 2.7.8, p. 126. The erratum has no entry for pp. 126–127.

The regressions for 2.7.8, that commutative groups have modular function 1 and satisfy lambda(u o p) = lambda(u), are added with this item.

**Divergences.**

- *The character chi (p. 126).* "A continuous homomorphism of G into the
  multiplicative group R ~ {0}" is expressed by three hypotheses;
  chi(e) = 1 and chi(g^{-1}) = chi(g)^{-1} are derived ([`character_unit`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L211),
  [`character_inverse`](../../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L219)). This is an interpretation.
- *sigma is a Daniell integral (p. 127).* Federer uses this without comment.
  It is proved by 2.5.13 ([`continuous_multiplier_daniell`](../../theories/DaniellContinuousMultiplier.v#L100)).
- *Invariance of sigma+ and sigma- (p. 127).* Federer asserts it. The proof
  combines "mu+ + mu- is |chi| covariant" of 2.7.1, p. 122
  ([`covariant_variation_integral`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L172)), for chi = 1, with sigma = sigma+ - sigma-
  on K(G)+ (2.5.5). Since a Haar integral is a functional on K(G), the
  variations, defined on K(G)+, are first extended to monotone Daniell
  integrals on K(G) by u |-> sigma+(u+) - sigma+(u-) (2.5.14); each extension
  is zero on K(G) or a left Haar integral, and 2.7.7 applies. These steps are
  supplied.
- *"sigma is right Delta_G covariant by 2.7.8(1)" in (3) (p. 127).* 2.7.8(1)
  is applied to the two monotone parts of sigma, each zero on K(G) or a left
  Haar integral, instead of to sigma = r lambda. The direct implication of (3)
  therefore uses only 2.7.8(1) for Delta_G. The computation follows Federer.
- *The converse of (3) (p. 127).* "The converse follows by use of p#" is
  carried out by applying the direct implication to p#(nu) and the character
  Delta_G chi^{-1}. That this function is multiplicative follows from 2.7.1,
  p. 122, when nu does not vanish on K(G); if nu vanishes on K(G), nu is
  trivially left chi covariant. Federer uses the multiplicativity of Delta_G
  without comment.
- *Order of factors (p. 127).* The statement has "Delta_G chi^{-1}", the
  proof "chi^{-1} Delta_G". The formal character is g |-> Delta_G(g) chi(g)^{-1}.
- The proofs of (1) and (2) follow Federer, with mu = p#(lambda) in (2).

<a id="item-2-7-10"></a>

### 2.7.10 — The epimorphism mu^x: K(G) -> K(X)

Federer, pp. 127–128. The lemma is proved with all six properties, the
linearity and the surjectivity of mu^x, and the uniqueness of mu^x. G is a
locally compact Hausdorff topological group
(`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`), X is a homogeneous space of
G (`is_homogeneous_space mul A`) and X is Hausdorff (`hausdorff_space X`). The
point gx is `A g x`, A^x is `orbit_map A x`, and u_h is
`fun y => u (mul h y)`.

- [`IsotropyGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v). The isotropy group G^x is the type
  `set_type (isotropy_subgroup A x)` with MathComp's relative topology, the
  initial topology of the inclusion `set_val`. The predicate
  `is_subgroup_restriction mul inv e S mulS invS eS` states that the inclusion
  carries mulS, invS and eS to mul, inv and e. [`subgroup_restriction_exists`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L128)
  gives such operations for every subgroup, [`subgroup_restriction_complete`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L142)
  extends a restricted multiplication to the whole structure, and
  [`subgroup_restriction_unique`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L155) shows that the structure is unique.
  [`subgroup_restriction_topological`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L179) shows that the restricted structure is a
  topological group. [`subgroup_restriction_locally_compact_hausdorff`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L211) shows
  that it is a locally compact Hausdorff group when S is closed. This rests on
  [`set_type_val_proper`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L71) (the inclusion of a closed set is proper),
  [`set_type_compact_preimage`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L49) and [`set_type_locally_compact`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L86).
  [`isotropy_group_exists`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L236) applies this to G^x, which is closed because X is
  Hausdorff. [`isotropy_left_haar_integral_exists`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L251) gives a left Haar integral
  of G^x by 2.7.5.
- [`CosetIntegralUP.v`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v). `is_coset_integral mul A x mu M` specifies mu^x by
  "mu^x: K(G) -> K(X)" (`coset_integral_compact`) and by (1),
  `M u (A g x) = mu (fun s => u (mul g (set_val s)))` for u in K(G)
  (`coset_integral_value`). Here mu is any function on the real functions on
  G^x.
- [`CosetIntegralLaws.v`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v). Let mu be a left Haar integral of G^x
  (`is_left_haar_integral mulS mu`, with [`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104) for
  mulS, invS, eS) and let M satisfy [`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29).
  [`coset_section_compact`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L96) shows that s |-> u(gs) belongs to K(G^x), and
  [`coset_section_translate`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L106) is "w(gt) = w(g) whenever t in G^x".
  [`coset_section_positive`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L149) gives mu_s[u(gs)] > 0 for u in K(G)+ with
  u(g) <> 0, by [`left_haar_integral_positive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L208) (2.7.6).
  [`coset_integral_unique`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L215): M is determined on K(G) by (1).
  [`coset_integral_add`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L225) and [`coset_integral_scale`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L237) (every real scalar):
  linearity. [`coset_integral_support`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L310) is (2); [`coset_integral_support_sub`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L295)
  gives the inclusion spt mu^x(u) subset A^x(spt u) for every u in K(G).
  [`coset_integral_multiplier_compact`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L348) is (3); [`coset_integral_multiplier`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L328)
  proves it for every continuous v. [`coset_integral_translate`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L356) is (4).
  [`coset_integral_cover`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L374) and [`coset_integral_quotient`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L421) are the two parts of
  (5); in the second, v/mu^x(f) is the pointwise quotient
  `fun y => v y / M f y`. [`coset_integral_onto`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L436) is the surjectivity.
  [`coset_integral_le`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L247), [`coset_integral_nonnegative`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L258) and
  [`coset_integral_increases`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L269) give the order properties of M.
  [`coset_integral_daniell`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L476), [`coset_integral_monotone_daniell`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L453) and
  [`coset_integral_covariant`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L499) are (6).
- [`CosetIntegralExistence.v`](../../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v). [`coset_section_integral_continuous`](../../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L100) is the
  continuity of w(g) = mu_s[u(gs)]. [`open_map_factor_continuous`](../../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L48) shows that f
  is continuous when f o p is continuous, for an open map p onto Y.
  [`coset_integral_exists`](../../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L155) gives M with [`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29). The lemma itself
  is [`isotropy_integral_epimorphism`](../../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L195): there is M with [`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29),
  linear on K(G), onto K(X), and with the properties (2)–(6).

Three regressions. Under left multiplication of G on itself, G^x = {e}. If mu
is the evaluation at the identity of G^x, then mu^x(u)(gx) = u(g). Finally,
mu^x maps the zero function to 0.

Sources: Federer 2.7.10, pp. 127–128, with 2.7.1, pp. 121–122; 2.7.2, p. 122;
2.7.5, p. 125; 2.7.6, p. 125; 2.6.4, p. 119; 2.5.13–2.5.14, pp. 106–108;
2.5.5, p. 96; 2.5.2, p. 92. The erratum has no entry for pp. 127–128.

**Divergences.**

- *The isotropy group (p. 127).* Federer regards the closed subgroup G^x as a
  topological group without comment. Here it is the subtype with the
  relative topology. Its operations are specified by
  [`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104). That this is a locally compact Hausdorff
  topological group is proved. This is an interpretation.
- *The map mu^x (p. 127).* mu^x is specified by the predicate
  [`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29): it maps K(G) into K(X) and satisfies (1). The values
  outside K(G) are not specified. Linearity and surjectivity refer to K(G)
  and K(X). Federer's "corresponds" is made precise by the uniqueness
  theorem [`coset_integral_unique`](../../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L215). This is an interpretation.
- *Continuity of w (p. 128).* The proof follows Federer. The uniform
  continuity of u is that of 2.7.5 for the sets {(y,z): y z^{-1} in V}.
  Federer does not state the passage from |u(gs) - u(g0 s)| <= delta to
  |w(g) - w(g0)| <= delta C. It uses the bound of a Daniell integral on the
  functions supported in a fixed compact subset of G^x (2.6.4).
- *The factorisation through A^x (p. 128).* mu^x(u) is obtained from the
  openness and the surjectivity of A^x. The homeomorphism G/G^x ~ X of 2.7.1
  is not used. This is a different proof route.
- *(2) and (5) (pp. 127–128).* Federer writes "easily verified" for (2) and
  "we see that spt v subset {y: mu^x(f)(y) > 0}" for (5). Both use the fact
  that a left Haar integral of G^x is positive on the nonzero members of
  K(G^x)+. This is proved in 2.7.6 ([`left_haar_integral_positive`](../../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L208)).
- *Existence in (5) (p. 128).* Federer infers the existence of f from the
  fact that the sets A^x{g: u(g) > 0}, u in K(G)+, form a base of X. Here
  finitely many open sets A^x(Int N), N compact neighbourhoods in G, cover
  spt v. f is a cutoff function equal to 1 on the union of these N
  (2.5.13–2.5.14). This is a different proof route.
- *The quotient in (5) (p. 127).* v/mu^x(f) is the pointwise quotient of real
  numbers with r/0 = 0. It belongs to K(X), since it vanishes off spt v and
  spt v subset {y: mu^x(f)(y) > 0}. This is an interpretation.
- *(3) (p. 127).* (3) is proved for every continuous real function v on X,
  and is then specialised to v in K(X). This is a generalisation. The
  membership of (v o A^x) . u in K(G) is proved.
- *(6) (p. 128).* Federer writes "(6) follows from (1), (4), (5)". The proof
  also uses the monotonicity of mu^x and the monotone convergence
  mu^x(u_n) -> mu^x(u) pointwise for increasing u_n, which follow from (1)
  and from the corresponding properties of mu. For a Daniell integral nu in
  the sense of 2.5.5, the bound of nu on {k: 0 <= k <= mu^x(f)} bounds
  nu o mu^x on {k: 0 <= k <= f}. The covariance equivalence assumes that nu
  is a Daniell integral on K(X), as the printed sentence does.
- *Hypotheses (p. 127).* Of the standing hypotheses on X, only the
  Hausdorff property is used. It makes G^x closed and A^x(spt u) closed. The
  local compactness of X is not assumed. This is a generalisation.

<a id="item-2-7-11"></a>

### 2.7.11 — Weil's condition; uniqueness of covariant integrals on K(X)

Federer, pp. 128–129. Both assertions are proved. G is a locally compact
Hausdorff topological group (`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`),
X is a locally compact Hausdorff homogeneous space of G
(`is_homogeneous_space mul A`, `hausdorff_space X`,
`locally_compact [set: X]`) and x is a point of X. The isotropy group G^x is
the subtype of `isotropy_subgroup A x` with operations mulS, invS, eS
satisfying [`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104) (2.7.10). Delta_G and Delta_{G^x} are
functions with `is_modular_function mul inv DG` and
`is_modular_function mulS invS DS`. The homomorphism chi is given by
chi(g h) = chi(g) chi(h), chi(g) <> 0 and continuity. A nonzero integral on
K(X) is one that does not vanish on K(X).

- [`WeilCondition.v`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v). `weil_condition chi DG DS` is Weil's condition
  chi(t) Delta_{G^x}(t) = Delta_G(t) for t in G^x; [`weil_condition_ratio`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L100)
  restates it as Delta_G(t) chi(t)^{-1} = Delta_{G^x}(t).
  (1) is [`covariant_integral_exists_iff_weil`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L208): a nonzero chi covariant
  integral on K(X) exists if and only if `weil_condition chi DG DS`.
  Its two halves are [`weil_condition_necessary`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L138) and
  [`weil_condition_sufficient`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L177). (2) is [`covariant_integral_unique_multiple`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L241):
  for nonzero chi covariant integrals nu_1, nu_2 there is a real c <> 0 with
  nu_2 = c nu_1 on K(X). [`weil_condition_factorisation`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L115) states the
  construction used in the sufficiency. Let mu be a left Haar integral of
  G^x, let mu^x be given by [`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29), and let lambda be a left chi
  covariant integral on K(G). Under Weil's condition there is a chi
  covariant integral nu on K(X) with nu o mu^x = lambda on K(G).
- [`CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v) contains the argument after the choice
  of lambda. Let lambda be a Daniell integral on K(G) that is right psi
  covariant for a continuous psi with psi = Delta_{G^x} on G^x.
  [`coset_integral_exchange`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L240) is Federer's computation
  lambda_g[w(g) mu^x(u)(gx)] = lambda_g[u(g) mu^x(w)(gx)] for u, w in K(G).
  It uses two applications of Fubini's theorem
  ([`compact_daniell_iterations_commute`](../../theories/CompactDaniellIterationCommutation.v#L44)) and 2.7.8(3) for mu. The integrands
  belong to K(G x G^x) by [`subgroup_product_compactly_supported`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L70),
  [`coset_exchange_left_compact`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L165) and [`coset_exchange_right_compact`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L197).
  [`coset_integral_unit_section`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L284) gives w with mu^x(w)(gx) = 1 on spt u.
  [`coset_integral_kernel`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L301) is ker mu^x subset ker lambda, and
  [`coset_integral_factor_exists`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L322) gives nu with nu o mu^x = lambda.
  [`coset_integral_factor_daniell`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L352) shows that every such nu is a Daniell
  integral on K(X). [`coset_integral_factor_covariant`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L403) and
  [`coset_integral_factorisation`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L422) add the chi covariance.

Sources: Federer 2.7.11, pp. 128–129, with 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–123; 2.7.5, p.
125; 2.7.8, p. 126; 2.7.9, pp. 126–127; 2.7.10, pp. 127–128; 2.6.4 and 2.6.6,
pp. 119–120; 2.5.13, p. 106. The erratum has no entry for pp. 128–129.

**Divergences.**

- *Weil's condition and Delta_{G^x} (p. 128).* G^x is the subtype with the
  restricted structure, and Delta_{G^x} is any modular function of this
  group. Weil's condition is the predicate [`weil_condition`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L59). This is an
  interpretation.
- *Constant real multiples in (2) (p. 128).* The conclusion is
  nu_2 = c nu_1 on K(X) with c <> 0. The symmetric statement follows with
  c^{-1}. This is an interpretation. The proof follows Federer. The
  coefficient r_1 of 2.7.9(1) for nu_1 o mu^x is nonzero because
  im mu^x = K(X).
- *Necessity in (1) (pp. 128–129).* The identity
  mu_s[u(g s t^{-1})] = Delta_{G^x}(t) mu^x(u)(gx) is 2.7.8(1) for mu.
  Federer leaves this step implicit. Otherwise the proof follows Federer.
- *The integral lambda in the sufficiency (p. 129).* "Use 2.7.9 to choose" is
  made explicit as lambda(u) = lambda_0(u chi^{-1}) for a left Haar
  integral lambda_0 of G (2.7.5). This lambda is left chi covariant by
  2.7.9(1) and right Delta_G chi^{-1} covariant by 2.7.9(3). It need not be
  monotone. Fubini's theorem for Daniell integrals that are not monotone is
  available from 2.6.4 and 2.6.6.
- *The function w (p. 129).* w is a preimage under mu^x of a cutoff
  function equal to 1 on A^x(spt u) (2.5.13, 2.7.10). Federer's w is
  nonnegative; the nonnegativity is not used. The membership of both
  Fubini integrands in K(G x G^x) is supplied.
- *Generality of the computation (p. 129).* [`coset_integral_exchange`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L240) and
  the factorisation lemmas are stated for every continuous psi with
  psi = Delta_{G^x} on G^x. The printed case is psi = Delta_G chi^{-1}
  under Weil's condition. This is a generalisation.
- *The function nu (p. 129).* "There exists a linear function nu" is proved
  by choosing, for each v in K(X), a preimage of v under mu^x. Federer's
  "we see from 2.7.10(5), (6) that nu is a chi covariant integral" is
  expanded. Fix f in K(X)+ and phi from 2.7.10(5). Each k with 0 <= k <= f
  then equals mu^x[((k / mu^x(phi)) o A^x) phi]. The bound of lambda on the
  positive order interval of ((f / mu^x(phi)) o A^x) phi bounds nu, so nu is
  a Daniell integral by the criterion of 2.5.13
  ([`compact_support_order_bounded_daniell`](../../theories/CompactSupportRieszRepresentation.v#L20)). The covariance follows from
  2.7.10(4). These steps are supplied.

<a id="item-2-7-12"></a>

### 2.7.12 — Compact isotropy groups; unimodular groups; G/S

Federer, p. 129. Every assertion of the paragraph is proved, under the
standing hypotheses of 2.7.11. The quotient G/S is any space of left cosets
(`is_left_coset_space mul inv S pi`) with the action A(g, hS) = g h S
(`is_coset_action mul pi A`).

- [`CompactGroupCharacters.v`](../../theories/CompactGroupCharacters.v). [`compact_group_positive_character_unit`](../../theories/CompactGroupCharacters.v#L95)
  states that a continuous homomorphism of a compact group into the positive
  numbers is constant with value 1. Here a compact group is a group with
  `compact [set: G]`.
- [`CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v), for compact `isotropy_subgroup A x`.
  [`compact_isotropy_weil_condition`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L87): if chi is positive, then
  `weil_condition chi DG DS` holds. [`compact_isotropy_orbit_proper`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L125): A^x is
  proper. [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_covariant`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L174): for every left chi
  covariant integral lambda of G, A^x_#(lambda) =
  `daniell_pushforward (orbit_map A x) lambda` is a chi covariant integral on
  K(X). For a proper A^x this is [`orbit_pushforward_covariant_integral`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L156).
  [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_coset_scaled`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L284) covers every left Haar
  integral mu of G^x and positive chi: [A^x_#(lambda) o mu^x] u =
  mu(1) lambda(u) for u in K(G). [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_coset`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L303) is the
  printed case mu(1) = 1. [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_factor`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L312): then
  A^x_#(lambda) = nu on K(X) for every nu with nu o mu^x = lambda, which
  includes the nu of 2.7.11.
- [`UnimodularGroupUP.v`](../../theories/UnimodularGroupUP.v), [`UnimodularGroups.v`](../../theories/UnimodularGroups.v).
  `is_unimodular R mul inv`: the left Haar integrals of G are exactly its
  right Haar integrals. [`unimodular_iff_modular_unit`](../../theories/UnimodularGroups.v#L40): G is unimodular if and
  only if Delta_G = 1. [`compact_group_unimodular`](../../theories/UnimodularGroups.v#L63): compact groups are
  unimodular.
- [`NormalSubgroupUP.v`](../../theories/NormalSubgroupUP.v), [`CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v).
  `is_normal_subgroup mul inv e S`: S is a subgroup with g s g^{-1} in S.
  [`coset_space_invariant_integral_iff`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L61): for a closed subgroup S, G/S admits a
  nonzero invariant integral if and only if Delta_S(t) = Delta_G(t) for t in
  S. [`normal_coset_action_uniform`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L138) and [`normal_coset_action_acts_uniformly`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L172):
  G acts uniformly on G/S when S is normal. [`normal_subgroup_invariant_integral`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L199)
  and [`normal_subgroup_modular_restriction`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L216): for a closed normal subgroup S,
  G/S has a nonzero monotone invariant integral and Delta_S = Delta_G | S.
  [`modular_kernel_normal`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L247) and [`modular_kernel_closed`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L266): ker Delta_G is a
  closed normal subgroup. [`modular_kernel_unimodular`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L278): ker Delta_G, with its
  restricted structure, is unimodular.

Three regressions. For X = G under left multiplication, G^x = {e}, so that
Weil's condition holds for every chi. For S = {e}, G/S admits a nonzero
invariant integral. The modular function of a compact group is 1.

Sources: Federer 2.7.12, p. 129, with 2.7.1–2.7.4, pp. 121–123; 2.7.5, p. 125;
2.7.8–2.7.11, pp. 126–129; 2.6.4 and 2.6.6, pp. 119–120; 2.5.13, p. 106;
2.5.19, p. 113. The erratum has no entry for p. 129.

The regressions for 2.7.11 and 2.7.12 are added with this item.

**Divergences.**

- *Compact groups (p. 129).* Federer gives no proof of "every continuous
  homomorphism of a compact group into the multiplicative group of positive
  numbers is constant with value 1". The proof supplied uses the
  boundedness of a continuous function on a compact space and the estimate
  phi(g^n) >= 1 + n (phi(g) - 1). Compactness of G^x refers to G^x as a
  subset of G.
- *Weil's condition for compact G^x (p. 129).* The fact about compact
  groups is applied to t |-> chi(t) Delta_{G^x}(t) Delta_G(t)^{-1}. This
  step is supplied.
- *A^x is proper (p. 129).* Federer gives no proof. A compact C subset X
  lies in A^x(K) for a compact K subset G, since A^x is open and onto and G
  is locally compact. Then (A^x)^{-1}(C) is a closed subset of the compact
  set K G^x. This proof is supplied.
- *A^x_#(lambda) (p. 129).* A^x_# is the Daniell pushforward of 2.5.19. Its
  covariance is proved for every chi and every proper A^x. This is a
  generalisation.
- *The identity with nu (p. 129).* "Provided mu is chosen so that
  mu(1) = 1" is generalised to [A^x_#(lambda) o mu^x] u = mu(1) lambda(u)
  for every left Haar integral mu of G^x. The existence of a normalised mu,
  and the identity for it, are proved in [`CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v)
  (entry "2.7.16 — Examples (2) and (3): covariant measures over G; 2.7.12:
  normalised Haar integrals"). The equation lambda_g u(g s) = lambda(u) for s in G^x
  combines 2.7.9(3), Weil's condition and Delta_{G^x} = 1 for the compact
  group G^x. Federer leaves it implicit. "Equals the function nu
  constructed in 2.7.11" is stated for every nu with nu o mu^x = lambda on
  K(G). Such a nu is unique on K(X). This is an interpretation.
- *Unimodular groups (p. 129).* "The left and right Haar integrals of G
  coincide" is read as equality of the two classes of functionals. This is
  an interpretation. The equivalence with Delta_G = 1 and the compact case
  are proved; Federer gives no argument.
- *G/S and Delta_S (p. 129).* G/S is any model of the coset space of 2.7.1.
  The isotropy subgroup of pi(e) equals S as a set, and the restricted
  structure on S is transported along this equation before 2.7.11(1) is
  applied with chi = 1. This is an interpretation.
- *Normal subgroups (p. 129).* Normality is the predicate
  [`is_normal_subgroup`](../../theories/NormalSubgroupUP.v#L16); Federer does not define it. For S normal, the proof
  follows Federer's alternative "because G acts uniformly on G/S". The
  criterion of 2.7.3 holds with W = pi(h M), where M M^{-1} M lies in a
  given neighbourhood of e. A nonzero monotone invariant integral then
  exists by 2.7.4. Federer's first reason, that the Haar integrals of the
  group G/S are G invariant, is not formalised; it would require the
  topological group structure on G/S. S is assumed closed, as in the
  sentence that introduces S.
- *ker Delta_G (p. 129).* The closedness and normality of ker Delta_G are
  supplied. Unimodularity is stated for the restricted group structure on
  ker Delta_G.

<a id="item-2-7-13"></a>

### 2.7.13 — The epimorphism beta^x: L_1(alpha) -> L_1(gamma)

Federer, pp. 130–131. The theorem is proved: beta^x exists, is linear, is
defined on L_1(alpha) modulo null functions, maps L_1(alpha) into L_1(gamma)
and is onto, and it extends mu^x. G is a locally compact Hausdorff
topological group (`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`), X is a
homogeneous space of G (`is_homogeneous_space mul A`) and X is Hausdorff. G^x
is `set_type (isotropy_subgroup A x)` with the restricted operations of
[`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104), mu is a left Haar integral of G^x and mu^x is a map
[`M`](../../theories/InteriorSigns.v#L30) with `is_coset_integral mul A x mu M` (2.7.10). The point gx is `A g x`,
u(gs) is `u (mul g (set_val s))`. "The K(X) regular measure associated with
nu" is a measure gamma with `is_real_integral_functional gamma KX nu` and
