`is_function_lattice_regular KX gamma`; likewise alpha for `nu o M` on K(G)
and beta for mu on K(G^x). L_1(phi) is `total_scalar_lp phi 1`, whose members
are the phi summable real functions ([`summable_real_functions`](../../theories/SummableRealFunctionUP.v#L22)).

- [`SummableIncreasingLimits.v`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v): Lebesgue's increasing convergence theorem in
  the forms used. [`summable_increasing_limit_integral`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v#L138): summable f_n with
  integrals a_n increase everywhere to g and a_n -> b in R; then
  integral g = b. [`summable_almost_increasing_limit`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v#L173): summable f_n increase
  almost everywhere with integrals a_n <= M; then some summable g is their
  limit almost everywhere and a_n increases to integral g. Also
  [`summable_total_almost_equal_integral`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v#L65), [`summable_total_integral_le_almost`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v#L80),
  [`summable_total_nonnegative_zero_almost`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v#L96) and
  [`summable_total_dominated_zero_integral`](../../theories/SummableIncreasingLimits.v#L116).
- [`CosetLoneIntegralUP.v`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralUP.v): `coset_fibre_integral mul A x beta u y r` says that
  for every g with gx = y the function s |-> u(gs) has beta integral r.
  `is_coset_lone_integral mul A x alpha beta gamma B` specifies beta^x by the
  two printed laws: `coset_lone_integral_identity` (integral B(u) d gamma =
  integral u d alpha) and `coset_lone_integral_fibre` (for each alpha summable
  u the fibre formula holds at gamma almost all y with value B(u)(y)).
- [`CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v), from the specification and the transitivity of
  the action: [`coset_lone_integral_lone`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L122) (B maps L_1(alpha) into L_1(gamma)),
  [`coset_lone_integral_unique`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L134) (two such maps agree gamma almost everywhere
  on each u), [`coset_lone_integral_add`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L149) and [`coset_lone_integral_scale`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L163)
  (linearity gamma almost everywhere), [`coset_lone_integral_le`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L175),
  [`coset_lone_integral_nonnegative`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L188), [`coset_lone_integral_absolute`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L200)
  (|B(u)| <= B(|u|) almost everywhere), [`coset_lone_integral_almost_equal`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L221)
  (alpha almost equal functions have gamma almost equal images) and
  [`coset_lone_integral_contraction`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L256) (integral |B(u)| d gamma <= integral |u|
  d alpha).
- [`CosetLoneClass.v`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v): Federer's class Phi is [`coset_lone_class`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L45); the function
  T(u) is represented by a gamma summable b satisfying the fibre formula
  almost everywhere. [`coset_lone_class_compact`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L200) (K(G) subset Phi, with
  T(u) = mu^x(u)), [`coset_lone_class_add`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L227), `_scale`, `_subtract`,
  [`coset_lone_class_increasing`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L268) (closure under limits increasing at every
  point, with alpha summable limit) and [`coset_lone_class_dominated_null`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L309)
  (0 <= v <= w, w in Phi, integral w d alpha = 0 imply v in Phi).
- [`CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v), with K(G) regularity of alpha:
  [`coset_lone_class_strict_level`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L85) (F_0, by the ramps with h = 1/(n+1)),
  [`coset_lone_class_increasing_level`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L107) (F_1), [`coset_lone_class_finite_hull`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L139)
  (F_2), [`coset_lone_class_null`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L168), [`coset_lone_class_measurable`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L210) (alpha
  measurable sets of finite measure), [`coset_lone_class_nonnegative`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L249),
  [`coset_lone_class_summable`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L305) (Phi contains every alpha summable function),
  [`coset_lone_integral_exists`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L328) (some B satisfies [`is_coset_lone_integral`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralUP.v#L53)) and
  [`coset_lone_integral_extends`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L347) (B(u) = mu^x(u) gamma almost everywhere for u
  in K(G), Federer's "mu^x = T|K(G)").
- [`CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v), with the Haar property of mu and K(X) regularity
  of gamma: [`coset_integral_positive_lift`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v#L115) (each v in K(X)+ is mu^x(u) with u
  in K(G)+ and integral u d alpha = nu(v)), [`regular_compact_density`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v#L141),
  [`coset_lone_integral_onto_nonnegative`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v#L161) and [`coset_lone_integral_onto`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v#L374)
  (every gamma summable v is gamma almost equal to B(u) for some alpha
  summable u).
- [`CosetLoneIntegralEpimorphism.v`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralEpimorphism.v): the theorem
  [`coset_lone_integral_epimorphism`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralEpimorphism.v#L74): there is B with
  [`is_coset_lone_integral`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralUP.v#L53), mapping `total_scalar_lp alpha 1` into
  `total_scalar_lp gamma 1`, additive and homogeneous gamma almost
  everywhere, sending alpha almost equal functions to gamma almost equal
  functions, onto `total_scalar_lp gamma 1` modulo gamma null functions, and
  with B(u) = mu^x(u) gamma almost everywhere for u in K(G).

Three regressions. The fibre formula for u = 0 holds with value 0 at every
point. Changing each B(u) on a gamma null set preserves both laws, so the
fibre formula can be required only almost everywhere. B(0) = 0 gamma almost
everywhere.

Sources: Federer 2.7.13, pp. 130–131, with 2.7.10, pp. 127–128; 2.5.3, pp.
94–95; 2.5.2, p. 92; 2.4.2, p. 81; 2.4.4, p. 82; 2.4.5, p. 83; 2.4.7, p. 84;
2.3.3, p. 76; 2.1.2–2.1.3, pp. 53–54; erratum p. 2 (book p. 98 line 10) for
the total-domain L_1. The erratum has no entry for 2.7.13; its entry for
p. 131 concerns 2.7.14.

**Divergences.**

- *L_1 and linearity (p. 130).* Federer's L_1(alpha), L_1(gamma) are read as
  the total-domain spaces of the erratum (`total_scalar_lp phi 1`), with
  their members taken modulo null functions. "Linear" means additive and
  homogeneous gamma almost everywhere; "epimorphism" means that every member
  of L_1(gamma) is gamma almost equal to some B(u). That B is defined on
  L_1(alpha) modulo null functions ([`coset_lone_integral_almost_equal`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L221)) and
  is unique modulo null functions ([`coset_lone_integral_unique`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L134)) is added.
  This is an interpretation.
- *The fibre formula (p. 130).* "beta^x(u)(gx) = integral u(gs) d beta s
  whenever g in G" is required for gamma almost all points y and every g with
  gx = y; at such points s |-> u(gs) is beta summable. This is the content
  of "gamma[X ~ E(u)] = 0" in the proof. A null modification of B(u)
  preserves the laws (regression), so the formula cannot be required at
  every point. This is an interpretation.
- *The functions T(u) (p. 130).* Federer's definition
  T(u)(gx) = integral u(gs) d beta s presupposes that the integral does not
  depend on the representative g of the point gx, by the left invariance of
  beta. Here T(u) is any gamma summable b satisfying the fibre formula for
  every g with gx = y at gamma almost all y, and Phi is [`coset_lone_class`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L45);
  the independence of g holds for u in K(G) by 2.7.10(1) and is carried
  through the closure arguments. This is a different proof route.
- *The method of 2.5.3 (p. 130).* The proof follows Federer: F_0 by the
  ramps with h = 1/(n+1), then F_1, F_2, null sets, measurable sets of finite
  measure. Two steps are made explicit: the F_2 case uses
  chi_{V_1} - chi_{V_n} increasing to chi_{V_1} - chi_W, and the null sets
  of finite measure are covered by F_2 sets of measure 0 by K(G)
  regularity. "Now it becomes clear that Phi equals the class of all alpha
  summable functions" is proved by the expansion 2.3.3 of a nonnegative
  summable u as sum r_n chi_{A_n}, whose partial sums increase to u at every
  point, and by u = (|u| + u)/2 - (|u| - u)/2.
- *Surjectivity (pp. 130–131).* Federer treats the characteristic functions
  of the gamma measurable subsets of a compact set C through
  F_j[K(X)], with a fixed f from 2.7.10(5), and states that this suffices.
  Here 2.7.10(5) gives, for each v in K(X)+, a lift u in K(G)+ with
  mu^x(u) = v and integral u d alpha = integral v d gamma. K(X)+ is dense in
  the nonnegative part of L_1(gamma) by 2.5.3 (gamma is K(X) regular). For
  nonnegative v, the lifts of the positive and negative parts of
  k_{n+1} - k_n, where integral |k_n - v| d gamma < 1/(n+1) - 1/(n+2), are
  summed by increasing convergence, and the contraction
  integral |B(w)| d gamma <= integral |w| d alpha gives
  integral |B(u) - v| d gamma = 0. The general case uses
  v = (|v| + v)/2 - (|v| - v)/2. This is a different proof route.
- *Hypotheses (p. 130).* That nu is a monotone Daniell integral is not
  assumed separately; it follows from its representation by gamma. The
  K(G^x) regularity of beta is not used, and existence needs only that beta
  represents mu on K(G^x); the Haar property of mu is used only for the
  surjectivity, through 2.7.10(5). Of the standing hypotheses on X only the
  Hausdorff property is used (through 2.7.10). This is a generalisation.
- *Extension of mu^x (p. 130).* "mu^x = T|K(G)" is stated as
  B(u) = mu^x(u) gamma almost everywhere for u in K(G)
  ([`coset_lone_integral_extends`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L347)); the fibre formula itself holds at every
  point for u in K(G) ([`coset_lone_class_compact_fibre`](../../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L190)).

<a id="item-2-7-14"></a>

### 2.7.14 — Covariant measures over G are Radon measures

Federer, pp. 131–133. The theorem is proved in both versions, left and
right, for a locally compact Hausdorff topological group G
(`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`), with one added alternative
hypothesis: the associated integral does not vanish on K(G), or G is a
countable union of compact sets (`is_sigma_compact_space G`). Without it the
statement is false; see the counterexample below. The assertion that carries
the printed proof, the measurability of every open set with compact closure,
holds without the added hypothesis. The measure alpha is "the K(G) regular
measure associated with" lambda: `is_measure alpha`,
`is_real_integral_functional alpha K lambda` and
`is_function_lattice_regular K alpha`. A left chi covariant integral is
`is_covariant_on K (left_multiplication_action mul) chi lambda` with
`is_monotone_daniell_integral K lambda`; the function chi: G -> R is arbitrary.

- [`CompactSubgroupCharacter.v`](../../theories/CompactSubgroupCharacter.v): [`compact_subgroup_positive_character_unit`](../../theories/CompactSubgroupCharacter.v#L99).
  A continuous multiplicative function phi: G -> R with phi > 0 equals 1 on
  every compact subgroup S. This is the fact quoted in 2.7.12 ("every
  continuous homomorphism of a compact group into the multiplicative group of
  positive numbers is constant with value 1"), in the form used in 2.7.14:
  S is a compact subset of G closed under the operations, not a compact
  group of its own. The Bernoulli estimate is [`real_bernoulli_inequality`](../../theories/CompactGroupCharacters.v#L28)
  of [`CompactGroupCharacters.v`](../../theories/CompactGroupCharacters.v).
- [`CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v`](../../theories/CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v), for a monotone left chi covariant
  integral lambda. [`covariant_positive_witness`](../../theories/CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v#L69) and
  [`covariant_character_positive`](../../theories/CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v#L102) show that chi > 0 when lambda does not
  vanish on K(G). [`left_covariant_integral_positive`](../../theories/CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v#L124) shows that lambda(u) > 0
  for every nonzero u in K(G)+, by 2.7.9(1) and the positivity of Haar
  integrals (2.7.6). [`left_covariant_compact_right_invariant`](../../theories/CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v#L162) shows that
  lambda_g u(g s) = lambda(u) whenever u in K(G) and s in S, S a compact
  subgroup. This is Federer's "Delta_G and chi map the compact subgroup S
  onto {1}": by 2.7.9(3) lambda is right Delta_G chi^{-1} covariant.
- [`CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v) contains the proof of pp. 131–133.
  Its section hypotheses are the monotonicity of lambda, the representation
  of lambda by alpha and the right invariance on compact subgroups. The class
  Phi is `open_test_functions U`. The class Psi is a sequence f_n with
  lambda(w) <= lambda(f_n) + 1/(n+1) for every w in Phi
  ([`open_test_sequence_exists`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L744)). The set S is `sequence_subgroup f`, a compact
  subgroup by [`sequence_subgroup_subgroup`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L325), [`sequence_subgroup_closed`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L338) and
  [`sequence_subgroup_compact`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L357). The set P is `sequence_null_locus f`. The set
  {omega > 0} . S is `subgroup_saturation S omega`. The condition
  rho_{f_k}(g,h) <= r for k < n is `sequence_ball f n g r h`. The steps
  are as follows. [`saturated_open_contains_ball`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L519) is the finite intersection
  argument of p. 132. [`countable_saturation_cover`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L638) gives countably many
  omega in Phi with U subset Union {omega > 0} . S.
  [`null_locus_right_translate_null`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L384) and [`null_locus_saturation_null`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L438) show
  that P cap ({omega > 0} . S) is alpha null. [`null_locus_open_null`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L687) gives
  alpha(P cap U) = 0, and [`fixed_open_set_measurable`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L706) gives
  U = (G ~ P) cup (P cap U). The theorem is
  [`covariant_open_compact_closure_measurable`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L784): every open U with compact
  closure is alpha measurable.
- [`RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v), for the K(X) regular measure of a
  Daniell integral on K(X), X locally compact Hausdorff.
  [`regular_open_measurable`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v#L113) derives the measurability of all open sets from
  that of the open sets with compact closure (fourth condition of 2.5.3).
  [`regular_inner_sigma_compact`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v#L153) proves inner regularity of an open set that
  lies in a countable union of compact sets. [`regular_inner_partition`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v#L260)
  proves it in general, given a partition of X into open sets each lying in
  a countable union of compact sets and phi(V) > 0 for nonempty open V. The
  criteria are [`regular_radon_sigma_compact`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v#L204) and [`regular_radon_partition`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v#L313).
  [`regular_vanishing_compact_null`](../../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v#L90) shows that phi(K) = 0 for every compact K
  if the integral vanishes on K(X).
- [`OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v`](../../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v): [`open_sigma_compact_subgroup`](../../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v#L136) (an open
  subgroup H = Union N^{n+1}, N a compact symmetric neighbourhood of e) and
  the orbits `subgroup_orbit A H y` of a homogeneous space
  ([`subgroup_orbit_open`](../../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v#L165), [`subgroup_orbit_self`](../../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v#L170), [`subgroup_orbit_eq`](../../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v#L175),
  [`subgroup_orbit_sigma_compact`](../../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v#L187)). For left multiplication on G they are the
  cosets H y.
- [`CovariantMeasureRadon.v`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v): [`left_covariant_integral_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L107), the
  covariant-measure form [`left_covariant_measure_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L137) (hypothesis
  `(exists C, compact C /\ alpha C <> 0) \/ is_sigma_compact_space G`, the
  first alternative being equivalent to the nonvanishing of the integral),
  and [`right_covariant_integral_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L210) and [`right_covariant_measure_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L224).
  These are derived from the left versions for the opposite group
  ([`opposite_multiplication`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L155), [`opposite_group_lch`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L175),
  [`right_covariant_opposite_left`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L195)).
- [`CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v). [`zero_integral_regular_not_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L41):
  if X is not a countable union of compact sets, the K(X) regular measure of
  the zero integral is not a Radon measure. [`subset_group_lch`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L87) and
  [`subset_group_not_sigma_compact`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L110) concern the subsets of N under symmetric
  difference with the discrete topology: they form a locally compact
  Hausdorff topological group that is not a countable union of compact sets.
  [`zero_left_invariant_measure_not_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L128) exhibits a left invariant measure
  over that group which vanishes on compact sets and is not a Radon measure.

Three regressions: the counterexample satisfies neither alternative; every
left covariant measure over a compact group is a Radon measure; every
covariant measure over an empty homogeneous space is a Radon measure.

Sources: Federer 2.7.14, pp. 131–133, with 2.2.5, p. 62, 2.5.3, p. 94, 2.5.14,
p. 107, 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–122, 2.7.6, p. 125, 2.7.9, pp. 126–127, and
2.7.12, p. 129. Federer's erratum p. 3 corrects book p. 131 line 10,
p. 132 lines 22 and 25, and p. 133 line 3; all three corrections are
applied.

The regressions for 2.7.14 are added with 2.7.15.

**Divergences.**

- *Added hypothesis (p. 131).* "Every left (right) chi covariant measure over
  G is a Radon measure" is proved when the integral does not vanish on K(G)
  or G is a countable union of compact sets. The zero integral is a monotone
  chi covariant integral for every chi (2.7.1). On a group that is not a
  countable union of compact sets its K(G) regular measure is infinite on G
  and vanishes on compact sets. Inner regularity of G, required by 2.2.5,
  therefore fails ([`zero_left_invariant_measure_not_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L128)). Federer's
  erratum p. 3, book p. 131 line 10, makes the same correction: the
  measure is associated with a nonzero monotone left (right) chi covariant
  integral.
- *"In view of 2.5.3, 2.5.14" (p. 131).* The reduction yields the
  measurability of all open sets, finiteness on compact sets, outer
  regularity, and inner regularity of open sets of finite measure. Inner
  regularity of open sets of infinite measure does not follow. It is proved
  in two ways: from the added sigma compactness, or from the positivity of
  lambda together with the cosets of an open subgroup that is a countable
  union of compact sets. These are supporting results not in the book.
- *P and "f in Phi implies" (p. 132).* Federer prints
  P = Intersection_{f in Phi} {g: f(g) = 0} and "f in Phi implies
  p omega + f <= sup{omega, f}". With Phi, P = G ~ U, so its alpha
  measurability would be the assertion to be proved, and P . S = P fails.
  Both are read with Psi in place of Phi; then P is a countable intersection
  of alpha measurable sets and P . S = P by the definition of S. This is
  Federer's correction, erratum p. 3, book p. 132 lines 22 and 25.
- *"U = (X ~ P) cup (P cap U)" (p. 133).* Read with G in place of X, since
  P is a subset of G. This is Federer's correction, erratum p. 3, book
  p. 133 line 3.
- *The choice of Psi (p. 131).* The class Psi is a sequence f_n with lambda(w) <=
  lambda(f_n) + 1/(n+1) for w in Phi. Each f_n is replaced by
  sup{omega_0, f_n} with omega_0(g_0) = 1, which keeps sup lambda(Psi). Then
  f_0 is not zero, which "S ... is compact" needs: if every member of Psi
  vanished, S would be G. The compactness proof uses
  S subset g_0^{-1} . spt f_0 instead of the pseudometric rho_f.
- *The quotient X = G/S and its metric (p. 132).* They are not formed. The
  finitely many pseudometrics rho_{f_0}, ..., rho_{f_{n-1}} are used on G.
  The finite intersection argument, in open cover form, shows that every
  open W with W . S = W contains such a ball about each of its points; only
  radii r < sup im f_0 occur, because for r = sup im f the printed sets need
  not be compact. Finitely many balls of each radius 1/(j+1) cover Clos U.
  This replaces "the topology of A^x(U) has a countable base" and yields Omega
  with U subset Union_{omega in Omega} {omega > 0} . S. This is a different
  proof route.
- *The integral computation (p. 132).* Federer uses mu^x, a Haar measure
  beta of S and Fubini's theorem. These are replaced by set estimates. For
  omega in Phi, s in S and t > 0, the function sup{omega(. s), f_n} - f_n of
  K(G)+ exceeds t on P cap {g: omega(g s) > t}. Its integral is at most
  1/(n+1), by the right invariance on S and lambda(sup{omega, f_n}) <=
  lambda(f_n) + 1/(n+1). Chebyshev's inequality for the levels of lambda
  (2.5.3) then makes P cap {g: omega(g s) > 0} alpha null. Finitely many
  open sets {g: omega(g s^{-1}) > 0}, s in S, cover the compact sets
  {omega >= 1/(m+1)} . S. This is a different proof route. The proof uses
  "p omega + f <= sup{omega, f} in Phi" in the same way.
- *"Delta_G and chi map the compact subgroup S onto {1}" (p. 133).* It is
  used as lambda_g u(g s) = lambda(u) for s in S, through 2.7.9(3). When
  lambda vanishes on K(G) the identity is trivial. Otherwise chi > 0 follows
  from monotonicity; Federer leaves this implicit. The fact on compact groups
  is proved for compact subgroups, since 2.7.12 is stated for compact
  groups.
- *Right version (p. 131).* Federer states it in parentheses. It is deduced
  from the left version for the opposite group (G, (g,h) |-> h g), where a
  right chi covariant integral of G is a left chi o inv covariant integral.
  This is a different proof route.

<a id="item-2-7-15"></a>

### 2.7.15 — Covariant measures over X are Radon measures

Federer, p. 133. The corollary is proved for a locally compact Hausdorff
topological group G, a homogeneous space X of G (`is_homogeneous_space mul A`)
which is Hausdorff and locally compact, and the K(X) regular measure gamma
associated with a monotone chi covariant integral nu on K(X). It carries the
alternative hypothesis of 2.7.14: nu does not vanish on K(X), or X is a
countable union of compact sets. The measurability of the open sets with
compact closure holds without it.

- [`HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v), section `Point`, for x in X, the
  isotropy group G^x with [`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104), a left Haar integral mu
  of G^x and mu^x = M with [`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29) (2.7.10). Then
  [`coset_composite_monotone`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L144) and [`coset_composite_covariant`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L150) show that
  nu o mu^x is a monotone left chi covariant integral (2.7.10(6)).
  [`coset_composite_nonzero`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L161) and [`homogeneous_covariant_integral_positive`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L175)
  show that nu(v) > 0 for nonzero v in K(X)+ when nu does not vanish
  (2.7.10(5)). [`isotropy_open_positive`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L219) gives beta(W) > 0 for nonempty open
  W subset G^x. [`coset_section_characteristic_value`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L233): if the fibre formula
  of 2.7.13 holds at y = g x for the characteristic function u of U, then
  beta^x(u)(y) = beta{s: g s in U}. The lemma
  [`homogeneous_open_compact_closure_measurable_at`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L260) is the printed argument.
- [`homogeneous_open_compact_closure_measurable`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L338): every open V with compact
  closure is gamma measurable. The isotropy group G^x, mu, mu^x, alpha,
  beta and beta^x ([`coset_lone_integral_epimorphism`](../../theories/CosetLoneIntegralEpimorphism.v#L74), 2.7.13) are constructed here.
- [`positive_integral_open_positive`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L77) and [`real_measurable_strict_level`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L62)
  (supporting); [`homogeneous_covariant_integral_radon`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L362) and the
  covariant-measure form [`homogeneous_covariant_measure_radon`](../../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L393) (hypothesis
  `(exists C, compact C /\ gamma C <> 0) \/ is_sigma_compact_space X`).

The regression on an empty homogeneous space is listed under 2.7.14.

Sources: Federer 2.7.15, p. 133, with 2.7.10, pp. 127–128, 2.7.13, pp.
130–131, and 2.7.14, pp. 131–133. Federer's erratum p. 3 corrects book
p. 133 line 4, adding "nonzero"; the correction is applied.

The regressions for 2.7.14 and 2.7.15 are added with this item.

**Divergences.**

- *Added hypothesis (p. 133).* As in 2.7.14: nu does not vanish on K(X), or
  X is a countable union of compact sets. With X = G and left
  multiplication, [`zero_left_invariant_measure_not_radon`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L128) shows that the
  hypothesis cannot be dropped. Inner regularity of open sets of infinite
  measure uses the orbits of an open subgroup of G that is a countable union
  of compact sets, together with the positivity of nu. The hypothesis is
  Federer's correction (erratum p. 3, book p. 133 line 4); the alternative
  of a countable union of compact sets and the inner-regularity argument
  are supporting results.
- *"hence from 2.7.14 that alpha is a Radon measure" (p. 133).* Only the
  measurability of the open sets of G with compact closure is used
  ([`covariant_open_compact_closure_measurable`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L784)). The added hypothesis of
  2.7.14 is therefore not needed for alpha.
- *"we can choose an open subset U of G with compact closure such that
  A^x(U) = V" (p. 133).* Federer gives no proof. The set U is {g: f(g) > 0} cap
  (A^x)^{-1}(V), with f in K(G)+ and Clos V subset A^x{g: f(g) > 0} given by
  2.7.10(5), applied to a cutoff function equal to 1 on Clos V.
- *"V = {y: beta^x(u)(y) > 0}" (p. 133).* The function beta^x(u) is determined only up to
  gamma null sets (2.7.13). The equality is proved at the gamma almost all
  points where the fibre formula holds. There beta^x(u)(g x) =
  beta{s: g s in U}, and {s: g s in U} is open in G^x. It is nonempty for
  some g with g x = y when y in V, and empty for every such g otherwise.
  Positivity of beta on nonempty open subsets of G^x (2.7.6) is used. The
  gamma measurability of V follows, since V differs from
  {y: beta^x(u)(y) > 0} by a subset of a gamma null set. This is an
  interpretation.
- *The point x (p. 133).* "We readopt the hypothesis of 2.7.10" presupposes
  a point x in X. For the measurability of an open V a point of V is used;
  for empty V there is nothing to prove. In the nonvanishing case X is
  nonempty.

<a id="item-2-7-16"></a>

### 2.7.16 — Examples and supporting topology

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (1): Lebesgue measure as a Haar measure, determinants, balls, boxes and simplices

Federer, pp. 133–136, from "2.7.16. Here we will illustrate" to the end of
example (1). The assertions of example (1) are proved; the subgroup H of
GL(n, R) is replaced by a direct factorisation argument and the covariance
on R^n ~ {0} is formalised in the next entry (see Divergences). The algebraic part of the
introductory remarks on affine maps and the displayed estimates for
composition and inversion are proved; the topological statements of the
introduction that are not reduced to these estimates are listed at the end.
R^n is `inner_metric_space (@euclidean_inner_productP R n)`, the coordinate
space of 1.6.2 with the dot product of 1.7.1, and L^n is
`euclidean_lebesgue_measure n` of 2.6.5 (unit mass on R^0). The Haar
notions are those of 2.7.2: a Haar measure is the K(G) regular measure
associated with a Haar integral.

*The additive groups of R and R^n.*

- [`EuclideanAdditiveGroup.v`](../../theories/EuclideanAdditiveGroup.v). [`euclidean_additive_lch_group`](../../theories/EuclideanAdditiveGroup.v#L113): the additive
  group of R^n is a locally compact Hausdorff topological group
  ([`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L56)); closed balls are compact
  ([`euclidean_closed_ball_compact`](../../theories/EuclideanAdditiveGroup.v#L74)). [`real_additive_lch_group`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L244) in
  [`EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v) is the case of R.
- [`EuclideanLebesgueTranslation.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueTranslation.v). [`lebesgue_scalar_translation`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueTranslation.v#L60): t |-> c + t
  is a measure bijection of L^1, by the characterisation of 2.5.17.
  [`euclidean_coordinatewise_bijection`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueTranslation.v#L125): coordinatewise measure bijections of
  L^1 give measure bijections of L^n, by induction over the product laws of
  2.6.5. [`euclidean_lebesgue_translation`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueTranslation.v#L162): L^n[z + T] = L^n(T) for every
  T; [`lebesgue_scalar_translation_set`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueTranslation.v#L175) is the case of R.
- [`EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v). [`euclidean_coordinate_box_measure`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v#L77):
  L^n{x : x_i in A_i} = prod_i L^1(A_i) for L^1 measurable A_i, in every
  dimension. [`euclidean_open_box`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v#L133) and [`euclidean_closed_box`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v#L148) give
  prod_i (b_i - a_i); [`euclidean_unit_cube`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v#L162) is
  L^n{x : 0 < x_i < 1} = 1; [`euclidean_compact_finite`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueBoxes.v#L182): compact sets have
  finite measure.

*Haar measures.*

- [`CompactSupportSummability.v`](../../theories/CompactSupportSummability.v). [`compactly_supported_summable`](../../theories/CompactSupportSummability.v#L53): if the
  Borel sets are phi measurable and compact sets have finite phi measure,
  every member of K(X) is phi summable.
- [`RepresentingMeasureComparison.v`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v). [`represented_borel_regular_scaled`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L177): on
  a locally compact Hausdorff metric space with a countable base, covered
  by countably many open sets with compact closure, two Borel regular
  measures representing c B and B on K(X) (0 < c) satisfy mu = c nu.
  Neither measure is assumed K(X) regular. The proof uses the strict-level
  limits of 2.5.3 ([`represented_scaled_strict_level`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L61),
  [`represented_scaled_increasing_level`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L79)), 2.5.14 for open sets, and the open
  outer approximation of 2.2.3 ([`borel_regular_open_scaled`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L128)).
- [`TranslationInvariantHaar.v`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v), for a commutative locally compact Hausdorff
  metric group with a countable base and such a cover.
  `is_invariant_borel_measure add phi` records Borel regularity, finiteness
  on compact sets and phi(tau_z^{-1} T) = phi(T).
  [`invariant_borel_function_lattice_regular`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L171): such phi is K(G) regular.
  [`invariant_borel_left_haar`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L220) and [`invariant_borel_right_haar`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L237): if phi is
  positive on a compact set, it is a left and a right Haar measure; this is
  Federer's "thus f_#(L^n) is a Haar measure". [`left_haar_measure_multiple`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L280),
  [`right_haar_measure_multiple`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L301), [`invariant_borel_multiple`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L315) and
  [`invariant_borel_integral_multiple`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L331) give psi = c phi with 0 < c < oo, by
  2.7.7.
- [`EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v). [`euclidean_lebesgue_function_lattice_regular`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L162),
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_left_haar`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L173), [`euclidean_lebesgue_right_haar`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L186),
  [`euclidean_haar_measure_multiple`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L202) and [`euclidean_lebesgue_haar_unique`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L224):
  L^n is the unique left and right Haar measure of R^n with
  L^n{x : 0 < x_i < 1} = 1 (erratum page 3, book p. 134 line 26).
  [`lebesgue_scalar_left_haar`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L306), [`lebesgue_scalar_right_haar`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L318) and
  [`lebesgue_scalar_haar_unique`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L332) are the statements for L^1 and
  L^1{t : 0 < t < 1} = 1. [`euclidean_lebesgue_integral`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L116) is the integral
  u |-> int u dL^n on K(R^n).

*The determinant formula.*

- [`EuclideanLinearMaps.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v). `euclidean_det f` is det f of 1.3.4 in the
  standard base. [`euclidean_linear_continuous`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L60), [`euclidean_det_injective`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L84)
  (det f <> 0 iff f is univalent), [`euclidean_linear_inverse`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L116),
  [`euclidean_det_inverse`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L127), [`euclidean_orthogonal_det`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L137) (|det g| = 1),
  [`euclidean_diagonal_map`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L164) with [`euclidean_diagonal_det`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L182)
  (det = c_1 ... c_n), and `euclidean_frame_map v` (x |-> sum_i x_i v_i) with
  [`euclidean_frame_orthogonal`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearMaps.v#L243) for orthonormal v.
- [`EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v). [`euclidean_linear_image_invariant`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v#L64) is the
  displayed computation f_#(L^n)[z + T] = f_#(L^n)[T].
  [`euclidean_linear_pushforward_scale`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v#L107): f_#(L^n) = chi(f) L^n with
  chi(f) > 0, together with int u o f dL^n = chi(f) int u dL^n.
  [`euclidean_orthogonal_pushforward`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v#L161) (chi = 1 for orthogonal f, via the open
  unit ball, [`euclidean_unit_ball_bounds`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v#L136)) and
  [`euclidean_diagonal_pushforward`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueLinear.v#L182) (chi = (c_1 ... c_n)^{-1}).
- [`EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v). [`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_pushforward`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v#L147):
  for det f <> 0, L^n[f^{-1}(S)] = |det f|^{-1} L^n(S), that is
  f_#(L^n) = |det f|^{-1} L^n. [`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_image`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v#L198): for every
  linear endomorphism f, L^n[f(S)] = |det f| L^n(S) whenever S is a subset
  of R^n. [`euclidean_singular_hyperplane`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v#L95) and [`euclidean_hyperplane_null`](../../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v#L117)
  treat det f = 0.

*The linear groups.*

- [`EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v). [`euclidean_general_linear`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L39) and
  [`euclidean_special_linear`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L43) are the types of f with det f <> 0, resp.
  det f = 1, acting by evaluation. [`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_integral`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L58),
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_general_linear_covariant`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L79) (L^n is |det|^{-1}
  covariant for GL(n, R) on R^n, in the sense of 2.7.1) and
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_special_linear_invariant`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L94).
  [`general_linear_action_not_transitive`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L124) (0 is fixed) and
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_origin_null`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L109) (L^n{0} = 0 for n >= 1) are the
  parenthetical remark of p. 135.
- [`SpecialLinearTransitive.v`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTransitive.v). [`column_replacement_det`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTransitive.v#L37) and
  [`special_linear_transitive`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTransitive.v#L156): for n >= 2 and x, y <> 0 there is f with
  det f = 1 and f(x) = y. [`general_linear_transitive_punctured`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTransitive.v#L177): GL(n, R)
  acts transitively on R^n ~ {0} for every n.
- [`LinearInvariantRadon.v`](../../theories/LinearInvariantRadon.v). [`no_general_linear_invariant_radon`](../../theories/LinearInvariantRadon.v#L184): for n >= 2,
  every Radon measure xi over R^n ~ {0} (the relative topology,
  `set_type`) with xi(f[T]) = xi(T) for f in GL(n, R) vanishes.

*Balls, boxes and simplices.*

- [`EuclideanBallVolume.v`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v). `euclidean_unit_ball_volume n` is
  alpha(n) = L^n{x : |x| < 1}, finite and positive
  ([`euclidean_unit_ball_volumeE`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L51)). [`euclidean_ball_measure`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L65):
  L^n{x : |x - a| < r} = alpha(n) r^n. [`euclidean_unit_ball_volume1`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L109):
  alpha(1) = 2; [`euclidean_unit_ball_volume0`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L92): alpha(0) = 1.
  [`euclidean_ball_recursion_sections`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L180): alpha(n) = int L^{n-1}{y : |y| <
  (1 - t^2)^{1/2}} dL^1 t for n >= 2; [`euclidean_ball_recursion`](../../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L224):
  alpha(n) = alpha(n-1) c with c = int_{|t|<1} ((1 - t^2)^{1/2})^{n-1} dL^1 t
  (erratum page 3, book p. 135 line 35).
- [`EuclideanRectangularBox.v`](../../theories/EuclideanRectangularBox.v). [`euclidean_rectangular_box`](../../theories/EuclideanRectangularBox.v#L35):
  L^n{x : 0 <= (x - a) . e_i <= c_i} = c_1 ... c_n for an orthonormal base e
  and c_i >= 0.
- [`PowerSegmentIntegral.v`](../../theories/PowerSegmentIntegral.v). [`complement_power_segment_integral`](../../theories/PowerSegmentIntegral.v#L77):
  int_0^1 (1 - t)^m dL^1 t = 1/(m+1).
- [`EuclideanSimplexVolume.v`](../../theories/EuclideanSimplexVolume.v). `is_convex_hull`, [`convex_combinations_hull`](../../theories/EuclideanSimplexVolume.v#L67)
  and `convex_hull_unique`; [`euclidean_standard_simplex_measure`](../../theories/EuclideanSimplexVolume.v#L222):
  L^n(S) = 1/n! for S = {x : x_j >= 0, sum_j x_j <= 1};
  [`euclidean_wedge_frame_norm`](../../theories/EuclideanSimplexVolume.v#L303): |w_1 wedge ... wedge w_n| = |det f| for
  f(e_i) = w_i; [`euclidean_simplex_measure`](../../theories/EuclideanSimplexVolume.v#L323): if S is the convex hull of
  v_0, ..., v_n, then L^n(S) = |(v_1 - v_0) wedge ... wedge (v_n - v_0)|/n!.

*The introductory remarks.*

- [`AffineMaps.v`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v). [`is_affine_subspace`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L36), [`affine_subspace_linear`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L48)
  (with 0 in S, affine means linear), [`is_affine_map`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L69), [`affine_map_graph`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L80)
  (equivalently the graph is an affine subspace of V x V'),
  [`affine_map_zero_linear`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L91), closure of A(V, V') under the vector operations
  ([`affine_map_add`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L113), [`affine_map_scale`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L122), [`affine_map_zero`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L127)), L(f)(v) =
  f(v) - f(0) with [`affine_linear_part_linear`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L137), [`affine_linear_part_additive`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L146)
  and [`affine_linear_part_onto`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L152), the isomorphism M ([`affine_isomorphism`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L167),
  [`affine_decomposition`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L158)), [`affine_linear_part_comp`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L214) (L preserves
  composition), tau with [`affine_translation_injective`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L201),
  [`affine_translation_add`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L207), [`affine_translation_compose`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L229)
  (M^{-1}(g, z) = tau_z o g and g o tau_z = tau_{g(z)} o g) and
  [`affine_translation_image`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L238) (im tau = {f : L(f) = 1_V}), and
  [`affine_univalent_iff`](../../theories/AffineMaps.v#L251) (f is univalent iff L(f) is, so the affine group
  is L^{-1}(linear group)).
- [`LinearGroupEstimates.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v), for the operator norm of 1.7.6:
  [`linear_composition_difference`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L56), [`operator_norm_composition`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L95)
  (||f o g|| <= ||f|| ||g||), [`operator_norm_composition_estimate`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L110),
  [`linear_inverse_difference`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L63), [`operator_norm_inverse_estimate`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L155)
  (||f^{-1}|| (1 - ||g - f|| ||g^{-1}||) <= ||g^{-1}||),
  [`operator_norm_inverse_difference`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L134), [`linear_group_open`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L183)
  (||g - f|| ||g^{-1}|| < 1 implies f univalent) and [`linear_group_dense`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v#L233)
  (every linear endomorphism is a limit of automorphisms, by the
  factorisation f = h o g of 1.7.3).

The topological statements of the introduction are formalised in the
entries "2.7.16 — Examples, topology" (the topological groups, and the
covariance of L^n on R^n ~ {0} with the relative topology) and "2.7.16 —
Examples, introduction: norms on A(V, V)" (the equivalence of all norms,
continuity of composition on A(V, V), the open dense affine group and
continuity of inversion on it).

Six regressions: L^0 of the unit cube of R^0 is 1 and L^0 is a left Haar
measure of R^0; alpha(0) = 1 and alpha(1) = 2; a rectangular box with all
edges 0 is null in positive dimension and has measure 1 in R^0; the standard
triangle in R^2 has measure 1/2.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 133–136; 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–122; 2.7.7, p. 125;
1.3.4, p. 16; 1.7.2–1.7.6, pp. 28–34; 2.2.3 and 2.2.5, pp. 61–62; 2.5.1–2.5.3,
pp. 91–94; 2.5.14, p. 107; 2.5.17, p. 111; 2.6.2, p. 115; 2.6.5, pp. 119–120.
Federer's erratum, page 3, corrects book p. 134 line 26 ("L^n" for "L") and
book p. 135 line 35 (exponent (n-1)/2); both are applied. Its entry for p. 136
line 7 ("f on R^n") is typographical; its entries for p. 136 line 29 and p.
138 line 34 concern example (2) and later.

The topological group structures of GL(n, R), of the affine group and of the isometry group, and the covariance of L^n on R^n - {0} with the relative topology, are formalised in the entries that follow, together with the examples (2)-(7).

**Divergences.**

- *Haar measures of R and R^n (p. 134).* Federer states that the hypotheses
  of the general theory hold and that L^1 and L^n are the unique Haar
  measures, without proof. A Haar measure is read as in 2.7.2. K(R^n)
  regularity of L^n is proved by identifying L^n with the K(R^n) regular
  measure of its integral ([`invariant_borel_function_lattice_regular`](../../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L171)).
  Uniqueness follows from 2.7.7 for integrals and from the comparison of
  Borel regular measures representing proportional functionals
  ([`represented_borel_regular_scaled`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L177)), not from the uniqueness of K(X)
  regular representations in 2.5.3. This is a supporting proof route.
- *"L is the unique" (p. 134).* The book prints "L"; the erratum, page 3,
  book p. 134 line 26, corrects it to "L^n". Corrected statement from
  Federer's own erratum.
- *"Thus f_#(L^n) is a Haar measure" (p. 135).* The principle that a
  translation invariant Borel regular measure, finite on compact sets and
  positive on some compact set, is a Haar measure is proved for commutative
  locally compact Hausdorff metric groups with a countable base, covered by
  countably many open sets with compact closure. These hypotheses hold for
  R and R^n. Federer uses the principle tacitly.
- *H = GL(n, R) (p. 135).* The subgroup H and the generation of GL(n, R)
  are not formalised as such. For det f <> 0, 1.7.3 gives an orthonormal
  base e with f(e_i) . f(e_j) = 0 for i <> j; then f o g = h o d with g, h
  orthogonal and d diagonal with c_i = |f(e_i)| > 0, and chi(f) =
  chi(d) = (c_1 ... c_n)^{-1} = |det f|^{-1}. This is the use Federer makes
  of 1.7.3; the formal proof composes the three image measures directly.
- *det f = 0 (p. 134).* Federer writes "im f is a proper subspace of R^n,
  hence has L^n measure 0". The proof picks, by 1.7.3 applied to the adjoint
  f*, an orthonormal base containing a vector w with f*(w) = 0
  (det f* = det f, 1.7.6). Then im f lies in {y : y . w = 0}, which is the
  image of {x : x_j = 0} under an orthogonal map and is null by the box
  formula. The statement uses 0 . L^n(S) = 0 also when L^n(S) = oo.
- *Determinant.* det f is computed in the standard base; by 1.3.4
  ([`det_basis_independent`](../../theories/ExteriorDeterminant.v#L239)) the value does not depend on the base.
- *GL(n, R) and SL(n, R) (p. 135).* The groups are types of linear maps
  with det f <> 0, resp. det f = 1, acting by evaluation; their topology is
  not used. Covariance and invariance are stated on R^n, Federer's main
  sentence. Of the parenthetical remark, the non-transitivity on R^n, the
  transitivity on R^n ~ {0} and L^n{0} = 0 are proved; covariance on
  R^n ~ {0} with the relative topology is formalised in the topology entry
  ([`punctured_lebesgue_covariant`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L430)). Interpretation.
- *Transitivity of SL(n, R) (p. 135).* Federer gives no proof. The proof
  constructs f by a column replacement map (determinant x_k), a diagonal
  correction of the second coordinate (n >= 2) and a shear.
- *No GL(n, R) invariant Radon measure (p. 135).* Invariance is read as
  xi(f[T]) = xi(T) for every subset T of R^n ~ {0}, not as invariance of an
  associated integral in the sense of 2.7.1; this is an interpretation.
  Federer's argument applies the uniqueness of invariant measures on a
  homogeneous space (2.7.11(2)) to R^n ~ {0} as a homogeneous space of
  SL(n, R); this needs the topology of SL(n, R), which part (1) does not
  use. The proof is direct: halving the second
  coordinate maps a box P of finite measure into itself, so the box
  Q in P ~ d[P] is null; by transitivity the null sets f[Q] cover
  R^n ~ {0}, and inner regularity gives xi = 0. Different proof route.
- *alpha(n) (p. 135).* alpha(n) is the real number fine(L^n{x : |x| < 1});
  its finiteness and positivity are proved. With the unit-mass convention
  of 2.6.5 for L^0, Federer's definition alpha(0) = 1 is a consequence.
- *Recursion (p. 135).* The exponent (n-2)/2 is corrected to (n-1)/2 by the
  erratum, page 3, book p. 135 line 35. (1 - t^2)^{(n-1)/2} is written
  ((1 - t^2)^{1/2})^{n-1} with the real square root, and int_{-1}^{1} is
  the integral over R of an integrand vanishing for |t| >= 1. The first
  coordinate plays the role of t. Corrected statement and interpretation.
- *Rectangular box (p. 136).* Federer gives no proof. The proof writes the
  box as a + g(P) with g(z) = sum_i z_i e_i orthogonal and uses the
  determinant formula; only orthonormality of e is assumed.
- *Simplex (p. 136).* The convex hull is specified by its defining
  property (`is_convex_hull`) and identified with the convex combinations.
  The reduction to the standard simplex is done by translation and the
  determinant formula, as printed. The evaluation int_0^1 (1-t)^{n-1}
  /(n-1)! dL^1 t = 1/n!, for which Federer cites 2.9.20, is proved from the
  uniform primitive criterion of the Riemann integral and 2.5.17, since 2.9
  is not yet formalised. The first coordinate is integrated out. The cases
  n = 0 and n = 1 are included. Different proof route for the evaluation.
- *Affine maps (p. 133).* A(V, V') is the set of affine maps with the
  pointwise operations, and Hom(V, V') (+) V' is represented by pairs
  (g, z); the isomorphism M is stated as a bijection with explicit inverse
  together with the linearity of L. The scalars form a numeric field.
- *Composition and inversion (p. 134).* The displayed identities and
  estimates are proved for the operator norm of 1.7.6, together with
  ||f o g - h o k|| <= ||f - h|| ||g|| + ||h|| ||g - k||,
  ||f^{-1} - g^{-1}|| <= ||f^{-1}|| ||g - f|| ||g^{-1}||, the openness
  criterion and the density of the linear group. Continuity is expressed
  by these bounds; the topological formulations are in the next
  entry, where the affine group is represented through M. Federer gives no proof of
  density; it follows from f = h o g (1.7.3) with h orthogonal and g
  diagonal with nonnegative entries in an orthonormal base, since
  h o (g + t 1_V) is an automorphism for t > 0.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, topology: the groups of linear and affine maps

Federer, pp. 133–135 and 138–140: the topological remarks of the
introduction ("all norms on A(V, V) induce the same locally compact
topology", composition and inversion are continuous, "the topological
assumptions of our general theory are satisfied"), the transitive actions
of GL(n, R) and SL(n, R) on R^n ~ {0} in example (1), and the groups of
examples (5)–(7): the affine group, O(n) and the isometry group. This entry
is infrastructure for examples (2)–(7). Here V is an lmodType with an
inner product B ([`is_inner_product`](../../theories/InnerProductUP.v#L24)) and a finite basis b ([`is_basis`](../../theories/LinearBasis.v#L40)), and R^n is
`inner_metric_space (@euclidean_inner_productP R n)` with
`@coordinate_basisP R n`. Subsets carry the relative topology
(`set_type`), and closed subgroups are groups through
[`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104) of [`IsotropyGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v).

*Hom(V, W).*

- [`HomSpaceTopology.v`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v). The space Hom(V, W) of 1.4.1 carries the topology of the
  inner product of 1.7.9: `inner_metric_space (hom_inner_productP hB hC hb)`,
  a normed space. `hom_operator_norm hB hC hb f` is ||f|| of 1.7.6, with
  [`hom_operator_norm_bound`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L99), [`hom_operator_norm_least`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L106),
  [`hom_operator_norm_add`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L116) and [`hom_operator_norm_distC`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L137);
  [`hom_space_operator_le`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L153) and [`hom_space_le_operator`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L158) give
  ||f|| <= |f| <= (dim V)^{1/2} ||f||. [`hom_space_nbhs_operator`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L167): A is a
  neighbourhood of f if and only if A contains {g : ||g - f|| < r} for
  some r > 0. [`hom_evaluation_continuous`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L202): (f, x) |-> f(x) is continuous.
  `hom_rank_one hB a y` is x |-> (x . a) y ([`hom_rank_one_operator_norm`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L286)).
  If W has a finite basis, closed bounded sets are compact
  ([`hom_space_boundedly_compact`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L332), [`hom_space_closed_ball_compact`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L376)),
  Hom(V, W) is locally compact and Hausdorff ([`hom_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L392),
  [`hom_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L194)), and its additive group is a locally compact
  Hausdorff topological group ([`hom_additive_lch_group`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L408)).

*The linear group of V.*

- [`LinearGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v). `linear_group hB hb` is the set of univalent
  members of Hom(V, V); they are automorphisms
  ([`finite_injective_surjective`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L54), [`linear_group_inverse_exists`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L102)). The group
  is the type `set_type (linear_group hB hb)`. [`linear_group_set_open`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L146) and
  [`linear_group_set_dense`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L166) (MathComp `dense`). `hom_identity V` is 1_V.
  The predicate `is_linear_group_structure mul inv e` specifies the
  operations by `linear_group_mulE` (the inclusion carries [`mul`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v#L22) to
  [`hom_comp`](../../theories/Hom.v#L165)), `linear_group_invE` (g^{-1} o g = 1_V) and
  `linear_group_unitE`; [`linear_group_structure_exists`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L198),
  [`linear_group_structure_unique`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L420), [`linear_group_invE_right`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L220),
  [`linear_group_inv_cancel`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L231), [`linear_group_group_laws`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L243).
  [`hom_composition_continuous`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L259) (composition on Hom(V, V) x Hom(V, V)),
  [`linear_group_mul_continuous`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L301), [`linear_group_inv_continuous`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L322),
  [`linear_group_locally_compact`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L379) and [`linear_group_lch_group`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L406): for every
  such structure the linear group is a locally compact Hausdorff
  topological group ([`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L56)).
- [`LinearGroupSealed.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v). The sealed module `LinearGroupOperations` chooses
  the operations. The interface names are `linear_group_mul hB hb`,
  `linear_group_inv hB hb` and `linear_group_unit hB hb`, with
  [`linear_group_operations`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v#L107) (the specification) and
  [`linear_group_operations_lch`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v#L117). Later work should use these names and
  only the laws of [`is_linear_group_structure`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L182).

*GL(n, R) and SL(n, R) on R^n ~ {0} (example (1), p. 135).*

- [`GeneralLinearPunctured.v`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v). `general_linear_det g` is det g
  ([`general_linear_det_nonzero`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L97), [`general_linear_det_mul`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L103));
  `euclidean_linear_group_action g x` is g(x), and
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_group_covariant`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L120) states that L^n is |det|^{-1}
  covariant for this action of the topological group GL(n, R).
  X = `set_type (~` [set (0 : E)])` is Hausdorff and locally compact
  ([`punctured_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L137), [`punctured_space_open`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L142),
  [`punctured_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L151), by [`open_subspace_locally_compact`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L51)).
  [`punctured_linear_homogeneous`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L266): X is a homogeneous space of GL(n, R)
  through [`punctured_linear_action`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L173) ([`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33)), with
  [`punctured_linear_action_continuous`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L180),
  [`punctured_linear_action_compatible`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L200), [`punctured_linear_orbit_open`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L229) and
  [`punctured_linear_orbit_onto`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L211).
- [`SpecialLinearTopology.v`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v). [`euclidean_det_continuous`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L75) and
  [`general_linear_det_continuous`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L96); [`special_linear`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L106) (det g = 1) is a closed
  subgroup ([`special_linear_closed`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L110), [`special_linear_subgroup`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L135),
  [`special_linear_restriction_exists`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L147)); [`special_linear_lch`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L159): with any
  restricted structure SL(n, R) is a locally compact Hausdorff topological
  group. [`euclidean_lebesgue_special_linear_group_invariant`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L176): L^n is
  invariant for [`special_linear_euclidean_action`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L169). For n = k + 2,
  [`special_linear_punctured_homogeneous`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L412): R^n ~ {0} is a homogeneous space
  of SL(n, R) through [`special_linear_punctured_action`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L189)
  ([`special_linear_punctured_orbit_open`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L339), [`special_linear_section`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L270),
  [`hom_diagonal`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L196), [`hom_diagonal_operator_norm`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L202),
  [`second_scaling_continuous`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L253), [`hom_evaluation_at_continuous`](../../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L60)).
- [`PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v), for n = k + 1. `punctured_lebesgue T`
  is L^n(T) for T in R^n ~ {0}. [`punctured_lebesgue_image`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L129): its image under
  the inclusion is L^n; [`punctured_lebesgue_pushforward`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L103): it is the image
  of L^n under a retraction of R^n onto R^n ~ {0}; it is a Borel regular
  measure ([`punctured_lebesgue_is_measure`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L119),
  [`punctured_lebesgue_borel_regular`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L161)). `punctured_extension u` extends u by
  0, and [`punctured_extension_compact`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L261) maps K(R^n ~ {0}) into K(R^n);
  `punctured_integral u` is int u' dL^n ([`punctured_integral_monotone`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L325),
  [`punctured_integral_covariant`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L351), [`punctured_lebesgue_represents`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L375),
  [`punctured_lebesgue_function_lattice_regular`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L405),
  [`punctured_countable_open_base`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L182), [`punctured_shell_cover`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L204)). Main results:
  [`punctured_lebesgue_covariant`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L430) (a |det|^{-1} covariant measure for
  GL(n, R) on R^n ~ {0}, [`is_covariant_measure`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v#L61)) and
  [`punctured_lebesgue_special_linear_invariant`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L446) (an invariant measure for
  SL(n, R) on R^n ~ {0}, [`is_invariant_measure`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v#L73)).

*O(n) and isometries (examples (6) and (7), pp. 139–140).*

- [`OrthogonalGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v). [`distance_preserving_inner`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L55): if
  |g(x) - g(y)| = |x - y| and g(0) = 0, then g(x) . g(y) = x . y;
  [`inner_preserving_linear`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L71): such g is linear; [`length_preserving_orthogonal`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L93)
  and [`inner_difference_square`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L45). `orthogonal_maps hB hb` is the set of
  orthogonal members of Hom(V, V) ([`orthogonal_mapsE`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L128),
  [`orthogonal_maps_closed`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L137), [`orthogonal_maps_operator_norm`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L155),
  [`orthogonal_maps_linear_group`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L176)); [`orthogonal_maps_compact`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L165) is "O(n) is a
  compact subset of Hom(R^n, R^n)". `orthogonal_group hB hb`, the
  orthogonal group as a subset of the linear group, is compact, closed and
  a subgroup ([`orthogonal_group_compact`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L187), [`orthogonal_group_closed`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L195),
  [`orthogonal_group_subgroup`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L204), [`orthogonal_group_restriction_exists`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L218)); with
  any restricted structure it is a locally compact Hausdorff topological
  group ([`orthogonal_group_lch`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L230)) and a compact space
  ([`orthogonal_group_compact_space`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L239)).

*The affine group (pp. 133–134, examples (5) and (7)).*

- [`AffineGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v). [`product_hausdorff`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L50), [`product_locally_compact`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L72),
  [`inner_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L102), [`inner_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L108). The affine group
  is represented through M by the type `GL(V) * V` with the product
  topology. `affine_group_map hB hb (g, z)` is tau_z o g
  ([`affine_group_mapE`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L152), [`affine_group_map_affine`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L158),
  [`affine_group_map_zero`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L169), [`affine_group_map_linear_part`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L178),
  [`affine_group_map_univalent`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L184), [`affine_group_map_inj`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L189)), and
  [`affine_group_map_onto`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L204): every univalent affine map of V is tau_z o g
  with g in the linear group. `affine_group_mul hB hb` ((g, z)(h, w) =
  (g o h, z + g(w))), `affine_group_inv hB hb` ((g^{-1}, -g^{-1}(z))) and
  `affine_group_unit hB hb` ((1_V, 0)) correspond to composition, inverse
  maps and 1_V ([`affine_group_map_mul`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L233), [`affine_group_map_inv`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L250),
  [`affine_group_map_unit`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L245)); [`affine_group_laws`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L265),
  [`affine_group_mul_continuous`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L295), [`affine_group_inv_continuous`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L325) and
  [`affine_group_lch`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L344): the affine group is a locally compact Hausdorff
  topological group. `affine_group_action hB hb (g, z) v` is z + g(v);
  [`affine_group_homogeneous`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L366): V is a homogeneous space of the affine
  group; [`affine_group_isotropy_origin`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L400) (G^0 = {(g, 0)}) and
  [`affine_group_translations_commute`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L410) (the translations commute).
  `isometry_group hB hb` is the set of (g, z) with g orthogonal:
  [`isometry_group_maps`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L429) (a map of V is distance preserving if and only if
  it is tau_z o g with g orthogonal, G = im tau o O(n)),
  [`isometry_isotropy_orthogonal`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L454) (G^0 = O(n)), [`isometry_group_closed`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L467),
  [`isometry_group_subgroup`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L476), [`isometry_group_restriction_exists`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L489) and
  [`isometry_group_lch`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L498) (a closed subgroup, hence a locally compact
  Hausdorff topological group).

Five regressions: the linear group of R^0 is the whole of Hom(R^0, R^0);
1_V belongs to the orthogonal group; L^1 restricted to R ~ {0} is
|det|^{-1} covariant for GL(1, R); R^2 ~ {0} is a homogeneous space of
SL(2, R); (1_V, z) belongs to the isometry group and corresponds to tau_z.

The equivalence of all norms on A(V, V), the topology of A(V, V) and the
openness and density of the affine group in A(V, V) are formalised in the
entry "2.7.16 — Examples, introduction: norms on A(V, V)"; the continuity of
the action of GL(V) by composition on Hom(U, V) is part of example (2)
([`hom_monomorphisms_homogeneous`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L292)). The type
[`euclidean_general_linear`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L39) of part (1) is not identified with the
topological group of this entry; its statements are restated instead.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 133–135 and 138–140; 2.7.1, pp. 121–122; 2.7.10,
p. 127; 1.3.4, p. 16; 1.4.1, p. 17; 1.7.1–1.7.2, p. 28; 1.7.6, pp. 33–34;
1.7.9, p. 35; 2.1.2, p. 54; 2.2.3, p. 61; 2.4.18, p. 90; 2.5.2–2.5.3, pp.
92–94; 2.5.13–2.5.14, pp. 106–107. The erratum entries for these pages (p. 134
line 26, p. 135 line 35, p. 136 lines 7 and 29, p. 138 line 34) concern
statements not formalised here.

##### Divergences

- *Topology of Hom(V, W) (p. 134).* Federer: "all norms on A(V, V) induce
  the same locally compact topology. In particular we can choose an inner
  product on V and norm Hom(V, V) as in 1.7.6." The topology is that of
  the inner product of 1.7.9 on Hom(V, W); [`hom_space_nbhs_operator`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L167)
  proves that the operator norm of 1.7.6 has the same neighbourhoods. The
  equivalence of all norms is proved in the entry on norms on A(V, V).
  Local compactness is proved
  by the argument of 1.7.1 for a synthesis map from a finite product of
  closed balls of W. Interpretation and different proof route.
- *The linear group (p. 134).* "The automorphisms" are formalised as the
  univalent members of Hom(V, V); they are automorphisms because
  dim V < oo ([`linear_group_inverse_exists`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L102)). The group is the type of
  its members with the relative topology; its operations are specified by
  [`is_linear_group_structure`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L182) and chosen in a sealed module.
  Interpretation.
- *Continuity of composition and inversion (p. 134).* The proofs follow
  Federer's estimates; for inversion, ||g - f|| ||g^{-1}|| <= 1/2 gives
  ||f^{-1}|| <= 2 ||g^{-1}|| and ||f^{-1} - g^{-1}|| <=
  2 ||g^{-1}||^2 ||g - f||. Local compactness of the linear group, which
  Federer leaves to the reader, is proved from its openness in the locally
  compact space Hom(V, V). Supplied proof.
- *Homogeneous space R^n ~ {0} (p. 135).* Federer gives no proof that
  the orbit maps are open (part of the definition in 2.7.1). For GL(n, R)
  the proof perturbs f by (x . x)^{-1} (v |-> (v . x) w); for SL(n, R),
  n >= 2, it uses the continuous map
  rho(h) = h o k o d((det h)^{-1}) o k^{-1} of GL(n, R) into SL(n, R),
  where k(e_1) = x and d(c) multiplies the second coordinate by c.
  Supplied proof.
- *SL(n, R) (p. 135).* That SL(n, R) is a closed subgroup, needed for it to
  be a locally compact Hausdorff group, is proved from the continuity of
  det through the permutation formula of 1.3.4. The invariance of L^n is
  stated both for the action on R^n and for the action on R^n ~ {0}.
  Supplied proof.
- *L^n over R^n ~ {0} (p. 135).* Federer's "which really does not matter
  because L^n{0} = 0" is formalised as: the restriction T |-> L^n(T) of
  L^n to subsets of R^n ~ {0} is a |det|^{-1} covariant measure
  ([`punctured_lebesgue_covariant`](../../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L430)), for n >= 1 (for n = 0 the space
  R^0 ~ {0} is empty and L^0{0} = 1). The representation of the integral
  u |-> int u' dL^n uses 2.4.18 for the inclusion; K(R^n ~ {0})
  regularity follows from 2.5.14 and the comparison of Borel regular
  measures in [`RepresentingMeasureComparison.v`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v). Interpretation and
  supplied proof.
- *The affine group (pp. 133–134).* The affine group is represented
  through M by the pairs (g, z) of the linear group times V with the
  product topology, not as a subset of A(V, V); [`affine_group_map`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L148)
  identifies the pairs with the univalent affine maps, carrying the
  multiplication to composition. The topology is the one transported by M;
  it is the relative topology of A(V, V) for every norm
  ([`affine_space_group_homeomorphism`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L512)). Interpretation.
- *Isometries (p. 140).* "Distance preserving transformations" are read as
  maps f of V with |f(x) - f(y)| = |x - y|; surjectivity is not assumed and
  follows. Federer shows |g(x) + g(y) - g(x + y)|^2 = 0 and
  |g(t x) - t g(x)|^2 = 0; the formal proof expands the single quantity
  |g(t x + y) - t g(x) - g(y)|^2 = 0. The space V may be any space with an
  inner product and a finite basis. Minor change of proof route and
  generalisation.
- *O(n) (p. 139).* Federer states without proof that O(n) is a compact
  subset of Hom(R^n, R^n). The proof shows that it is closed, as the set
  of maps with |f(x)| = |x|, and bounded, since ||f|| <= 1. Supplied
  proof, for any space V with an inner product and a finite basis.
- *GL(n, R) of part (1).* The type [`euclidean_general_linear`](../../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L39) of part (1)
  carries no topology. The topological group here is
  `set_type (linear_group hB hb)`, and the covariance of L^n is restated
  for it ([`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_group_covariant`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L120)). Interpretation.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, introduction: norms on A(V, V)

Federer, p. 134, lines 3–24 (checked on the page image):

> If dim V < ∞, then dim 𝒜(V, V) < ∞ and all norms on 𝒜(V, V) induce
> the same locally compact topology. In particular we can choose an inner
> product on V and norm Hom(V, V) as in 1.7.6. Then we see that
> composition of linear maps is a continuous operation because
> f∘g − h∘k = (f − h)∘g + h∘(g − k), ‖f∘g‖ ≤ ‖f‖·‖g‖ whenever
> f, g, h, k ∈ Hom(V, V). Moreover the automorphisms form a dense open
> subset of Hom(V, V), called the linear group of V, and inversion is a
> continuous operation because f⁻¹ − g⁻¹ = f⁻¹∘(g − f)∘g⁻¹, hence
> ‖f⁻¹‖·(1 − ‖g − f‖·‖g⁻¹‖) ≤ ‖g⁻¹‖, whenever f, g are linear
> automorphisms of V. Using the maps L, M, τ we infer that functional
> composition is a continuous operation in 𝒜(V, V), and that inversion is
> continuous on the affine group of V, the open dense subset of univalent
> maps in 𝒜(V, V), which equals L⁻¹(linear group of V).

Every assertion is proved. The statements on Hom(V, V) and the linear
group were proved earlier ([`LinearGroupEstimates.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupEstimates.v),
[`LinearGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v)); this entry adds the equivalence of norms, the
vector space A(V, W) with its dimension, and the topology of A(V, V) and
of the affine group as a subset of A(V, V). Norms are those of 1.10.5
([`is_real_norm`](../../theories/RealNormUP.v#L26)), with the metric N(x − y) of 2.2.7
([`real_norm_metric_space`](../../theories/NormMetric.v#L19)). It closes the items "all norms on A(V, V)
induce the same locally compact topology", "a topology on A(V, V)" and
"the openness and density of the affine group in A(V, V)" listed as not
formalised in the two preceding 2.7.16 entries.

*Equivalence of norms.* [`FiniteNormEquivalence.v`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v), for an lmodType V over a
realType with a finite basis e.

- [`finite_norm_comparison`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L67): N₂ ≤ C N₁ for some C > 0;
  [`finite_norms_equivalent`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L95): c N₁ ≤ N₂ ≤ C N₁ with c, C > 0.
- [`finite_norms_same_nbhs`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L110): S is a neighbourhood of x for N₁ if and only if
  it is one for N₂; [`finite_norms_same_open`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L122),
  [`finite_norms_identity_homeomorphism`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L135).
- [`finite_norm_hausdorff`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L152), [`finite_norm_closed_ball_compact`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L160) and
  [`finite_norm_locally_compact`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L176): the topology of every norm is Hausdorff and
  locally compact.
- [`inner_real_norm_same_nbhs`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L201) and [`finite_norm_inner_same_nbhs`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L222): on a space
  with an inner product B and a finite basis, every norm induces the
  topology of `inner_metric_space hB` (1.7.1);
  [`finite_norm_inner_homeomorphism`](../../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L233).

*Norms on Hom(V, W).* [`HomSpaceNormTopology.v`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v), for V and W with inner
products and finite bases. [`hom_space_finite_basis`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L41);
[`hom_space_norm_same_nbhs`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L50) and [`hom_space_norm_homeomorphism`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L61): every norm
on Hom(V, W) induces the topology of 1.7.9 used in [`HomSpaceTopology.v`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v);
[`hom_space_norm_locally_compact`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L70). [`hom_operator_norm_real_norm`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L79): the
operator norm of 1.7.6 is a norm, and [`hom_operator_norm_same_nbhs`](../../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L113) is
"norm Hom(V, V) as in 1.7.6".

*The vector space A(V, W).* [`AffineMapSpaceUP.v`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v), over a numFieldType.

- `is_affine_map_space ev` specifies A(V, W) by an evaluation
  ev: A → (V → W) carrying the vector operations to pointwise operations
  (`affine_space_eval_operations`), univalent (`affine_space_eval_inj`),
  with values affine (`affine_space_eval_affine`) and every affine map in
  its image (`affine_space_eval_onto`). [`affine_map_space_unique`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L232): two such
  spaces are linearly isomorphic compatibly with the evaluations.
- [`affine_space_hom`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L90) is L(f) as a member of Hom(V, W), [`affine_space_iso`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L107) is
  M(f) = (L(f), f(0)) in Hom V W * W, [`affine_space_iso_inv`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L132) is
  M⁻¹(g, z) = (v ↦ z + g(v)) ([`affine_space_iso_invE`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L137));
  [`affine_space_iso_linear`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L112), [`affine_space_iso_bijective`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L162),
  [`affine_space_iso_inv_linear`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L167), [`affine_space_hom_onto`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L179) (L is an
  epimorphism), [`affine_space_decomposition`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L101).
- [`affine_space_basis`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L200) and [`affine_space_basisP`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L202): the image under M⁻¹ of the
  base of Hom(V, W) × W formed by (x ↦ eᵢ*(x) wⱼ, 0) and (0, wⱼ);
  [`affine_space_finite_dimensional`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L215): if dim V = n and dim W = k, then
  A(V, W) has a base of n k + k members.
- [`AffineMapSpaceConstruction.v`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceConstruction.v) (witness: the subtype of affine maps,
  [`affine_function_space`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceConstruction.v#L60)) and [`AffineMapSpaceSealed.v`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceSealed.v) (sealed module
  `AffineMapSpaceImpl`; interface `affine_maps V W`, [`affine_map_eval`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceSealed.v#L59),
  [`affine_mapsP`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceSealed.v#L64), [`affine_maps_finite_dimensional`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceSealed.v#L71)).

*Topology of A(V, V) and of the affine group.* [`AffineMapSpaceTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v),
for V with an inner product B and a finite basis b, any (A, ev) with
`is_affine_map_space ev`, and any norm N on A.

- [`affine_space_reference_norm`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L185) is N′(f) = |L(f)| + |f(0)|, a norm
  ([`affine_space_reference_normP`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L192)) equivalent to N
  ([`affine_space_reference_upper`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L216), [`affine_space_reference_lower`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L223)).
- [`affine_space_iso_homeomorphism`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L318): M is a homeomorphism of A with the
  topology of N onto Hom(V, V) × V with the product topology, so the
  topology of every norm is the one transported by M.
  [`affine_space_norms_same_nbhs`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L663) (any two norms on A(V, V) have the same
  neighbourhoods; no inner product needed), [`affine_space_norm_locally_compact`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L332)
  and [`affine_space_norm_hausdorff`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L337).
- `is_affine_composition comp` (ev(comp f g) = ev f ∘ ev g),
  [`affine_composition_exists`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L354), [`affine_space_iso_comp`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L364)
  (M(f ∘ g) = (L(f) ∘ L(g), f(0) + L(f)(g(0)))) and
  [`affine_composition_continuous`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L381): composition is continuous on
  A(V, V) × A(V, V).
- [`affine_space_group`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L415) (the univalent members); [`affine_space_groupE`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L422)
  (= L⁻¹(linear group of V)), [`affine_space_group_open`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L434),
  [`affine_space_group_dense`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L457).
- [`affine_space_group_map`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L480) (g, z) ↦ M⁻¹(g, z) and
