  [`affine_space_group_coordinates`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L502) f ↦ (L(f), f(0));
  [`affine_space_group_homeomorphism`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L512): the product of the linear group and
  V, the representation of [`AffineGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v), is homeomorphic to the
  affine group with the relative topology of A(V, V).
- `is_affine_inversion inv` (ev(inv f) is the inverse map of ev f),
  [`affine_inversion_exists`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L574), [`affine_inversion_unique`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L579),
  [`affine_inversion_continuous`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L591): inversion is continuous on the affine
  group.
- [`is_affine_group_structure`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L605), [`affine_group_structure_exists`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L612) and
  [`affine_space_group_lch`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L631): with composition, inversion and 1_V the affine
  group in A(V, V) is a locally compact Hausdorff topological group,
  transported from [`affine_group_lch`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L344) by [`lch_group_transport`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L89)
  (supporting lemmas [`first_factor_continuous`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L55), [`second_factor_continuous`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L65)).

Seven regressions: A(R, R) has a base of 2 members; the affine group of R⁰
is the whole of A(R⁰, R⁰); every translation M⁻¹(1_V, z) lies in the
affine group; M(τ_z ∘ τ_w) = (1_V, z + w); the affine group of V in the
sealed A(V, V) with the norm N′ is a locally compact Hausdorff group; every
set containing 0 is a neighbourhood of 0 in Hom(R⁰, R⁰) with the operator
norm; the comparison constants of two norms on R¹ are positive.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 133–134; 1.4.5, p. 21; 1.7.1, p. 28; 1.7.6, pp.
33–34; 1.7.9, p. 35; 1.7.11, p. 36; 1.10.5, pp. 47–48; 2.2.7, p. 64; 2.7.1, p.
121. The erratum has no entry for p. 134 lines 3–24. MathComp-analysis 1.16
has no theorem on the equivalence of norms on an abstract finite dimensional
lmodType, so none is reused.

##### Divergences

- *Equivalence of norms (p. 134).* Federer states it without proof. The
  proof chooses an inner product for which the basis is orthonormal
  ([`finite_basis_inner_product_witness`](../../theories/FiniteBasisInnerWitness.v#L18)), bounds every norm by a multiple of
  the inner product norm ([`finite_seminorm_inner_upper_bound`](../../theories/FiniteNormUpperBound.v#L28)) and the inner
  product norm by a multiple of every norm, through the positive minimum of
  a norm on the compact unit sphere ([`finite_norm_inner_lower_bound`](../../theories/FiniteNormLowerBound.v#L33)).
  Stated for any space with a finite basis; "induce the same topology" is
  formalised as equality of the neighbourhood filters of the metrics
  N(x − y). Supplied proof and generalisation.
- *A(V, V′) (p. 133).* "The class of all affine maps ... is a vectorspace"
  is formalised by the specification [`is_affine_map_space`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L40) of a space with
  an evaluation; the subtype of affine maps proves existence and is sealed.
  Hom(V, V′) ⊕ V′ is the product space Hom V W * W. Interpretation.
- *dim A(V, V) < ∞ (p. 134).* Proved for A(V, W) with finite dimensional V
  and W, with the dimension n k + k. Generalisation.
- *Norms on Hom(V, V) (p. 134).* "In particular we can ... norm Hom(V, V)
  as in 1.7.6" is formalised for Hom(V, W): every norm, the operator norm
  among them, induces the topology of 1.7.9 of [`HomSpaceTopology.v`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v).
  Generalisation.
- *"Using the maps L, M, τ we infer" (p. 134).* Federer gives no details.
  The proof shows that M is a homeomorphism, comparing N with the norm
  N′(f) = |L(f)| + |f(0)| by the equivalence of norms; composition is
  reduced to Hom(V, V) by M(f ∘ g) = (L(f) ∘ L(g), f(0) + L(f)(g(0))),
  which uses L and M but not τ. Supplied proof.
- *Composition and inversion as operations.* Composition on A(V, V) and
  inversion on the affine group are specified by their values
  ([`is_affine_composition`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L344), [`is_affine_inversion`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L553)); the continuity theorems
  hold for every operation with the specification, and existence and
  uniqueness are proved. Interpretation.
- *The affine group "open dense" (p. 134).* Openness follows from the
  continuity of L and the openness of the linear group; density from the
  continuity of g ↦ M⁻¹(g, f(0)) and the density of the linear group.
  Supplied proof.
- *Inversion continuous on the affine group (p. 134).* The affine group
  carries the relative topology of A(V, V) (`set_type`). The map M
  restricts to a homeomorphism onto the product of the linear group and V, the
  representation of [`AffineGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v); continuity of inversion is
  transported from [`affine_group_inv_continuous`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L325). The locally compact
  Hausdorff group structure ([`affine_space_group_lch`](../../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L631)), Federer's "the
  topological assumptions of our general theory are satisfied", is
  transported in the same way. Supplied proof.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (2): Haar measures of Hom(U, V) and the unimodularity of GL(V)

Federer, pp. 136–137, with the erratum (page 3, book p. 136 line 29):
"λ(u) = ∫ u · |det|^{-n} dφ" (the book prints |det|^n). Here U and V are
lmodTypes with inner products C, B ([`is_inner_product`](../../theories/InnerProductUP.v#L24)) and finite bases
c : 'I_m → U, b : 'I_n → V ([`is_basis`](../../theories/LinearBasis.v#L40)). X = Hom(U, V) carries the topology
of [`HomSpaceTopology.v`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v), `inner_metric_space (hom_inner_productP hC hB hc)`;
G is the linear group of V of [`LinearGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v) with the sealed
operations `linear_group_mul hB hb`, `linear_group_inv hB hb`,
`linear_group_unit hB hb` of [`LinearGroupSealed.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v). det g is det of 1.3.4,
`linear_group_det hB hb g` (= `hom_det hb (set_val g)`; for R^n it is
[`general_linear_det`](../../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L92), [`euclidean_linear_group_det`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L407)). A Haar measure of the
additive group of X is `is_left_haar_measure (fun f g : X => f + g)`.

*Determinants of composition maps.*

- [`BlockDeterminant.v`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v). [`block_det_power`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L152): if X has the base
  iota_k(w_i), (k, i) ∈ 'I_p × 'I_q ([`block_family`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L85), enumerated through
  `enum_val`), and T ∘ iota_k = iota_k ∘ h for all k, then
  det T = (det h)^p. `dual_map h` is ∧^1 h : ω ↦ ω ∘ h on
  ∧^1 V = Hom(V, R), and [`dual_map_det`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L223): det ∧^1 h = det h (permutation
  formula of 1.3.4 and [`permutation_index_inverse`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L172)).
- [`HomCompositionDeterminant.v`](../../theories/HomCompositionDeterminant.v). `hom_rank_one_base hc b` ([`hom_rank_one_baseP`](../../theories/HomCompositionDeterminant.v#L177)):
  the base x ↦ e_k*(x) v_i of Hom(U, V), i.e. the base of V^m under
  X ≅ V^m; [`hom_dual_rank_one_base`](../../theories/HomCompositionDeterminant.v#L187) is the same family indexed by (i, k).
  `hom_left_composition U g` (f ↦ g ∘ f) has determinant (det g)^m
  ([`hom_left_composition_det`](../../theories/HomCompositionDeterminant.v#L250)); `hom_right_composition W h` (f ↦ f ∘ h on
  Hom(V, W)) has determinant (det ∧^1 h)^p = (det h)^p
  ([`hom_right_composition_det`](../../theories/HomCompositionDeterminant.v#L266)), p = dim W.

*Haar measures of finite dimensional spaces.*

- [`FiniteHaarLinear.v`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v). For an orthonormal base e of an inner product space
  X with a finite base, `orthonormal_synthesis e` (Φ(x) = Σ x_i e_i) is an
  isometric homeomorphism of R^N onto X with inverse [`orthonormal_analysis`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L82)
  ([`orthonormal_synthesis_homeomorphism`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L148)). Every left Haar integral μ of X
  satisfies μ(u) = c ∫ u ∘ Φ dL^N ([`finite_haar_integral_lebesgue`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L290)), every
  Haar measure φ satisfies φ(S) = c L^N(Φ^{-1}(S)) with 0 < c
  ([`finite_haar_measure_lebesgue`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L330)), and for every linear automorphism T,
  μ(u ∘ T) = |det T|^{-1} μ(u) for u ∈ K(X) ([`finite_haar_integral_linear`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L381)).

*Example (2): covariance, X ≅ V^m, monomorphisms.*

- [`HomHaarCovariance.v`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v). `hom_base_values c f` = (f(e_1), …, f(e_m)) is a
  linear bijection of X onto V^m = {ffun 'I_m → V} carrying g ∘ f to
  (g(f(e_k)))_k ([`hom_base_values_linear`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L240), [`hom_base_values_bijective`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L247),
  [`hom_base_values_equivariant`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L261)). `hom_linear_group_action hC hc hB hb g f`
  = g ∘ f (compatible: [`hom_linear_group_action_compatible`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L274)). Every Haar
  integral, resp. Haar measure, of X is |det|^{-m} covariant in the sense of
  2.7.1: [`hom_haar_integral_covariant`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L296), [`hom_haar_measure_covariant`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L317)
  (no hypothesis m ≤ n). On the right, [`hom_haar_integral_right`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L351):
  μ(u ∘ (f ↦ f ∘ h)) = |det h|^{-p} μ(u) on Hom(V, W). Also
  [`linear_group_det_mul`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L104), [`linear_group_det_inv`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L121),
  [`linear_group_det_continuous`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L176), [`linear_group_det_power_continuous`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L186),
  [`hom_det_continuous`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L148), [`det_nonzero_injective`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L55).
- [`HomMonomorphisms.v`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v). `hom_monomorphisms hC hc hB` is the set of univalent
  f ∈ X; `hom_wedge_square c hB f` = |f(e_1) ∧ ⋯ ∧ f(e_m)|^2 with the inner
  product of 1.7.5 ([`exterior_inner_form`](../../theories/InnerExterior.v#L64)). [`hom_wedge_square_eq0`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L211): it
  vanishes exactly when f is not univalent; [`hom_wedge_square_polynomial`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L271):
  it is a polynomial function (1.10.4; built from [`polynomial_constant`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L67),
  [`polynomial_linear`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L77), [`polynomial_lincomb`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L94), [`polynomial_mul`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L132),
  [`polynomial_big_sum`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L113), [`polynomial_foldr_mul`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L147)). Hence
  [`hom_monomorphisms_open`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L298); for m ≤ n, [`hom_monomorphisms_complement_null`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L351)
  (φ of the complement is 0 for every Haar measure φ, by 2.6.5 on R^N) and
  [`hom_monomorphisms_transitive`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L384) (G acts transitively).
- [`HomMonomorphismSpace.v`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v). For m ≤ n the monomorphisms, with the relative
  topology, are a locally compact Hausdorff homogeneous space of G through
  [`monomorphism_action`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L184) ([`hom_monomorphisms_homogeneous`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L292);
  [`hom_left_composition_continuous`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L89), [`monomorphism_orbit_open`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L253),
  [`monomorphism_left_inverse`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L228)). The integral u ↦ μ(u') over them (u' the
  extension by 0) is |det|^{-m} covariant ([`monomorphism_haar_covariant`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L338)),
  and its K(M) regular measure is a |det|^{-m} covariant measure
  ([`monomorphism_covariant_measure`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L355)).

*Example (2), U = V: GL(V) is unimodular.*

- [`OpenSubspaceExtension.v`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceExtension.v). Extension by 0 from an open subset S of a
  Hausdorff space maps K(S) into K(T) ([`open_extension_compact`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceExtension.v#L58)), and
  u ↦ μ(u') is a monotone Daniell integral on K(S)
  ([`open_restricted_integral`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceExtension.v#L104), [`open_restricted_monotone`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceExtension.v#L119)).
- [`LinearGroupUnimodular.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v). For a Haar integral μ of X = Hom(V, V) and
  ν = `linear_group_restricted hB hb μ` (ν(u) = ∫ u' dφ, the integral of φ
  over the open set G): ν is left |det|^{-n} covariant
  ([`linear_group_haar_left_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L268)); λ(u) = ν(u · |det|^{-n}) is a left
  Haar integral of G ([`linear_group_left_haar_integral`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L324), erratum formula);
  ν is right Δ_G |det|^n covariant for every modular function Δ_G
  ([`linear_group_haar_right_modular`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L303), by 2.7.9(3)) and right |det|^n
  covariant ([`linear_group_haar_right_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L285)). Hence Δ_G = 1
  ([`linear_group_modular_unit`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L346)) and G is unimodular
  ([`linear_group_unimodular`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L367), 2.7.12). For R^n:
  [`euclidean_linear_group_modular_unit`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L390), [`euclidean_linear_group_unimodular`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L398)
  for `linear_group_mul (@euclidean_inner_productP R n) (@coordinate_basisP R n)`.

##### Divergences

- 2.7.16(2), covariance of φ ([`hom_haar_integral_covariant`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L296)): proof route.
  Federer passes to V^m; here the covariance factor of f ↦ g ∘ f is
  |det(f ↦ g ∘ f)|^{-1}, obtained by transporting φ to R^N through an
  isometry ([`finite_haar_measure_lebesgue`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L330), [`finite_haar_integral_linear`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L381))
  and the formula f_#(L^N) = |det f|^{-1} L^N of example (1); the
  isomorphism X ≅ V^m enters through the base of X and
  det(f ↦ g ∘ f) = (det g)^m. No product of Haar measures is formed.
- 2.7.16(2), covariance on X: interpretation. X is not a homogeneous space of
  G; the covariance identity of 2.7.1 is stated for the action g ∘ f on X.
  The hypothesis m ≤ n is not used for it.
- 2.7.16(2), topology: U and V carry inner products and finite bases, so that
  Hom(U, V) has the topology of [`HomSpaceTopology.v`](../../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v) (added structure, as in
  that module).
- 2.7.16(2), null complement ([`hom_monomorphisms_complement_null`](../../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L351)): proof
  route. The polynomial |f(e_1) ∧ ⋯ ∧ f(e_m)|^2 is as printed; its zero set
  is shown φ null through φ(S) = c L^N(Φ^{-1}(S)) and 2.6.5 for the nonzero
  polynomial function P ∘ Φ on R^N.
- 2.7.16(2), monomorphisms as a homogeneous space
  ([`hom_monomorphisms_homogeneous`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L292), [`monomorphism_covariant_measure`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L355)):
  addition. Federer states only openness and transitivity; the covariant
  measure over the monomorphisms is the K(M) regular measure associated
  with u ↦ ∫ u' dφ; it is the restriction of φ
  ([`monomorphism_covariant_measure_restriction`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L223)).
- 2.7.16(2), "φ is a left |det|^{-n} covariant measure over G", "right
  Δ_G |det|^n covariant", "right |det|^n covariant": interpretation. Stated
  for the integral ν(u) = ∫ u' dφ of φ over the open set G; the
  identification of φ over G with the K(G) regular measure associated with
  ν is proved in [`LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v)
  ([`linear_group_haar_measure_restricted`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L84)); it is not needed for Δ_G = 1.
- 2.7.16(2), λ(u) = ∫ u · |det|^{-n} dφ ([`linear_group_left_haar_integral`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L324)):
  erratum correction (page 3, book p. 136 line 29); the book prints |det|^n.
- 2.7.16(2), right |det|^n covariance ([`linear_group_haar_right_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L285)):
  proof route. Federer transports φ by ∧^1 to Hom(∧^1 V, ∧^1 V) and uses the
  commutative diagram; here the factor |det R|^{-1} of R(f) = f ∘ g^{-1} is
  computed directly, R acting as (∧^1 g)^{-1} on each of the n copies
  {x ↦ ω(x) v_i} of ∧^1 V in X, with det ∧^1 h = det h
  ([`dual_map_det`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L223)). The dual space ∧^1 V = Hom(V, R) is used; no inner
  product adjoint and no identification of vectors with covectors.
- 2.7.16(2), Δ_G = 1 and unimodularity: as Federer, through 2.7.9(3) and
  2.7.12 ([`unimodular_iff_modular_unit`](../../theories/UnimodularGroups.v#L40)); the existence of a Haar integral
  of X is 2.7.5.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (3): the group of automorphisms preserving a subspace

Federer, pp. 137–138; the erratum has no entry for this example (its entry
for p. 138, line 34, concerns example (5)). The space V is an lmodType with
an inner product B ([`is_inner_product`](../../theories/InnerProductUP.v#L24)) and a finite base b : 'I_n → V
([`is_basis`](../../theories/LinearBasis.v#L40)), so that the linear group GL(V) of [`LinearGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v), with
the sealed operations `linear_group_mul hB hb`, `linear_group_inv hB hb`,
`linear_group_unit hB hb`, is a locally compact Hausdorff group. The subspace
W of V is a `subspace V` ([`ModuleQuotient.v`](../../theories/ModuleQuotient.v)) with an m-element base, given by
hW : `grassmann_pred m W`; for V = R^n and W ∈ G(n, m) one takes
`grassmann_val W` and `grassmann_proof W`. The group G of the example is
`subspace_stabiliser hB hb W`, a subset of GL(V); every statement about G as
a group holds for every restricted structure mulS, invS, eS with
[`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104) (existence: [`subspace_stabiliser_restriction_exists`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L310)).
For g ∈ G, det(g|W) is `stabiliser_restriction_det hW g` and det g is
`stabiliser_det g`.

*Linear algebra.*

- [`BlockTriangularDeterminant.v`](../../theories/BlockTriangularDeterminant.v). If Y has the base ι_k(a_i), κ_j(c_l)
  ([`triangular_family`](../../theories/BlockTriangularDeterminant.v#L180), enumerated through `enum_val`), T ∘ ι_k = ι_k ∘ h, and
  T(κ_j(v)) − κ_j(h′(v)) lies in the span of the vectors ι_k(a_i), then
  det T = (det h)^{p_1} (det h′)^{p_2} ([`triangular_block_det`](../../theories/BlockTriangularDeterminant.v#L207)). The proof uses
  [`exterior_word_span_shift`](../../theories/BlockTriangularDeterminant.v#L72): adding to later factors linear combinations of
  earlier ones does not change a product of vectors.
- [`SubspaceStabiliser.v`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v). A base of W extends to a base e of V indexed by
  m + (n − m) whose first m members form the base of W ([`is_adapted_basis`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L87),
  [`adapted_basis_exists`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L94)), and W is the common zero set of e_{m+1}*, …, e_n*
  ([`adapted_subspaceE`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L155)). The predicate `maps_subspace_onto W f` says
  W = f[W]. The set G is a subgroup ([`subspace_stabiliser_subgroup`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L289)). A
  univalent g with g[W] ⊂ W belongs to it ([`subspace_stabiliser_iff`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L318)). It is
  closed ([`subspace_stabiliser_closed`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L343), with [`linear_functional_continuous`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L183)),
  hence a locally compact Hausdorff group ([`subspace_stabiliser_lch`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L502)). The
  restriction `stabiliser_restriction g` is g|W, and
  `stabiliser_restriction_det hW g` may be computed with any base of W
  ([`stabiliser_restriction_detE`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L408)). It is multiplicative, nonzero and
  continuous ([`stabiliser_restriction_det_mul`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L545), [`stabiliser_restriction_det_inv`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L562),
  [`stabiliser_restriction_det_nonzero`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L428), [`stabiliser_restriction_det_continuous`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L452)).
  The lemmas [`stabiliser_mulE`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L510), [`stabiliser_unitE`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L515), [`stabiliser_inv_cancel`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L521)
  describe the restricted operations, and [`det_basis_any`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L48) changes the base of
  a determinant.
- [`StabiliserHomSpace.v`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v). The space X of the example is
  `subspace_preserving_maps W`, the maps f with f[W] ⊂ W. It is the subspace of
  Hom(V, V) generated by G ([`subspace_preserving_maps_generated`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L690)): G lies in it,
  and every subspace containing G contains it, since each rank one member
  x ↦ e_k*(x) e_i of X is (1_V + (x ↦ e_k*(x) e_i)) − 1_V. The maps
  f ↦ g ∘ f and f ↦ f ∘ g of X are `preserving_left_composition hg` and
  `preserving_right_composition hg`. For the restrictions g|W and ∧^1 g|W′,
  where W′ = `annihilator W` = ∧^1 V ∩ {u : u|W = 0}, see
  `preserving_restriction hg` and `annihilator_restriction hg`. The rank one
  maps x ↦ e_k*(x) e_i with i ≤ m or k > m form a base of X (criterion
  [`preserving_family_basis`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L267)). Grouped as m copies of W and n − m copies of V
  ([`preserving_left_basisP`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L353)), it gives
  det(f ↦ g ∘ f) = det(g|W)^m (det g)^{n−m} ([`preserving_left_composition_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L467)).
  Grouped as m copies of ∧^1 V and n − m copies of W′
  ([`preserving_right_basisP`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L445), [`annihilator_basisP`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L422)), it gives
  det(f ↦ f ∘ g) = (det ∧^1 g)^m det(∧^1 g|W′)^{n−m}
  ([`preserving_right_composition_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L487)). Federer's identity
  det(∧^1 g) = det g = det(g|W) det(∧^1 g|W′) is
  [`restriction_annihilator_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L584); ∧^1 V / W′ ≅ ∧^1 W through
  [`subspace_dual_extension`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L517). Also [`det_scalar_map`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L52): det(c 1_V) = c^n.
- [`StabiliserHomDecomposition.v`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v). The complement Z ([`adapted_complement`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L66)) and
  the projections [`projection_left`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L73), [`projection_right`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L82) give V = W ⊕ Z
  ([`projection_split`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L82)). The map ξ(f) = (f|W, f|Z) of X into
  Hom(W, W) × Hom(Z, V) is [`subspace_decomposition`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L135); it is linear, bijective
  and left G action preserving ([`subspace_decomposition_linear`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L142),
  [`subspace_decomposition_bijective`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L202), [`subspace_decomposition_equivariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v#L237)).

*Assertion: every Haar measure γ of X is χ covariant.*

- [`StabiliserHaarCovariance.v`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v). The space X carries the inner product of
  Hom(V, V) restricted to it, `inner_metric_space (restricted_inner_productP
  (hom_inner_productP hB hB hb) (subspace_preserving_maps W))`. Its topology
  is the relative topology of Hom(V, V) ([`preserving_val_norm`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L69),
  [`preserving_val_continuous`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L79), [`preserving_continuous_into`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L91)). Its additive
  group is a locally compact Hausdorff group ([`preserving_additive_lch_group`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L116)).
  The group G acts by `stabiliser_action g f` = g ∘ f, and
  χ(g) = |det(g|W)|^{−m} |det g|^{m−n} is `stabiliser_character hW g`. Every
  Haar integral of X satisfies μ(u ∘ A_g) = χ(g) μ(u)
  ([`preserving_haar_integral_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L220)), and every Haar measure of X is a
  χ covariant measure ([`preserving_haar_measure_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L244)). For right
  composition, μ(u ∘ (f ↦ f ∘ g)) = |det g|^{−n} |det(g|W)|^{n−m} μ(u)
  ([`preserving_haar_integral_right`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L259), from [`preserving_right_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L200) and
  [`stabiliser_restriction_annihilator_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L184)).

*Assertions: Δ_G(g) = |det(g|W)|^{−n} |det g|^m; G is not unimodular.*

- [`StabiliserModularFunction.v`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v). The univalent members of X
  ([`preserving_units`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L283), the set ξ(G)) form an open set
  ([`preserving_units_open`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L289)). Its complement is null for every Haar measure of
  X ([`preserving_units_complement_null`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L305)). The set is homeomorphic to G
  ([`preserving_unit_homeomorphism`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L358)). For a Haar integral μ of X,
  ν = `stabiliser_restricted μ`, ν(u) = μ(u′), represents γ over G. It is a
  monotone Daniell integral ([`stabiliser_restricted_monotone`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L462)), left χ
  covariant ([`stabiliser_haar_left_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L488)), right Δ_G χ^{−1} covariant for
  every modular function Δ_G ([`stabiliser_haar_right_modular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L500), 2.7.9(3)),
  and right |det|^n |det(·|W)|^{m−n} covariant
  ([`stabiliser_haar_right_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L516)). Hence
  Δ_G(g) χ^{−1}(g) = |det g|^n |det(g|W)|^{m−n}
  ([`subspace_stabiliser_modular_ratio`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L597)). This equals χ′(∧^1 g^{−1})
  ([`subspace_stabiliser_modular_dual`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L617)), and
  Δ_G(g) = |det(g|W)|^{−n} |det g|^m ([`subspace_stabiliser_modular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L645)). For
  α, β ≠ 0 some t ∈ G acts as α on W and as β on Z, with det(t|W) = α^m and
  det t = α^m β^{n−m} ([`stabiliser_scaling_exists`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L213)). For 0 < m < n the choice
  α = 1, β = 2 gives Δ_G(t) = 2^{m(n−m)} ≠ 1, so G is not unimodular
  ([`subspace_stabiliser_not_unimodular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L666)). The instances V = R^n,
  W ∈ G(n, m) are [`euclidean_subspace_stabiliser_modular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L698) and
  [`euclidean_subspace_stabiliser_not_unimodular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L709).

##### Divergences

- 2.7.16(3), X: interpretation. Federer defines X as the vector subspace
  generated by G. Here X is defined as the subspace of the maps f with
  f[W] ⊂ W, and [`subspace_preserving_maps_generated`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L690) proves that it is the
  subspace generated by G (universal property of the generated subspace).
- 2.7.16(3), topology: added structure, as in (2). V carries an inner product
  and a finite base, W is given with an m-element base, and X carries the
  restricted inner product of Hom(V, V).
- 2.7.16(3), G: interpretation. "Map W onto W" is W = g[W]
  ([`maps_subspace_onto`](../../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L273)); the group structure is any restriction of the sealed
  operations of GL(V), as for SL(n, R) and O(n).
- 2.7.16(3), "every Haar measure γ of X is χ covariant"
  ([`preserving_haar_integral_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L220), [`preserving_haar_measure_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L244)):
  proof route. Federer passes through ξ, the product φ × ψ and (2). Here the
  factor is |det(f ↦ g ∘ f)|^{−1} ([`finite_haar_integral_linear`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L381)), with
  det(f ↦ g ∘ f) = det(g|W)^m (det g)^{n−m} computed in the base of X which
  corresponds under ξ to the base of Hom(W, W) × Hom(Z, V). No product of Haar
  measures is formed, and the covariance of φ and ψ under the linear groups of
  W and V, instances of (2), is not stated separately. The isomorphism ξ is
  formalised in [`StabiliserHomDecomposition.v`](../../theories/StabiliserHomDecomposition.v) but not used in the proof.
- 2.7.16(3), covariance on X: interpretation, as in (2). The space X is not a
  homogeneous space of G; the identity of 2.7.1 is stated for the action
  g ∘ f. The hypotheses 0 < m < n are not used for it.
- 2.7.16(3), "ξ(G) is an open set whose complement has φ × ψ measure 0"
  ([`preserving_units_open`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L289), [`preserving_units_complement_null`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L305)):
  interpretation and proof route. The statement is made in X for every Haar
  measure γ of X instead of φ × ψ. The zero set of the polynomial function
  |f(b_1) ∧ ⋯ ∧ f(b_n)|^2 of (2) is γ null by 2.6.5, as in (2).
- 2.7.16(3), "γ is left χ covariant" and "right Δ_G χ^{−1} covariant over G":
  interpretation, as in (2). These are stated for the integral ν over the open
  set ξ(G); its identification with the K(G) regular measure associated with ν
  is proved in [`StabiliserHaarMeasure.v`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v) ([`stabiliser_haar_measure_restricted`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L128)).
- 2.7.16(3), "(∧^1)_# γ is a Haar measure of the additive group of X′, hence
  left χ′ covariant over G′" and the commutative diagram of (2): proof route.
  The right covariance factor |det(f ↦ f ∘ g^{−1})|^{−1} is computed directly
  ([`preserving_right_composition_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L487)): f ↦ f ∘ g acts as ∧^1 g on m copies of
  ∧^1 V and as ∧^1 g|W′ on n − m copies of W′ in X. The space X′, the group G′
  and (∧^1)_# γ are not formed; χ′ is written out at h = ∧^1 g^{−1}
  ([`subspace_stabiliser_modular_dual`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L617)). The dual space ∧^1 V = Hom(V, R) is
  used, without identification of vectors and covectors.
- 2.7.16(3), "det(∧^1 g) = det(g) = det(g|W) det(∧^1 g|W′)"
  ([`restriction_annihilator_det`](../../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L584)): supplied proof. The identity is asserted
  without proof; it is proved by the block triangular determinant
  ([`triangular_block_det`](../../theories/BlockTriangularDeterminant.v#L207), a new general lemma) applied to ∧^1 g, which maps W′
  into W′ and induces ∧^1(g|W) on ∧^1 V / W′ ≅ ∧^1 W, and by
  det ∧^1 h = det h ([`dual_map_det`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L223)).
- 2.7.16(3), Δ_G(g) = |det(g|W)|^{−n} |det g|^m ([`subspace_stabiliser_modular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L645)):
  generalisation. The formula holds without the hypotheses 0 < m < n (for
  m = 0 and m = n it gives Δ_G = 1).
- 2.7.16(3), "In particular, G is not unimodular"
  ([`subspace_stabiliser_not_unimodular`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L666)): supplied proof. Federer gives no
  element; the member of G acting as 1 on W and as 2 on Z has
  Δ_G = 2^{m(n−m)} ≠ 1 for 0 < m < n ([`stabiliser_scaling_exists`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L213)), and 2.7.12
  is applied through [`unimodular_iff_modular_unit`](../../theories/UnimodularGroups.v#L40).
- 2.7.16(3), exponents: the printed exponents were checked on the page
  images: χ(g) = |det(g|W)|^{−m} |det g|^{m−n},
  χ′(h) = |det(h|W′)|^{m−n} |det h|^{−m} and Δ_G(g) = |det(g|W)|^{−n} |det g|^m.
  The formalisation confirms them; no correction is needed.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples (2) and (3): covariant measures over G; 2.7.12: normalised Haar integrals

Federer, pp. 136–138 and p. 129. The entries for examples (2) and (3) and
for 2.7.12 prove the covariance assertions for the integral
ν(u) = ∫ u′ dφ over the open set G (u′ the extension of u by 0) and the
identity of 2.7.12 for every μ with μ(1) = 1. This entry proves the
assertions for the measures themselves and the existence of a normalised μ.
The reservations "not formalised" concerning φ over G, γ over G, the
measure over the monomorphisms, (∧^1)_# φ and the normalisation μ(1) = 1 in
those entries no longer apply.

*Restriction of a measure to an open subspace.*

- [`InnerSpaceCountableBase.v`](../../theories/InnerSpaceCountableBase.v). [`inner_space_countable_open_base`](../../theories/InnerSpaceCountableBase.v#L43): an inner
  product space with a finite base has a countable base, the inverse images
  under Φ^{-1} of a countable base of R^N, Φ the isometry given by an
  orthonormal base ([`orthonormal_synthesis_homeomorphism`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L148)).
- [`OpenSubspaceMeasure.v`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v). Let T be a locally compact Hausdorff metric space
  with a countable base and S an open subset of T with the relative topology
  (`set_type S`). The restriction of φ to S is `open_subspace_measure phi`,
  T′ ↦ φ(T′) for T′ ⊂ S. Supporting lemmas: [`open_compact_neighbourhood`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L80),
  [`pseudometric_open_subspace_locally_compact`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L101),
  [`open_subspace_countable_open_base`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L118), [`open_subspace_compact_cover`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L141),
  [`open_subspace_measure_is_measure`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L184), [`open_subspace_measure_pushforward`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L197)
  (the image under the inclusion is φ ⌞ S, 2.1.2),
  [`open_subspace_borel_regular`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L229) (2.2.3) and [`open_subspace_measure_represents`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L255)
  (∫ u d(φ over S) = ∫ u′ d(φ ⌞ S) = ∫ u′ dφ, by 2.4.18 and 2.4.10).
  [`open_subspace_measure_associated`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L298): if φ is K(T) regular and represents a
  monotone Daniell integral μ on K(T), then the restriction is the K(S)
  regular measure associated with ν(u) = μ(u′): it is a measure, represents
  ν, is K(S) regular and a Radon measure, and every K(S) regular measure
  representing ν equals it. The proof compares the restriction with the
  K(S) regular measure representing ν, a Radon measure by 2.5.14, through
  [`represented_borel_regular_scaled`](../../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L177). [`open_subspace_covariant_measure`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L340): if ν
  satisfies ν(u ∘ A_g) = χ(g) ν(u), the restriction is a χ covariant
  measure (2.7.1).

*Example (2).*

- [`LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v). φ is a Haar measure of the additive group of
  H = Hom(V, V) (`is_left_haar_measure (fun f g : H => f + g) phi`) and
  φ over G is `open_subspace_measure (S := linear_group hB hb) phi`.
  [`linear_group_haar_measure_restricted`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L84): it is the K(G) regular measure
  associated with `linear_group_restricted hB hb mu`, and a Radon measure.
  [`linear_group_haar_measure_left_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L103): "φ is a left |det|^{-n}
  covariant measure over G" (`is_left_covariant_measure mul
  (linear_group_character hB hb)`). `linear_group_haar_measure_left_haar`:
  λ(u) = ∫ u · |det|^{-n} d(φ over G) is a left Haar integral of G (erratum
  exponent). [`linear_group_haar_measure_right_modular`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L137): "φ is right
  Δ_G |det|^n covariant over G", for every modular function Δ_G.
  [`linear_group_haar_measure_right_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L155): "φ is right |det|^n
  covariant" ([`is_right_covariant_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L134)). For U, V with dim U = m:
  [`monomorphism_haar_measure_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L208), the restriction of a Haar measure
  of Hom(U, V) to the open set of monomorphisms is a |det|^{-m} covariant
  measure for [`monomorphism_action`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L184), and
  [`monomorphism_covariant_measure_restriction`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L223), the measure of
  [`monomorphism_covariant_measure`](../../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L355) is this restriction. Countable bases:
  [`hom_endomorphism_countable_open_base`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L67), [`hom_space_countable_open_base`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L190).
- [`DualMapHaarMeasure.v`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v). ∧^1 V = Hom(V, R) carries the inner product
  `hom_inner_productP hB (scalar_inner_productP R) hb` of 1.7.9 and the dual
  base `dual_basisP hb`; `exterior_one_fun h` is ∧^1 h, ω ↦ ω ∘ h
  ([`exterior_one_funE`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L175), through [`dual_map`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L208)). [`finite_linear_continuous`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L70): a
  linear map of a finite dimensional inner product space is continuous
  (|L x| ≤ (Σ_i |L e_i|) |x|, 1.10.5). [`additive_homeomorphism_haar_measure`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L125):
  an additive homeomorphism carries Haar measures to Haar measures (2.7.2,
  2.5.19). [`exterior_one_homeomorphism`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L245): ∧^1 is a linear homeomorphism of
  Hom(V, V) onto Hom(∧^1 V, ∧^1 V) with inverse
  k ↦ (x ↦ Σ_i k(b*_i)(x) b_i) ([`exterior_one_inverseK`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L220),
  [`exterior_one_funK`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L230)). [`exterior_one_linear_group`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L264): ∧^1 maps G onto G′.
  [`exterior_one_comp`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L285), [`exterior_one_identity`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L295) and [`exterior_one_diagram`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L306):
  ∧^1(h g^{-1}) = ∧^1(g^{-1}) ∘ ∧^1 h with ∧^1(g^{-1}) = (∧^1 g)^{-1}, the
  commutative diagram. [`exterior_one_inverse_det`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L326):
  |det(∧^1 g)^{-1}|^{-n} = |det g|^n. [`exterior_one_haar_measure`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L341):
  "(∧^1)_# φ is a Haar measure of the additive group of
  Hom(∧^1 V, ∧^1 V)". [`exterior_one_haar_measure_left_covariant`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L355): its
  restriction to G′ is a left |det|^{-n} covariant measure.

*Example (3).*

- [`StabiliserHaarMeasure.v`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v). γ is a Haar measure of the additive group of X
  and γ over G is `stabiliser_haar_restriction gamma`, T ↦ γ(ι[T]) for the
  inclusion ι = [`stabiliser_inclusion`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L83) of G into X.
  [`stabiliser_haar_restriction_pushforward`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L105): it is the image of the
  restriction of γ to the open set ξ(G) ([`preserving_units`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L283)) under the
  homeomorphism [`preserving_unit_map`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L346) onto G.
  [`stabiliser_haar_measure_restricted`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L128): it is the K(G) regular measure
  associated with `stabiliser_restricted hW mu`. For every restricted group
  structure of G: [`stabiliser_haar_measure_left_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L169) (γ is a left χ
  covariant measure over G), [`stabiliser_haar_measure_right_modular`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L182) ("every
  Haar measure γ of X is right Δ_G χ^{-1} covariant over G", every modular
  function Δ_G) and [`stabiliser_haar_measure_right_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L200) (right
  |det|^n |det(·|W)|^{m−n} covariant measure). Countable base:
  [`preserving_countable_open_base`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L94).

*2.7.12.*

- [`CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v), under the standing hypotheses of
  [`CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v) (compact G^x).
  [`compact_isotropy_normalised_haar_exists`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L68): G^x has a left Haar integral μ
  with μ(1) = 1, namely λ′(1)^{-1} λ′ for a left Haar integral λ′ (2.7.5;
  [`left_haar_integral_total_positive`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L127), [`nonzero_invariant_integral_scale`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L75)).
  [`compact_isotropy_normalised_coset`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L92): for this μ and the epimorphism μ^x
  ([`coset_integral_exists`](../../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L155)), [A^x_#(λ) ∘ μ^x] u = λ(u) for u ∈ K(G).
  [`compact_isotropy_normalised_factor`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L113): if X is locally compact, then
  moreover the function ν of 2.7.11 with ν ∘ μ^x = λ exists, is a χ
  covariant integral, and A^x_#(λ) = ν on K(X).

Regressions: the restriction to the empty open set is 0 and the image of
the restriction to T is φ; for n = 0 the restriction of φ to G is right
invariant; for m = dim W = 0 γ over G is left |det|^{-n} covariant;
∧^1 1_V = 1; for X = G under left multiplication, G^x = {e} has a
normalised left Haar integral.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 136–138, and 2.7.12, p. 129, with 2.1.2, p. 54;
2.2.3, p. 61; 2.4.10, p. 85; 2.4.18, p. 90; 2.5.3, p. 94; 2.5.14, p. 107;
2.5.19, p. 113; 2.7.1–2.7.2, p. 122; 2.7.5, p. 125; 2.7.9–2.7.11, pp. 126–129;
1.4.1, p. 17; 1.7.9, p. 35; 1.10.5, p. 48. Erratum: page 3, book p. 136 line
29 (exponent of λ); no entry for pp. 129, 137, 138 concerns these assertions.

##### Divergences

- 2.7.16(2), (3), "φ ... over G", "γ ... over G"
  ([`open_subspace_measure`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L51), [`stabiliser_haar_restriction`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L88)): interpretation.
  φ over G is the restriction T ↦ φ(T) to subsets of the open set G, a
  measure over G with the relative topology; in (3) G is identified with the
  open set ξ(G) of X by the inclusion ι, and γ over G is T ↦ γ(ι[T]). The
  identification with the K(G) regular measure associated with
  ν(u) = ∫ u′ dφ is proved ([`linear_group_haar_measure_restricted`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L84),
  [`stabiliser_haar_measure_restricted`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L128)).
- Restriction to an open subspace ([`open_subspace_measure_associated`](../../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L298)):
  supplied proof, with hypotheses made explicit. Federer passes from φ to
  φ over G without comment. The space is assumed metric, locally compact
  Hausdorff and with a countable base, and φ K(T) regular and representing
  a monotone Daniell integral; Haar measures satisfy the last two by
  definition, and the spaces Hom(U, V) and X the first ones
  ([`inner_space_countable_open_base`](../../theories/InnerSpaceCountableBase.v#L43)). The countable base is used for
  2.5.14 and the metric for the comparison of Borel regular measures
  (2.2.3).
- 2.7.16(2), "therefore φ is right |det|^n covariant"
  ([`linear_group_haar_measure_right_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L155)): proof route. The steps of
  Federer's argument are formalised separately: ∧^1 maps G onto G′
  ([`exterior_one_linear_group`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L264)), (∧^1)_# φ is a Haar measure and left
  |det|^{-n} covariant over G′ ([`exterior_one_haar_measure`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L341),
  [`exterior_one_haar_measure_left_covariant`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L355)), the diagram commutes
  ([`exterior_one_diagram`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L306)) and |det(∧^1 g)^{-1}|^{-n} = |det g|^n
  ([`exterior_one_inverse_det`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L326)). The conclusion itself follows from the
  direct computation of det(f ↦ f ∘ g^{-1}) in [`LinearGroupUnimodular.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v),
  not from transporting the covariance over G′ back to G.
- 2.7.16(2), ∧^1 V: interpretation. ∧^1 V is the dual space Hom(V, R); its
  inner product (1.7.9, from B and the scalar product of R) only fixes the
  topology of Hom(∧^1 V, ∧^1 V). No vector is identified with a covector;
  the inverse of ∧^1 is written with the dual base.
- 2.7.16(2), "∧^1 ... maps G onto G′" ([`exterior_one_linear_group`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L264)) and
  "(∧^1)_# φ is a Haar measure" ([`exterior_one_haar_measure`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L341)): supplied
  proofs. Federer gives no argument. The first uses det ∧^1 h = det h
  ([`dual_map_det`](../../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L223)) and [`det_nonzero_injective`](../../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L55); the second uses that ∧^1 is
  additive, bijective and, with its inverse, continuous
  ([`finite_linear_continuous`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L70)), and [`additive_homeomorphism_haar_measure`](../../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L125).
- 2.7.16(2), monomorphisms ([`monomorphism_haar_measure_covariant`](../../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L208)):
  addition. Federer does not state the covariance of φ over the set of
  monomorphisms; the hypothesis m ≤ n is not used for it.
- 2.7.16(3), "γ ... left χ covariant over G"
  ([`stabiliser_haar_measure_left_covariant`](../../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L169)): Federer uses this implicitly
  in "Therefore every Haar measure γ of X is right Δ_G χ^{-1} covariant over
  G". The image measure (∧^1)_# γ, X′ and G′ are not formed, as in the entry
  for part (3).
- 2.7.12, "provided μ is chosen so that μ(1) = 1"
  ([`compact_isotropy_normalised_haar_exists`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L68)): supplied proof of
  existence, by scaling a left Haar integral of the compact group G^x.
- 2.7.12, "equals the function ν constructed in 2.7.11"
  ([`compact_isotropy_normalised_factor`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L113)): supplied steps. ν is obtained
  from [`coset_integral_factor_exists`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L322) with Weil's condition
  ([`compact_isotropy_weil_condition`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L87)) and the right Δ_G χ^{-1} covariance of
  λ (2.7.9(3)). Local compactness of X, a standing hypothesis of 2.7.1,
  appears as an explicit hypothesis because [`CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v`](../../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v)
  assumes only that X is Hausdorff.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (4): Weil's condition on G(n, m) and simple m-vectors

Federer, p. 138, example (4), from "For 0 < m < n, the action of GL(n, R) on
G(n, m)" to "equals |det|^m". The display dropped by the OCR was read from
the page image: Weil's condition becomes
χ(t) · |det(t|W)|^{-n} · |det t|^m = 1 for t ∈ GL(n, R)^W. The erratum has no
entry for this example. Both assertions are proved, together with the
commutator-subgroup claim. Here V is an lmodType with an inner product B
([`is_inner_product`](../../theories/InnerProductUP.v#L24)) and a finite base b : 'I_n → V, GL(V) is the linear
group of [`LinearGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v) with the sealed operations `linear_group_mul hB hb`,
`linear_group_inv hB hb`, `linear_group_unit hB hb`, and det g is
`linear_group_det hB hb g`. G(V, m) is `grassmann_space hC hB hc hb` of
[`GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v) (the auxiliary space U carries a base c of m vectors); for
R^n one takes the inner product of 1.7.1, the standard bases and
`euclidean_grassmann_space R n m`.

*G(V, m) as a homogeneous space of GL(V).*

- [`SubgroupOrbitOpen.v`](../../theories/SubgroupOrbitOpen.v). `subgroup_orbit_open`: if a subgroup S of G
  containing e acts by the restriction of A and its orbit maps are open, so
  are those of G. [`homogeneous_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/SubgroupOrbitOpen.v#L82): a Hausdorff
  homogeneous space of a locally compact Hausdorff group is locally compact.
- [`LinearGrassmannAction.v`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v). `linear_grassmann_action hC hB hc hb g W` is g[W].
  [`grassmann_projection_difference_bound`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L93): if Q maps V into M′ and fixes M′,
  and Q₀ maps V into M and fixes M, then ||P_{M′} − P_M|| ≤ 2 ||Q − Q₀||.
  With Q = g ∘ P_W ∘ g^{-1} ([`linear_grassmann_conjugate`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L191)) this gives
  [`linear_grassmann_projection_continuous`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L245) and
  [`linear_grassmann_action_continuous`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L265). Also
  [`linear_grassmann_action_compatible`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L272), [`linear_grassmann_action_transitive`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L291),
  [`linear_grassmann_orbit_open`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L302) (from O(V)),
  [`linear_grassmann_homogeneous`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L317), [`linear_grassmann_locally_compact`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L331), and
  [`linear_grassmann_isotropy`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L343): the isotropy group of W is
  `subspace_stabiliser hB hb (grassmann_val W)`, the group of example (3).

*The commutator subgroup.* [`SpecialLinearCommutators.v`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v). `commutator hB hb a c`
is a c a^{-1} c^{-1}; `commutator_subgroup hB hb` is the intersection of all
subgroups containing every commutator. [`special_linear_commutator`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L465):
det g = 1 if and only if g lies in the commutator subgroup.
[`special_linear_character_unit`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L482): χ(g) = 1 whenever det g = 1, for every
function χ : GL(V) → R with χ(gh) = χ(g) χ(h) and χ(g) ≠ 0. Supporting
lemmas: [`det_fix_all_but_one`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L60) (if f fixes e_i for i ≠ i₀, then
det f = e_{i₀}*(f(e_{i₀}))), [`transvection_commutator`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L228), [`column_step`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L353),
[`column_reduction_exists`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L416).

*No covariant integral on G(n, m).* [`GroupIsomorphismModular.v`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v):
[`isomorphism_modular_function`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L93) (Δ_H = Δ_S ∘ φ for a homeomorphic
isomorphism φ), [`equal_subgroups_modular`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L239) (two restricted structures on equal
closed subgroups), [`nonzero_integral_point`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L260). [`GrassmannWeilCondition.v`](../../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v):

- [`grassmann_weil_condition`](../../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v#L81): for a continuous homomorphism χ, a nonzero
  χ covariant integral on K(G(V, m)) exists if and only if
  χ(t) |det(t|W)|^{-n} |det t|^m = 1 for every t in the stabiliser of W.
- [`grassmann_no_covariant_integral`](../../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v#L120): for 0 < m < n and every function
  χ : GL(V) → R there is no nonzero χ covariant integral;
  [`euclidean_grassmann_no_covariant_integral`](../../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v#L167) is the instance for R^n.

*Nonzero simple m-vectors.* The space X is `set_type (nonzero_simple_set hB m)`,
the nonzero simple m-vectors (1.6.1) with the relative topology of ∧_m V and
the inner product [`exterior_inner_form`](../../theories/InnerExterior.v#L64) of 1.7.5; GL(V) acts by
`simple_vector_action hB hb m g ξ` = (∧_m g) ξ ([`exterior_group_map`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L163)).

- [`SimpleVectorAction.v`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v). [`word_image_continuous`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L105) (f ↦ f(v_1) ∧ ⋯ ∧ f(v_m) is
  continuous, its squared distance from the value at f₀ being a polynomial
  function), [`exterior_group_map_bound`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L244) (|(∧_m g) ξ| ≤ ||g||^m |ξ|),
  [`exterior_action_continuous_at`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L256), [`simple_vector_action_continuous`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L299),
  [`simple_vector_action_compatible`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L315), [`simple_vector_action_transitive`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L335),
  [`simple_vector_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/SimpleVectorAction.v#L360).
- [`SimpleVectorFrames.v`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v). [`diagonal_permutation_sum`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v#L42), [`word_diagonal_scale`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v#L68),
  [`word_change_basis`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v#L99), [`orthonormal_word_norm`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v#L141), [`unit_simple_frame`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v#L155) (a simple
  m-vector of norm 1 is the product of an orthonormal family, m > 0),
  [`orthonormal_frames_orthogonal`](../../theories/SimpleVectorFrames.v#L212).
- [`SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v). [`orbit_open_from_point`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L62); [`unit_simple_homogeneous`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L224)
  (O(V) on the simple m-vectors of norm 1); [`normalise`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L288), [`frame_point`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L324),
  [`frame_dilation`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L357), [`frame_orbit_nbhs`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L408); [`simple_vector_homogeneous`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L466) and
  [`simple_vector_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L491), for 0 < m ≤ n.
- [`SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v). With W the associated subspace of ξ:
  [`simple_isotropy_stabiliser`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L90), [`simple_stabiliser_word`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L109)
  ((∧_m g) ξ = det(g|W) ξ for g in the stabiliser of W),
  [`simple_isotropy_det`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L142) and [`simple_isotropy_characterisation`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L161) (the isotropy
  group at ξ consists of the g with g(W) = W and det(g|W) = 1);
  [`simple_det_kernel_normal`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L181) and [`simple_det_kernel_closed`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L197) (a closed normal
  subgroup of the group of example (3)); [`simple_isotropy_modular`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L262)
  (Δ_{G^ξ}(t) = |det t|^m); [`simple_vector_covariant_integral`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L324): for
  0 < m ≤ n a nonzero |det|^{-m} covariant integral on K(X) exists;
  [`euclidean_simple_vector_covariant_integral`](../../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L370) is the instance for R^n.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, p. 138, with 1.3.1–1.3.4, pp. 13–16, 1.6.1–1.6.2, pp.
23–24, 1.7.1–1.7.6, pp. 28–34, 1.10.4, p. 46, 2.7.1, pp. 121–122, 2.7.8, p.
126, 2.7.10–2.7.12, pp. 127–129, and examples (2), (3) and (6) of 2.7.16, pp.
136–139. The erratum has no entry for this example.

##### Divergences

- *Homogeneous spaces (p. 138).* Federer presupposes that G(n, m) and the
  space of nonzero simple m-vectors are homogeneous spaces of GL(n, R) in
  the sense of 2.7.1. The proofs are supplied: continuity on G(n, m) through
  [`grassmann_projection_difference_bound`](../../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L93), openness inherited from O(n);
  for the simple m-vectors, continuity through the operator bound of 1.7.6
  and polynomial functions of 1.10.4, openness from the action of O(n) on
  the simple m-vectors of norm 1 together with the maps d_r multiplying one
  vector of an orthonormal frame by r. The space of simple m-vectors carries
  the relative topology of ∧_m R^n with the norm of 1.7.5. Interpretation and
  supplied proofs.
- *"For any homomorphism χ" (p. 138).* The nonexistence is stated for every
  function χ : GL(n, R) → R; a nonzero χ covariant integral forces χ to be
  a continuous homomorphism (2.7.1, [`covariant_character_homomorphism`](../../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v#L116)).
  Generalisation.
- *"Weil's condition becomes" (p. 138).* Formalised as an equivalence for
  continuous homomorphisms χ ([`grassmann_weil_condition`](../../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v#L81)), 2.7.11(1) being
  applied to the isotropy group of W, which equals the stabiliser of example
  (3) as a set; the modular functions are compared by
  [`equal_subgroups_modular`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L239). Interpretation.
- *Commutator subgroup (p. 138).* Federer asserts without proof that
  {g : det g = 1} is the commutator subgroup. The commutator subgroup is
  the intersection of all subgroups containing the commutators. Proof
  supplied: a transvection x ↦ x + φ(x) v with φ(v) = 0 is the commutator
  D T D^{-1} T^{-1} for D(x) = x + ψ(x) v, ψ(v) = 1; a map with determinant 1
  is reduced to 1_V column by column with at most four transvections per
  column, the last coordinate being the determinant ([`det_fix_all_but_one`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L60)).
  Both inclusions are proved; continuity of χ is not needed.
- *"det(t|W) ≠ 1" (p. 138).* Correction: Weil's condition fails at t with
  det t = 1 only if |det(t|W)| ≠ 1; for det(t|W) = −1 the display holds,
  since |det(t|W)|^{-n} = 1. The element used acts as 2^{n−m} on W and as
  2^{-m} on a complement ([`stabiliser_scaling_exists`](../../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L213)), so that det t = 1 and
  |det(t|W)| = 2^{m(n−m)}.
- *Simple m-vectors (p. 138).* The existence of the covariant integral is
  proved for 0 < m ≤ n, inside the context 0 < m < n of example (4); m ≤ n
  makes the space nonempty, and m > 0 is used for the frames of the openness
  proof. "Endomorphisms g of R^n" are members of GL(n, R). The isotropy
  characterisation and the modular function hold for every m. The steps
  ξ ∧ g(v) = (∧g)(ξ ∧ v) = 0 for v in W and (∧_m g) ξ = det(g|W) ξ, the
  normality and closedness of {h ∈ H : det(h|W) = 1}, and the
  identification of the isotropy group (a subset of GL(n, R)) with this
  subgroup of H ([`isomorphism_modular_function`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L93)) are supplied; 2.7.12 enters
  through [`normal_subgroup_modular_restriction`](../../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L216).
- *Generality.* V is any finite dimensional inner product space; the R^n
  statements are instances. Generalisation.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (5): the affine group

Federer, p. 138, example (5), from "If G is the affine group of R^n" to "in
the present case.", with the erratum (book p. 138, line 34): the modular
function of G^W has the value |det[L(g)|W]|^{-1} Δ_H[L(g)]. Both assertions
are proved. The affine group is that of [`AffineGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v): pairs
p = (g, z) of GL(V) × V standing for τ_z ∘ g, with L(p) = g and the operations
`affine_group_mul hB hb`, `affine_group_inv hB hb`, `affine_group_unit hB hb`;
the usual action is `affine_group_action hB hb`.

*Δ_G(g) = |det L(g)|^{-1}.*

- [`GroupIsomorphismModular.v`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v). [`isomorphism_modular_function`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L93),
  [`commutative_group_modular_unit`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L132) (an abelian group has modular function
  1), [`abelian_normal_modular_unit`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L160) (Δ_G = 1 on a closed normal abelian
  subgroup, by 2.7.12).
- [`AffineGroupModular.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v). `affine_linear_character hB hb p` is
  |det L(p)|^{-1}; [`affine_haar_integral_covariant`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L123): every Haar integral of
  the additive group of V is |det ∘ L|^{-1} covariant for the usual action;
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_affine_covariant_integral`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L330) and
  [`euclidean_lebesgue_affine_covariant`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L339) ("L^n is |det ∘ L|^{-1} covariant",
  as an integral and as a covariant measure);
  [`affine_isotropy_homeomorphism`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L175) (G^0 = {(g, 0)} ≅ GL(V)),
  [`affine_isotropy_modular_unit`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L219) (Δ_{G^0} = 1, example (2)),
  [`affine_modular_linear_part`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L237) (Δ_G(g, 0) = |det g|^{-1}, by Weil's
  condition), [`affine_translations`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L253), [`affine_translations_normal`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L258),
  [`affine_translations_closed`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L273), [`affine_modular_translations`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L284)
  (Δ_G|ker L = 1), and [`affine_group_modular`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L297): Δ_G(p) = |det L(p)|^{-1} for
  every modular function Δ_G; [`euclidean_affine_group_modular`](../../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L353) for R^n.

*No covariant integral on the m dimensional affine subspaces.*

- [`AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v). [`affine_subspace_affine_action`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L61) is
  (τ_z ∘ g)[S] on the space X of [`AffineSubspaceSpace.v`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v);
  [`affine_subspace_affine_action_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L86), `..._compatible`,
  [`affine_subspace_affine_orbit_open`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L138) (from the isometry group),
  [`affine_subspace_affine_homogeneous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L156),
  [`affine_subspace_affine_locally_compact`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L171), and
  [`affine_subspace_affine_isotropy`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L184): the isotropy group of the vector
  subspace W (the point τ_0 W) is {(h, z) : h(W) = W, z ∈ W} = M^{-1}(H × W).
- [`AffineStabiliserModular.v`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v). `affine_subspace_stabiliser hB hb W` (M^{-1}(H × W)),
  closed subgroup ([`subspace_set_closed`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L122),
  [`affine_subspace_stabiliser_closed`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L159)); G^W acts on the space W by
  [`affine_stabiliser_action`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L204) ([`affine_stabiliser_homogeneous`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L328)); every Haar
  integral of W is |det[L(·)|W]|^{-1} covariant
  ([`affine_stabiliser_haar_covariant`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L390)); the isotropy group of 0 is
  isomorphic to H ([`affine_stabiliser_isotropy_homeomorphism`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L473)); hence
  [`affine_stabiliser_modular_linear_part`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L530),
  [`affine_stabiliser_modular_translations`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L582) and
  [`affine_subspace_stabiliser_modular`](../../theories/AffineStabiliserModular.v#L602):
  Δ_{G^W}(k) = |det(L(k)|W)|^{-1} Δ_H(L(k)).
- [`AffineSubspaceWeilCondition.v`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceWeilCondition.v). [`affine_isotropy_modular`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceWeilCondition.v#L86) (the same value
  for the isotropy group of W itself, with any restricted structure);
  [`affine_subspace_no_covariant_integral`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceWeilCondition.v#L117): for 0 < m < n and every function
  χ there is no nonzero χ covariant integral on K(X);
  [`euclidean_affine_subspace_no_covariant_integral`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceWeilCondition.v#L190) for R^n.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 133–138, with 1.3.4, p. 16, 1.7.1, p. 28, 2.7.1,
pp. 121–122, 2.7.5, p. 125, 2.7.8, p. 126, 2.7.10–2.7.12, pp. 127–129,
examples (1)–(4) and (7) of 2.7.16, pp. 134–140, and the erratum, book p. 138,
line 34.

##### Divergences

- *The affine group (p. 138).* Represented by the pairs (g, z) with the
  topology of the topology part, so that M is the identity of this
  representation; V is any finite dimensional inner product space, R^n an
  instance. Interpretation and generalisation.
- *"L^n is |det ∘ L|^{-1} covariant" (p. 138).* Proved for every Haar integral
  of the additive group of V and, for R^n, for L^n as integral and as
  covariant measure. Proof route: translation invariance and
  μ(u ∘ g) = |det g|^{-1} μ(u) ([`finite_haar_integral_linear`](../../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L381)); Federer gives
  no proof.
- *"With G^0 = GL(n, R)" (p. 138).* Formalised as a homeomorphic isomorphism
  g ↦ (g, 0) of GL(n, R) onto G^0 and the transport of modular functions
  ([`isomorphism_modular_function`](../../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L93)). Supplied proof.
- *"ker L|G = im τ is abelian, hence Δ_G|ker L = 1" (p. 138).* Supplied: ker L
  is a closed normal subgroup, Δ_{ker L} = Δ_G|ker L by 2.7.12, and an
  abelian group is unimodular.
- *X as a homogeneous space (p. 138).* Presupposed by Federer; supplied:
  continuity through dir (τ_z ∘ g)[S] = g[dir S] and the nearest point,
  openness inherited from the isometry group of example (7).
- *"Argue as above" (p. 138).* Carried out with W in place of R^n: G^W acts on
  the space W, Haar integrals of W are |det[L(·)|W]|^{-1} covariant, the
  isotropy group of 0 is isomorphic to H, and the translations by vectors of
  W form a closed normal abelian subgroup. Supplied proof.
- *|det[L(g)|W]|^{-1} (p. 138, line 34).* Erratum correction: the printed text
  has det[L(g)|W]^{-1} without absolute value.
- *"We can show as in (4)" (p. 138).* The nonexistence is stated for every
  function χ. g ↦ χ(g, 0) is a homomorphism of GL(n, R), hence equal to 1 at
  g with det g = 1 ([`special_linear_character_unit`](../../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L482)); at (t, 0), with t as in
  (4), Weil's condition would give |det(t|W)|^{-1-n} = 1. The vector subspace
  W is the direction of a point of X, which exists when a nonzero integral
  on K(X) exists. Supplied proof.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (6a): the Haar measure of O(n) and invariant measures on O(n, m), O*(n, m), G(n, m)

Federer, p. 139, example (6), from "Since the orthogonal group O(n) is a
compact subset" to "gamma_{n,m} over G(n, m)". All three assertions are
proved: the existence and uniqueness of theta_n, the invariance of
(A^x)_# theta_n with total measure 1, and the measures theta_{n,m},
theta*_{n,m}, gamma_{n,m}. The orthogonal group is `orthogonal_group hB hb`
of [`OrthogonalGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v), a closed subgroup of the linear group, with
any restricted structure mulS, invS, eS satisfying [`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104)
(existence: [`orthogonal_group_restriction_exists`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L218)). A Haar measure is that of
2.7.2 ([`is_left_haar_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L118), [`is_right_haar_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L152)), an invariant measure
that of 2.7.1 ([`is_invariant_measure`](../../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v#L73)), and (A^x)_# theta is the image measure
`measure_pushforward (orbit_map A x) theta` of 2.1.2.

*Compact groups.* The two general assertions are proved for every compact
group G satisfying the standing hypothesis of 2.7.1
(`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`, `compact [set: G]`).

- [`CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v). [`compact_representing_measure_total`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L49):
  if phi represents lambda on K(X) and X is compact, then phi(X) = lambda(1).
  [`nonzero_invariant_integral_scale`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L75): c lambda is a nonzero monotone invariant
  integral for c > 0. `is_normalised_haar_measure mul theta`: theta is a left
  Haar measure with theta(G) = 1; [`normalised_haar_measure_right`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L117),
  [`normalised_haar_measure_exists`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L142), [`normalised_haar_measure_unique`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L162) (two left
  or right Haar measures with total measure 1 are equal), and
  [`compact_group_unique_haar_measure`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L192), the printed statement: there is theta,
  a left and a right Haar measure with theta(G) = 1, equal to every left or
  right Haar measure psi with psi(G) = 1.
- [`CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v). [`radon_representations_coincide`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L87): two
  Radon measures representing the same integral on K(X) are equal.
  [`compact_transitive_homogeneous`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L174): a continuous compatible transitive action
  of a compact topological group on a Hausdorff space satisfies
  [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33) (the orbit maps are open);
  [`compact_homogeneous_space_compact`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L194), [`compact_homogeneous_orbit_proper`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L209).
  `is_normalised_invariant_measure A phi`: phi is an invariant measure with
  phi(X) = 1. [`compact_orbit_pushforward_invariant`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L280): for a homogeneous space X
  of G that is Hausdorff and for every x in X,
  `measure_pushforward (orbit_map A x) theta` is a normalised invariant
  measure. [`compact_invariant_measure_unique`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L312): two normalised invariant
  measures over X coincide; [`compact_orbit_pushforward_independent`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L350): the image
  measure does not depend on x. `normalised_invariant_measure_exists x0`:
  there is exactly one normalised invariant measure phi over X, and
  (A^x)_# theta = phi for every x and every normalised Haar measure theta.

*O(V).* [`OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v), for a space V with an inner product
B and a finite basis b. `orthogonal_element_map g` is g in Hom(V, V), with
[`orthogonal_element_orthogonal`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L50), [`orthogonal_element_inner`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L56),
[`orthogonal_element_continuous`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L63), [`orthogonal_element_mul`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L93), [`orthogonal_element_mulE`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L102),
[`orthogonal_element_unit`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L108), [`orthogonal_element_inv`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L117), [`orthogonal_element_ext`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L127);
`orthogonal_element_of hB hb hf` is the member of O(V) acting as an orthogonal
linear map f ([`orthogonal_element_ofE`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L82)). The printed assertions are
[`orthogonal_group_unique_haar_measure`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L137) (theta_n) and
[`orthogonal_orbit_invariant_measure`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L187) ((A^x)_# theta_n), with
[`orthogonal_normalised_haar_measure_exists`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L152),
[`orthogonal_normalised_haar_measure_unique`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L163) and
[`orthogonal_normalised_haar_measure_right`](../../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L174).

*O(n, m) and O*(n, m).* [`OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v), for spaces U (inner
product C, basis c, U = R^m) and V (U, V finite dimensional).
[`hom_operator_norm_comp_general`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L62) and [`hom_composition_continuous_general`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L78):
composition Hom(V, W) x Hom(U, V) -> Hom(U, W) is continuous.
[`orthonormal_dimension_bound`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L130), [`orthonormal_family_full_basis`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L141) and
[`orthogonal_injection_transitive`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L154): for orthogonal injections f_1, f_2 of U
into V there is an orthogonal g of V with g o f_1 = f_2.
`orthogonal_injections hC hB hc`, a subset of Hom(U, V), and
`orthogonal_projections hC hB hb`, the set of p in Hom(V, U) with
[`is_orthogonal_projection`](../../theories/AdjointUP.v#L21), carry the relative topologies. The actions are
`orthogonal_injection_action hC hB hc hb g f` = g o f
([`orthogonal_injection_actionE`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L275)) and `orthogonal_projection_action hC hB hb hS g p`
= p o g^{-1} ([`orthogonal_projection_actionE`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L318)); points are built by
[`orthogonal_injection_of`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L231) and [`orthogonal_projection_of`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L237), and read by
[`orthogonal_injection_map`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L209), [`orthogonal_projection_map`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L213). Both spaces are
Hausdorff, compact and homogeneous spaces of O(V)
([`orthogonal_injections_homogeneous`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L441), [`orthogonal_projections_homogeneous`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L452),
with the continuity, compatibility and transitivity lemmas). The measures are
`orthogonal_injections_invariant_measure f0` and
`orthogonal_projections_invariant_measure p0`: exactly one normalised
invariant measure, equal to (A^f)_# theta_n, respectively (A^p)_# theta_n.

*G(n, m).* [`GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v). [`linear_image_subspace`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L52),
`linear_image_grassmann hc hf` (f[U] in G(V, m) for univalent f),
[`grassmann_orthogonal_frame`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L114) (every W in G(V, m) is f[U] for an orthogonal
injection f), [`orthogonal_injection_image_span`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L153). The orthogonal projection
P_W is specified by [`is_grassmann_projection`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L167) (P_W(x) in W, x - P_W(x)
perpendicular to W); [`grassmann_projection_exists`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L174),
[`grassmann_projection_unique`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L206), the chosen `grassmann_projection hC hB hc W`
with [`grassmann_projectionP`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L225), [`grassmann_projection_fixed`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L230),
[`grassmann_projection_injective`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L242). [`subspace_image`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L265), [`grassmann_image`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L297) and
[`grassmann_imageE`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L296) give g[W]. `grassmann_space hC hB hc hb` is G(V, m) with
the initial topology of W |-> P_W; [`grassmann_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L337),
`grassmann_action hC hB hc hb g W` = g[W] ([`grassmann_actionE`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L354)),
[`grassmann_action_projection`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L373) (P_{g[W]} = g o P_W o g^{-1}),
[`grassmann_space_homogeneous`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L454), [`grassmann_space_compact`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L463) and
`grassmann_invariant_measure W0`.

*R^n.* [`EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v), with V = R^n, U = R^m, the
inner product of 1.7.1 and the standard bases. `euclidean_orthogonal_group R n`
(O(n); [`euclidean_orthogonal_groupE`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L51) relates it to `FedererOrthogonal.O_group`),
`euclidean_orthogonal_injections R n m` (O(n, m); [`euclidean_orthogonal_injectionsE`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L62)
relates it to `FedererOrthogonal.O`), `euclidean_orthogonal_projections R n m`
(O*(n, m); [`euclidean_orthogonal_projectionsE`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L73) relates it to [`O_star`](../../theories/CoordinateProjection.v#L21)) and
`euclidean_grassmann_space R n m` (G(n, m), whose points are those of
`FedererEuclideanGrassmann.G`; [`euclidean_grassmann_spaceE`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L85)).
[`euclidean_orthogonal_injection_exists`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L91): O(n, m) is nonempty for m <= n.
[`euclidean_theta_exists_unique`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L114) (theta_n), [`euclidean_orbit_invariant_measure`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L127)
((A^x)_# theta_n), and, for m <= n, [`euclidean_theta_nm_exists_unique`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L141)
(theta_{n,m}), [`euclidean_theta_star_nm_exists_unique`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L159) (theta*_{n,m}) and
[`euclidean_gamma_nm_exists_unique`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L180) (gamma_{n,m}): in each case exactly one
O(n) invariant measure of total measure 1, equal to (A^x)_# theta_n for every
point x and every normalised Haar measure theta_n.

Three regressions. For n < m the set O(n, m) is empty
([`euclidean_orthogonal_injections_empty_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6779)), so no measure over it has
total measure 1 ([`euclidean_theta_nm_dimension_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6795)); a space with a
normalised invariant measure is nonempty
([`normalised_invariant_measure_point_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6810)), which is why
[`normalised_invariant_measure_exists`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L365) takes a point.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, p. 139, with 1.6.2, p. 24, 1.7.1–1.7.4, pp. 28–31,
1.7.9, p. 35, 2.1.2, p. 54, 2.2.5, p. 62, 2.2.17, p. 72, 2.4.5, p. 83,
2.5.2–2.5.3, pp. 92–94, 2.5.13–2.5.14, pp. 106–107, 2.5.19, p. 113,
2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–122, 2.7.7, p. 125, 2.7.11–2.7.12, pp. 128–129,
2.7.14–2.7.15, pp. 131–133, and 3.2.28(4), pp. 266–267. neither treats Haar or
invariant measures. The erratum has no entry for p. 139.

##### Divergences

- *Proofs (p. 139).* Federer gives no proof of any assertion of the passage.
  The proofs are supplied: normalisation of a Haar integral by its value at
  the constant function 1 and uniqueness by 2.7.7 and 2.5.3; invariance of
  (A^x)_# theta through the integral (A^x)_#(lambda) of 2.5.19 and 2.7.12,
  and equality with the K(X) regular measure because both are Radon measures
  (2.2.17, 2.7.14, 2.7.15) representing the same integral
  ([`radon_representations_coincide`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L87)).
- *Generalisation (p. 139).* Both general assertions are proved for every
  compact group satisfying the standing hypothesis of 2.7.1, not only O(n),
  and for O(V) with V any finite dimensional inner product space. Of the
  standing hypotheses on X only the Hausdorff property is assumed; X is then
