  compact, hence locally compact.
- *"Unique left and right Haar measure" (p. 139).* Read as: theta is a left
  and a right Haar measure with theta[O(n)] = 1, and every left or right Haar
  measure with total measure 1 equals theta. This is an interpretation.
- *"Transitive action A of O(n) on a homogeneous space X" (p. 139).* Read as
  [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33), which contains transitivity. For the three spaces
  the openness of the orbit maps, part of 2.7.1, is derived from compactness
  ([`compact_transitive_homogeneous`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L174)), a supplied lemma.
- *Definite article in "the O(n) invariant measures" (p. 139).* Uniqueness of
  the invariant measure with total measure 1 and its independence of x are
  added ([`compact_invariant_measure_unique`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L312), by 2.7.11(2)); Federer states
  neither. The existence statements take a point of X, since an empty space
  carries no measure with total measure 1.
- *Topologies and actions (p. 139).* Federer names neither. O(n, m) and
  O*(n, m) carry the relative topologies of Hom(R^m, R^n) and
  Hom(R^n, R^m), and O(n) acts by A(g, f) = g o f and A(g, p) = p o g^{-1}.
  G(n, m), the set of subspaces of 1.6.2, carries the initial topology of
  W |-> P_W into Hom(R^n, R^n), and O(n) acts by A(g, W) = g[W]. This
  topology is supplied; Federer's later identification in 3.2.28(4),
  pp. 266–267, is not used. The projection P_W is chosen from its
  characterising laws, and later results use only those laws.
- *Theta*_{n,m} and adjoints (p. 139).* O*(n, m) is the set of orthogonal
  projections ([`is_orthogonal_projection`](../../theories/AdjointUP.v#L21)), which equals
  {f* : f in O(n, m)} of 1.7.4 ([`O_star_projection`](../../theories/CoordinateProjection.v#L30)). The action on O*(n, m)
  is stated with the restricted inversion invS of O(n) and depends on the
  restriction hypothesis hS.
- *n >= m (p. 139).* The measures theta_{n,m}, theta*_{n,m}, gamma_{n,m}
  are obtained for m <= n; for n < m the space O(n, m) is empty (regression).

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (6b): the constants beta_t(n, m)

Federer, pp. 139–140, example (6), from "Whenever n >= m are positive
integers and 1 <= t < oo" to "whenever 1 <= t <= oo". Every assertion of
the passage is proved: the existence of beta_t(n, m), the integral over
O(n) of |<xi, Lambda_m g> wedge eta|^t, the corollary
beta_t(n, m)/beta_t(n - k, m) = beta_t(n, k)/beta_t(n - m, k), the
definition beta_oo(n, m) = 1 and the formula [theta*_{n,m}]_(t)(f) =
beta_t(n, m) |xi| for 1 <= t <= oo. The computation of beta_t(n, m) in
3.2.13 is not part of the item. Here <xi, Lambda_m g> = (Lambda_m g) xi
is [`exterior_power_map`](../../theories/ExteriorMaps.v#L219), |.| is [`exterior_norm`](../../theories/ExteriorNorm.v#L23) of 1.7.5, xi wedge eta
is [`exterior_product`](../../theories/ExteriorHomogeneousProducts.v#L30), simple m-vectors are [`simple`](../../theories/AssociatedSubspace.v#L27) of 1.6.1, and O(n),
O*(n, m), theta_n and theta*_{n,m} are those of part (6a)
([`euclidean_orthogonal_group`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L46), [`euclidean_orthogonal_projections`](../../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L68),
[`is_normalised_haar_measure`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L110), [`is_normalised_invariant_measure`](../../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L224) for
[`orthogonal_projection_action`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L312), with a restricted group structure hS of
O(n)). Integrals are [`is_measure_integral`](../../theories/MeasureIntegralUP.v#L71) of 2.4.2 with
[`real_total_integrand`](../../theories/SummableRealFunctionUP.v#L17); powers are [`real_power`](../../theories/RealPower.v#L18) of 2.4.12.

*Scalar and continuity facts.* [`RealPowerContinuity.v`](../../theories/RealPowerContinuity.v):
[`real_power_continuous_comp`](../../theories/RealPowerContinuity.v#L124) (g continuous and nonnegative, t > 0, then
x |-> g(x)^t is continuous), [`real_power_local_bound`](../../theories/RealPowerContinuity.v#L84),
[`real_power_square_half`](../../theories/RealPowerContinuity.v#L41), [`real_power_negative_exponent`](../../theories/RealPowerContinuity.v#L51),
[`real_power_injective`](../../theories/RealPowerContinuity.v#L63). [`ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v`](../../theories/ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v):
[`exterior_power_map_e_lambda`](../../theories/ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v#L35) ((Lambda_m f)(v_1 wedge ... wedge v_m) =
f(v_1) wedge ... wedge f(v_m)), [`hom_value_inner_continuous`](../../theories/ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v#L79),
[`exterior_map_norm_square_continuous`](../../theories/ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v#L125) (by the permutation formula of
1.7.5), [`exterior_map_norm_power_continuous`](../../theories/ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v#L151) and
[`exterior_map_norm_continuous`](../../theories/ExteriorMapNormContinuity.v#L172): for simple xi, p |-> |(Lambda_m p) xi|^t is
continuous on Hom(V, U).

*The constants beta_t(n, m).* [`OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v).
`projection_norm h2 xi p` is |(Lambda_m p) xi| for p in O*(n, m), with
[`projection_norm_scale`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L79), [`projection_norm_bound`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L90) (1.7.7),
[`projection_norm_power_compact`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L159) (the integrand lies in K(O*(n, m))) and
[`normalised_invariant_measure_integral`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L194) (theta*_{n,m} represents a nonzero
monotone invariant integral nu with nu(1) = 1).
`is_projection_constant h2 t n m beta` states beta > 0 and, for every
restricted structure of O(n), every normalised invariant measure phi over
O*(n, m) and every simple xi, that the integral a of p |->
|(Lambda_m p) xi|^t satisfies `extended_positive_power a t^-1 = (beta *
|xi|)%:E`, the printed equation. `projection_constant_exists`: for
m <= n and t > 0 such a beta exists; [`projection_constant_unique`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L390): it is
unique; [`projection_constant_integral`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L356): the integral equals beta^t |xi|^t.
[`projection_constant_integral_value`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L217) is the computation of the proof,
[`projection_frame_translate`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L172) the transitivity on orthonormal frames and
[`orthogonal_projection_action_independent`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L302) the independence of the action
of the restricted structure. [`SimpleUnitTransitivity.v`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v) proves the two
facts quoted by Federer, for 0 < m: [`simple_unit_transitive`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L71) (O(n) acts
transitively on the simple m-vectors of norm 1) and
[`projection_integral_rotation`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L143) (the integrals for xi and (Lambda_m g) xi
are equal), with [`e_lambda_negate_one`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L44) and [`projection_norm_rotation`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L127).

*The map q.* [`WedgeKernelProjection.v`](../../theories/WedgeKernelProjection.v), [`wedge_kernel_projection_exists`](../../theories/WedgeKernelProjection.v#L93):
for simple eta in Lambda_k V with |eta| = 1, V with an inner product and a
basis of n vectors, there are q : V -> R^{n-k} and h = q*, h an
orthogonal injection with adjoint q, such that {v : v wedge eta = 0} =
ker q = im(1 - q* o q) and |zeta wedge eta| = |<zeta, Lambda_m q>| for
every simple zeta; [`wedge_kernel_projection_star`](../../theories/WedgeKernelProjection.v#L206) (q is in O*(n, n - k)),
[`wedge_word_vector_invariance`](../../theories/WedgeKernelProjection.v#L50) (v wedge eta = (q* o q)(v) wedge eta for
all v gives the same for products).

*The integral over O(n) and the corollary.* [`OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v).
[`orthogonal_wedge_integral`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L207): for t > 0, m + k <= n, beta_1, beta_2 with
`is_projection_constant h2 t n m beta1` and `is_projection_constant h2 t
(n - k) m beta2`, a normalised Haar measure theta of O(n) and simple xi,
eta, the integral of g |-> |<xi, Lambda_m g> wedge eta|^t is
beta_1^t beta_2^{-t} |xi|^t |eta|^t. Supporting:
[`compact_haar_integral_inversion`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L67) (lambda(u o g^{-1}) = lambda(u) for the
Haar integral of a compact group), [`orthogonal_projection_compose`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L87)
(r o q in O*(n, m)), [`orthogonal_wedge_integrand_compact`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L165),
[`orthogonal_composite_norm_power_compact`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L139).
[`orthogonal_wedge_inverse`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L372): |<xi, Lambda_m g> wedge eta| =
|xi wedge <eta, Lambda_k g^{-1}>| for g in O(n) and all xi, eta.
[`projection_constant_ratio`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L419): for t > 0 and m + k <= n,
beta_t(n, m)/beta_t(n - k, m) = beta_t(n, k)/beta_t(n - m, k); with
[`standard_frame_product`](../../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L397).

*Seminorms.* [`ProjectionConstantNorms.v`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v). `is_extended_projection_constant
h2 t n m beta` for t in the extended reals: beta = 1 at t = +oo
([`projection_constant_infinity`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L63), "beta_oo(n, m) = 1") and
[`is_projection_constant`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L286) at finite t. With f = `projection_norm h2 xi`,
real valued and N = |.|: [`projection_norm_lp`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L80) (finite t >= 1, the
specification [`is_measure_lp_norm`](../../theories/MeasureLpNormUP.v#L21) of 2.4.12 with value beta |xi|),
[`projection_norm_essential`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L100) ([`is_measure_essential_norm`](../../theories/MeasureEssentialNormUP.v#L27) with value |xi|),
[`projection_norm_essential_value`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L167) (the public [`measure_essential_norm`](../../theories/MeasureEssentialNorm.v#L18)
equals 1 |xi|) and [`projection_norm_seminorm`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L187) (both cases for 1 <= t <=
oo).

Three regressions: beta_t(n, 0) = 1 ([`projection_constant_degree_zero_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6890));
for n < m every positive number satisfies [`is_projection_constant`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L286), since
O*(n, m) is empty ([`projection_constant_vacuous_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6912)), which is why
uniqueness assumes m <= n; the extended constant at +oo is 1
([`projection_constant_infinity_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6931)).

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 139–140, with 1.3.1, pp. 13–14, 1.6.1, p. 23,
1.7.1–1.7.7, pp. 27–34, 1.7.9, p. 35, 2.4.2, p. 81, 2.4.12, pp. 86–87, 2.5.13,
p. 106, 2.6.4 and 2.6.6, pp. 119–120, 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp. 121–122, 2.7.8, p. 126,
and 2.7.12, p. 129. neither treats these integrals. The erratum has no entry
for pp. 139–140.

##### Divergences

- *beta_t(n, m) (p. 139).* The number is specified by the predicate
  [`is_projection_constant`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L286), for every normalised invariant measure over
  O*(n, m) and every restricted group structure of O(n); no function
  beta_t is defined, and later results take numbers satisfying the
  predicate. Existence and uniqueness are proved. This is an
  interpretation.
- *Range of t, n, m (p. 139).* Existence, uniqueness, the integral over
  O(n) and the corollary are proved for every t > 0, and for m = 0 and
  n = 0, where Federer has 1 <= t and positive n >= m. This is a
  generalisation. The transitivity of [`simple_unit_transitive`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L71) keeps
  0 < m.
- *Proof of existence (p. 139).* Federer uses the transitivity of O(n) on
  the simple unit m-vectors. The proof of [`projection_constant_exists`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L322)
  uses the transitivity on orthonormal m-frames, on which the integrand
  depends only up to the sign of the m-vector; it also covers m = 0.
  Federer's two facts are proved separately ([`simple_unit_transitive`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L71),
  [`projection_integral_rotation`](../../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L143)). The positivity of beta, the continuity
  of the integrand and its membership in K(O*(n, m)) are supplied (1.7.7,
  the permutation formula of 1.7.5, positivity of nonzero invariant
  integrals).
- *The map q (p. 139).* Federer gives no proof of the existence of q or of
  the displayed equations; the proofs are supplied. The equation
  |zeta wedge eta| = |<zeta, Lambda_m(q* o q)> wedge eta| = |<zeta,
  Lambda_m q>| is proved for simple zeta, where Federer writes "for any
  zeta"; the next display, which uses the definition of beta_t(n - k, m)
  on simple vectors, is applied, as by Federer, to the simple zeta =
  <xi, Lambda_m g>. This is a restriction of the first display.
- *Reduction to |xi| = 1 = |eta| (p. 139).* Only eta is normalised; the
  factor |xi|^t is carried by the definition of beta_t(n, m).
- *Fubini's theorem (p. 139).* The interchange is proved for the Daniell
  integrals of theta_n and theta*_{n-k,m} on K(O(n) x O*(n - k, m))
  (2.6.4, 2.6.6; [`compact_daniell_iterations_commute`](../../theories/CompactDaniellIterationCommutation.v#L44)), since the
  integrand is continuous on a compact space; the measure-theoretic
  2.6.2 for theta_n x theta*_{n-k,m} is not used. This is a different
  proof route.
- *Inner integral over O(n) (p. 139).* The step int |<xi, Lambda_m(r o q
  o g)>|^t dtheta_n g = beta_t(n, m)^t is supplied: theta*_{n,m} =
  (A^p)_# theta_n with p = r o q and A(g, p) = p o g^{-1}, and the
  inversion invariance of the Haar integral of the compact group O(n),
  from 2.7.8(3) and 2.7.12.
- *Corollary (p. 139).* The proof follows Federer's indication: the
  identity |<xi, Lambda_m g> wedge eta| = |xi wedge <eta, Lambda_k
  g^{-1}>| (proved for all xi, eta), the symmetry |xi wedge zeta| =
  |zeta wedge xi| of 1.3.1, and the unimodularity of O(n); it is applied
  to unit simple vectors of degrees m and k, with m + k <= n.
- *[theta*_{n,m}]_(t)(f) (p. 140).* f is real valued with N = |.|. For
  finite t the assertion is stated with the specification
  [`is_measure_lp_norm`](../../theories/MeasureLpNormUP.v#L21) of 2.4.12; the public value [`measure_lp_norm`](../../theories/MeasureLpNorm.v#L17)
  requires a separate measurability hypothesis for f and is not used. For
  t = oo both the specification and the public value
  [`measure_essential_norm`](../../theories/MeasureEssentialNorm.v#L18) are given. Federer's "from 2.4.12 and 1.7.7"
  is expanded for t = oo by the positivity argument described in
  [`ProjectionConstantNorms.v`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v).
- *beta_oo(n, m) = 1 (p. 140).* Formalised as the value at +oo of
  [`is_extended_projection_constant`](../../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L54). This is an interpretation.

#### 2.7.16 — Examples, part (7): the isometry group of R^n and invariant measures on affine subspaces

Federer, pp. 140–141, example (7), from "Suppose G is the group of all
isometries" to the alternate formula before 2.7.17. Every assertion is
proved: G^0 = O(n) and G = im tau o O(n) (already in
[`AffineGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v)), the G invariance of L^n, the unimodularity of G,
the Haar integral lambda(u) = int theta_n^0(u) dL^n =
int int u(tau_z o s) dtheta_n s dL^n z, the topological properties of xi and
delta, the Radon property of gamma = xi_#(theta_n x L^{n-m}), the G
invariance of gamma and of mu(v) = int int v[s(p^{-1}{y})] dtheta_n s
dL^{n-m} y, and the alternate formula with theta*_{n,n-m}. G is
`isometry_group hB hb` of [`AffineGroupTopology.v`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v) with any restricted
structure mulS, invS, eS ([`is_subgroup_restriction`](../../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104)), O(n) is
`orthogonal_group hB hb` with a restricted structure mulO, invO, eO, and
O*(n, n - m) is [`orthogonal_projections`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L201) of [`OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v).
In the measure-theoretic statements V = R^n, the coordinate space with the
inner product of 1.7.1, and m <= n.

*The isometry group.* [`IsometryGroupStructure.v`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v), for a space V with an
inner product and a finite basis. `isometry_linear g` (L(g) in O(n)),
`isometry_translation g` (g(0)), `isometry_of s z` (tau_z o s) and
`isometry_map g` (g as a map of V);
[`isometry_product_homeomorphism`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L150): (s, z) |-> tau_z o s is a homeomorphism of
O(n) x V onto G ("G = im tau o O(n)"). [`isometry_map_mul`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L185),
[`isometry_linear_mul`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L201), [`isometry_linear_inv`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L237), [`isometry_translation_mul`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L217),
[`isometry_of_mul`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L223) and [`isometry_of_unit`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L232) express the group structure of G
through that of O(n). [`isometry_point_homogeneous`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L258): V is a homogeneous
space of G through A(g, v) = g(v); [`isometry_point_isotropy_origin`](../../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L288):
G^0 = {g : g(0) = 0}.

*The space X of m dimensional affine subspaces.* [`AffineSubspaceSpace.v`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v),
for V as above and an auxiliary space U = R^m with a basis of m vectors
(the parameter of G(V, m) in [`GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v`](../../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v)).

- `affine_coset a W` is tau_a W = {x : x - a in W} for W in G(V, m);
  [`affine_coset_is_affine_subspace`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L90) (an affine subspace in the sense of
  p. 133), [`affine_coset_move`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L62) and [`affine_coset_direction_unique`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L74).
  `is_affine_subspace_dim S` (S = tau_a W for some a and W in G(V, m)),
  `affine_subspace_carrier V m` (the set of these S as a type),
  `affine_points S`, `affine_subspace_of a W` (tau_a W as a point of X),
  [`affine_subspace_ext`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L123), [`affine_subspace_eq_point`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L183) (same direction and a
  common point).
- `affine_direction S` (dir S, specified by [`affine_subspace_cosetE`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L150) and
  unique by [`affine_coset_direction_unique`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L74)), [`affine_points_diff`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L163).
  `affine_nearest hC hB hc S`, the nearest point of S to 0:
  [`affine_nearest_mem`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L241), [`affine_nearest_perp`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L249), [`affine_nearest_unique`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L254) (the
  only point of S perpendicular to dir S), [`affine_nearest_eq`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L228)
  (x
- P_{dir S}(x) for any x in S), [`affine_subspace_nearest_eta`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L275)
  (S = tau_{nearest S} dir S).
- `affine_origin_distance hC hB hc S` is delta(S) = distance(S, 0) =
  |nearest S|, with [`affine_origin_distance_least`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L294) (delta(S) <= |x| for x in
  S) and [`affine_origin_distance_attained`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L310).
- `affine_subspace_space hC hB hc hb` is X with the initial topology of
  S |-> (dir S, nearest S) into G(V, m) x V. [`affine_direction_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L350),
  [`affine_nearest_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L360), [`affine_projection_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L371),
  [`affine_subspace_continuous_into`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L384) (a map into X is continuous when its
  two components are), [`affine_subspace_space_hausdorff`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L396), and
  [`affine_origin_distance_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L413) ("delta is continuous").
- `affine_subspace_image hC hB hc hb p S` is f[S] for every member
  p = (g, z) of the affine group, f = tau_z o g ([`affine_group_map`](../../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L148)):
  [`affine_subspace_imageE`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L456), [`affine_subspace_image_mem`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L474),
  [`affine_subspace_image_direction`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L480) (dir f[S] = g[dir S]),
  [`affine_subspace_image_mul`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L488), [`affine_subspace_image_unit`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L500). These are the
  names for the action of the affine group of example (5).
- `affine_subspace_action hC hB hc hb g S` is g[S] for g in G:
  [`affine_subspace_actionE`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L538), [`affine_subspace_action_direction`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L551)
  (L(g)[dir S], the action of example (6) on G(n, m)),
  [`affine_subspace_action_nearest`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L561), [`affine_subspace_action_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L577),
  [`affine_subspace_action_compatible`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L617), [`affine_subspace_action_transitive`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L625),
  [`affine_subspace_orbit_open`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L646), and the theorem
  [`affine_subspace_homogeneous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L715): X is a homogeneous space of G (2.7.1).
  [`affine_subspace_space_locally_compact`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L731): X is locally compact.

*Unimodularity and the Haar integral of G.* [`IsometryGroupHaar.v`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v), V = R^n.

- [`isometry_lebesgue_preimage`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L121) (L^n[g^{-1}(S)] = L^n(S)),
  [`isometry_lebesgue_invariant_integral`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L163) (u |-> int u dL^n is a nonzero G
  invariant integral on K(R^n)) and [`isometry_lebesgue_invariant_measure`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L173)
  (L^n is a G invariant measure): "L^n is G invariant".
- `isometry_isotropy_of s` (tau_0 o s in G^0), [`isometry_isotropy_onto`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L209),
  [`isometry_isotropy_compact`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L231), [`isometry_isotropy_of_mul`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L300).
  [`isometry_unimodular_isotropy`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L314): Delta_G = 1 on G^0, by Weil's condition
  (2.7.11(1), [`weil_condition_necessary`](../../theories/WeilCondition.v#L138)) and the compactness of G^0.
  [`isometry_group_unimodular`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L424): G is unimodular ([`is_unimodular`](../../theories/UnimodularGroupUP.v#L21)).
- `isotropy_transport Theta` (the image of a left Haar integral Theta of
  O(n) under s |-> tau_0 o s), [`isotropy_transport_haar`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L341) (a left Haar
  integral of G^0), `isometry_coset_map Theta u z` = Theta_s[u(tau_z o s)],
  and [`isometry_coset_integral`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L389): this is the map mu^0 of 2.7.10
  ([`is_coset_integral`](../../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29)), Federer's theta_n^0.
  [`isometry_haar_integral`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L463): lambda(u) = int theta_n^0(u) dL^n is a left and a
  right Haar integral of G. [`isometry_haar_integral_theta`](../../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L492): for the
  normalised Haar measure theta_n of O(n) there is a left Haar integral
  Theta of O(n) represented by theta_n, the formula defines a left and
  right Haar integral, and its values on K(G) do not depend on the choice
  of Theta represented by theta_n.

*The Radon property of theta_n x L^{n-m}.* [`CompactProductRadon.v`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v).
[`compact_subset_relative_base`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L56): a compact subset of a pseudometric space has
a countable relative base of open sets. [`radon_agreement`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L187) (with
[`radon_agreement_compact`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L110), [`radon_agreement_upper`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L138), [`radon_agreement_borel`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L148)):
on a Hausdorff space covered by an increasing sequence of open sets with
compact closures, a Borel regular measure that agrees on open sets with a
Radon measure equals it. [`compact_product_measure_radon`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L246): if X is a compact
Hausdorff space and Y a locally compact Hausdorff space, both with countable
bases, Y is covered by an increasing sequence of open sets with compact
closures, and alpha, beta are Borel regular measures representing monotone
Daniell integrals A, B on K(X), K(Y), then the cartesian product
alpha x beta of 2.6.1 is a Radon measure; [`compact_product_measure_represents`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L232):
it represents h |-> B_y[A_x h(x, y)] on K(X x Y), which is the product
functional ([`compact_product_iterated_daniell_product`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L219)).

*The fibres of p.* [`OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v), for p in
O*(n, n - m). `projection_adjoint p` (p*, an orthogonal injection with
[`projection_adjointE`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L67) and [`projection_adjointK`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L72): p o p* = 1);
`projection_kernel hmn p` (ker p as a member of G(n, m), with
[`projection_kernelE`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L184); [`projection_kernel_exists`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L100) proves that ker p is m
dimensional); [`projection_adjoint_image`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L191): "im p* = im(p* o p) is the
orthogonal complement of ker p"; `projection_fibre hmn p y`, the point
p^{-1}{y} of X, with [`projection_fibreE`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L222) ("p^{-1}{y} = tau_{p*(y)} ker p":
x belongs to tau_{p*(y)} ker p if and only if p(x) = y),
[`projection_kernel_translate`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L232) ("tau_z ker p = tau_{(p* o p) z} ker p"),
[`projection_fibre_direction`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L241) and [`projection_fibre_nearest`](../../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L246) (nearest point
p*(y)).

*The map xi.* [`AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v).
`affine_parametrisation hmn p (s, y)` is xi(s, y) = s(p^{-1}{y}), with
[`affine_parametrisationE`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L96), [`affine_parametrisation_direction`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L106),
[`affine_parametrisation_nearest`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L114) (s(p*(y))). The displayed identities:
[`affine_rotated_translate_distance`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L153) (delta[(s o tau_z) ker p] = |p(z)|),
[`affine_parametrisation_distance`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L126) ((delta o xi)(s, y) = |y|),
[`affine_parametrisation_rotate`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L159) (t xi(s, y) = xi(t s, y)),
[`affine_parametrisation_translate`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L174) (tau_z xi(s, y) =
xi(s, y + (p o s^{-1}) z)) and [`affine_parametrisation_fibre`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L361)
(s[p^{-1}{y}] = (p s^{-1})^{-1}{y}). The topological assertions:
[`affine_parametrisation_continuous`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L230), [`affine_parametrisation_open`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L309)
(through [`rotation_shear_open`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L260) and [`affine_parametrisation_image`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L291)),
[`affine_parametrisation_onto`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L324) (im xi = X) and
[`affine_parametrisation_proper`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L337).

*The invariant measure.* [`AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v), for a
normalised Haar measure theta of O(n) ([`is_normalised_haar_measure`](../../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L110)) and a
left Haar integral Theta of O(n) represented by theta.
[`affine_product_measure`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L164) is theta_n x L^{n-m} ([`product_measure`](../../theories/ProductMeasure.v#L14)),
[`affine_invariant_measure`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L169) is gamma = xi_#(theta_n x L^{n-m})
([`measure_pushforward`](../../theories/MeasurePushforward.v#L17)), and `affine_invariant_integral v` is
int int v[xi(s, y)] dtheta_n s dL^{n-m} y.
[`affine_product_measure_radon`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L209), [`affine_invariant_measure_radon`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L233) ("gamma
is a Radon measure over X"), [`affine_invariant_measure_represents`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L264)
(int v dgamma = mu(v) for v in K(X)), [`affine_invariant_integral_invariant`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L343)
(mu is a monotone G invariant integral on K(X): "a G invariant integral mu
on K(X) is given by the formula"), with [`affine_invariant_integral_rotation`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L292)
and [`affine_invariant_integral_translation`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L319); [`affine_space_sigma_compact`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L369),
[`orthogonal_group_countable_base`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L194) (O(n) has a countable base) and
[`affine_invariant_measure_invariant`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L385) (gamma is a G invariant measure, the
K(X) regular measure associated with mu).
[`affine_invariant_integral_alternate`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L413): for every normalised O(n) invariant
measure psi = theta*_{n,n-m} over O*(n, n - m) and every Psi represented by
psi, mu(v) = int int v(q^{-1}{y}) dpsi q dL^{n-m} y.

Four regressions: a vector subspace W, as the point tau_0 W of X, has
distance 0 from the origin; tau_z o s maps 0 to z; the fibre of p over 0
contains the origin; xi(s, 0) passes through the origin.

Sources: Federer 2.7.16, pp. 140–141, with 1.6.2, p. 24, 1.7.1–1.7.4, pp.
28–31, 1.7.9, p. 35, 2.1.2, p. 54, 2.1.3, p. 55, 2.2.3, p. 61, 2.2.5, pp.
62–63, 2.2.17, p. 72, 2.4.18, p. 90, 2.5.3, p. 94, 2.5.14, p. 107, 2.5.19, p.
113, 2.6.1–2.6.5, pp. 114–120, 2.7.1, pp. 121–122, 2.7.8–2.7.12, pp. 126–129,
2.7.14–2.7.15, pp. 131–133, and examples (1), (5) and (6) of 2.7.16, pp.
134–139. The erratum has no entry for this example; its entry for p. 141
concerns 2.7.18.

##### Divergences

- *Topology of X (p. 140).* Federer gives X no topology. X carries the initial
topology of S |-> (dir S, nearest S) into G(n, m) x R^n, where G(n, m) has the
topology of W |-> P_W (example (6)); equivalently, S is identified with its
nearest point retraction x |-> nearest S + P_{dir S}(x), a member of A(R^n,
R^n). This topology is supplied, and it makes X a locally compact Hausdorff
homogeneous space of G. Interpretation.
- *m dimensional affine subspaces (p.
140).* An m dimensional affine subspace is a set tau_a W with W in G(n, m);
such a set is an affine subspace in the sense of p. 133. dir S and nearest S
are chosen from their characterising laws, and later results use only these
laws. delta(S) = distance(S, 0) is formalised as |nearest S| together with the
proof that it is the least norm of a point of S. Interpretation.
- *L^n is G
invariant (p. 140).* Federer states it without proof. The proof composes the
invariance of L^n under orthogonal maps and under translations from part (1),
and transfers it to the integral by 2.4.18. The invariance is stated both for
the integral (2.7.1) and for the measure (an invariant measure in the sense of
2.7.1). Supplied proof.
- *Unimodularity (p. 140).* Federer writes "Since L^n
is G invariant and O(n) is compact, G is unimodular". The proof: Weil's
condition 2.7.11(1) for the invariant integral of L^n gives Delta_G =
Delta_{G^0} = 1 on G^0; for tau_z, sigma o tau_z = tau_{-z} o sigma with sigma
= -1_V in G^0 and tau_z o tau_{-z} = 1_V give Delta_G(tau_z)^2 = 1; and g =
tau_{g(0)} o (tau_0 o L(g)). The last two steps are supplied.
- *The Haar
integral (p. 140).* theta_n^0 is the map mu^0 of 2.7.10 for the left Haar
integral of G^0 obtained from Theta by s |-> tau_0 o s; int . dtheta_n is any
left Haar integral Theta of O(n) represented by theta_n, and the value of
lambda on K(G) does not depend on this choice. That lambda is a right Haar
integral follows from the unimodularity. Interpretation.
- *ker p (p. 140).*
That ker p is m dimensional, so that p^{-1}{y} is a point of X, is presupposed
by Federer. The proof extends an orthonormal basis of im p* to one of R^n
(1.7.3). Supplied proof.
- *The Radon property (p. 141).* "xi is proper, hence
gamma is a Radon measure" presupposes that theta_n x L^{n-m} is a Radon
measure. This is proved
([`compact_product_measure_radon`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L246)):
the product is Borel regular and represents the product functional (2.6.4 as
corrected in the erratum); it agrees on open sets with the Radon measure
representing that functional (2.5.3, 2.5.14, using countable bases), and a
Borel regular measure agreeing with a Radon measure on open sets equals it
([`radon_agreement`](../../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L187): compact sets
by subtraction from open sets of finite measure, Borel sets by inner
approximation and continuity from below). O(n) has a countable base through
the traces of a relative base of the compact set O(n) in Hom(R^n, R^n)
([`orthogonal_group_countable_base`](../../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L194)).
No metric on the product is used. The image of the Radon measure under the
proper map xi is Radon by 2.2.17. Supplied proof.
- *Continuity, openness,
properness of xi (p. 141).* Federer writes "we see that". The proofs: xi(s, y)
= (tau_{s(v)} o s)[ker p] for p(v) = y, so xi(U) is the image under the open
orbit map of G at ker p of the image of {(s, v) : (s, p(v)) in U} under the
homeomorphism (s, v) |-> tau_{s(v)} o s; im xi = X by transitivity; xi^{-1}(C)
is a closed subset of O(n) x {y : |y| <= r} with r a bound of delta on C. The
openness of the orbit maps of G on X is proved from that of O(n) on G(n, m).
Supplied proofs.
- *Invariance (p. 141).* Federer says "to verify that gamma
is invariant". The invariance is proved for the integral mu: under tau_0 o t
by the left invariance of theta_n, under tau_z by Fubini's theorem (2.6.4) and
the translation invariance of L^{n-m}, and every isometry is tau_z o (tau_0 o
t). gamma is then the K(X) regular measure of mu, since both it and that
measure are Radon measures representing mu (2.7.15 with X a countable union of
compact sets). Supplied proof.
- *"y in R^n" (p. 141).* In "s[p^{-1}{y}] = (p
s^{-1})^{-1}{y} for s in O(n), y in R^n", y ranges over R^{n-m}.
Interpretation.
- *theta*_{n,n-m} (p. 141).* The alternate formula is stated
for every normalised O(n) invariant measure psi over O*(n, n - m) and every
Psi represented by psi; by example (6), psi is the image of theta_n under s
|-> p o s^{-1}. The function q |-> v(q^{-1}{y}) is shown to be continuous
through the open orbit map at p. Interpretation and supplied proof.
- *Dimensions.* m <= n is assumed, the codimension is n - m, and the point p of
O*(n, n - m) is a parameter. The topological results on X and on G hold for
every finite dimensional inner product space V. Generalisation.

<a id="item-2-7-17"></a>

### 2.7.17 — Nonmeasurable subsets of compact groups; Vitali's example

Federer, p. 141. The assertion and its proof are formalised, and so is the
remark on Vitali's example. G is a locally compact Hausdorff topological
group (`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group mul inv e`, the standing hypothesis
of 2.7.1) with `compact [set: G]`. "S is a countably infinite subgroup" is
`is_subgroup mul inv e S`, `countable S` and `infinite_set S`. "T has exactly
one point in common with each left coset of S" is
`forall g, exists! t, T t /\ left_coset mul g S t`. A Haar measure is a left
or right Haar measure of 2.7.2 ([`is_left_haar_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L118),
[`is_right_haar_measure`](../../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L152)). Measurability is that of 2.1.2
([`measure_measurable`](../../theories/MeasureUP.v#L30)).

- [`InvariantMeasureTranslates.v`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v), for a homogeneous space X of a group G.
  [`invariant_measure_preimage`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L124): an invariant measure phi of 2.7.1 satisfies
  phi(A_g^{-1}[B]) = phi(B) for every B subset X and g in G.
  [`invariant_measure_measurable_preimage`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L153): A_g^{-1}[B] is phi measurable
  whenever B is. The proof uses [`lattice_regular_inverse_pushforward`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L94): if
  f: X -> Y has the inverse g and h o g is in L_Y for every h in L_X, then the
  image measure of an L_X regular measure under f is L_Y regular (2.5.3). For
  the right action of 2.7.2, [`right_translate_preimage`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L179) identifies
  {t s : t in T} with A_s^{-1}[T], and [`right_invariant_measure_translate`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L192)
  and [`right_invariant_measure_translate_measurable`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L203) give phi(T s) = phi(T)
  and the measurability of T s for a right invariant measure phi.
- [`CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v). [`coset_transversal_translates_disjoint`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L55)
  and [`coset_transversal_translates_cover`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L73): the sets T s with s in S are
  disjoint and cover G (group laws only). [`left_coset_transversal_exists`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L87):
  such sets T exist, by the axiom of choice.
  [`compact_group_haar_measures_coincide`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L131): on a compact group the left and the
  right Haar measures coincide. [`compact_right_haar_measure_total`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L145):
  0 < phi(G) < infinity. [`compact_group_transversal_right_haar_nonmeasurable`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L164),
  [`compact_group_transversal_left_haar_nonmeasurable`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L200) and
  [`compact_group_transversal_nonmeasurable`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L215) (the statement of 2.7.17): T is
  not phi measurable for every left or right Haar measure phi.
  [`compact_group_nonmeasurable_set_exists`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L232): a compact group with a countably
  infinite subgroup has a left Haar measure and a subset that is
  nonmeasurable with respect to every Haar measure.
- [`VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v), the remark "Vitali (with G = R/Z, S = Q/Z)".
  [`real_integers`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L36) and [`real_rationals`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L41) are Z and Q in R.
  [`real_additive_topological_group`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L47): (R, +) is a topological group (the
  type R itself; [`EuclideanAdditiveGroup.v`](../../theories/EuclideanAdditiveGroup.v) of 2.7.16 treats the type R^n);
  [`real_integers_closed`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L78): Z is closed. `is_real_integer_quotient_group pi
  qadd qopp` specifies R/Z: pi is a projection onto the space of cosets x + Z
  ([`is_left_coset_space`](../../theories/CosetSpaceUP.v#L23)), and pi(x) + pi(y) = pi(x + y), -pi(x) = pi(-x).
  [`real_integer_quotient_operations_unique`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L126): these laws determine the
  operations. [`real_integer_quotient_group_exists`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L143): such an R/Z exists.
  [`real_quotient_compact_group`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L250): R/Z is a compact Hausdorff, locally compact
  topological group with identity pi(0). [`real_quotient_rationals_subgroup`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L265),
  [`real_quotient_rationals_countable`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L279), [`real_quotient_rationals_infinite`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L292):
  Q/Z = pi[Q] is a countably infinite subgroup.
  [`real_quotient_vitali_nonmeasurable`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L321) and [`vitali_nonmeasurable_set_exists`](../../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L345):
  R/Z has a left Haar measure and a subset meeting each coset of Q/Z in
  exactly one point, which is nonmeasurable with respect to every Haar
  measure of R/Z.

Two regressions. In every group the trivial subgroup {e} is finite, G meets
each coset {g} in exactly one point, and G is measurable for every measure.
In R/Z, for a left Haar measure phi, a transversal of Q/Z is phi
nonmeasurable, while R/Z is a phi measurable transversal of the finite
subgroup {pi(0)}. Hence the hypothesis that S is infinite cannot be omitted.

Sources: Federer 2.7.17, p. 141, with 2.1.1, p. 52, 2.1.2, p. 54, 2.1.3(3), p.
55, 2.5.2–2.5.3, pp. 92–95, 2.5.14, p. 107, 2.5.19, p. 113, 2.7.1–2.7.2, pp.
121–122, 2.7.5, p. 125, and 2.7.12, p. 129. The erratum has no entry for
2.7.17; its entry for p. 141 concerns 2.7.18.

#### Divergences

- *Hypotheses (p. 141).* "G is a compact topological group" is read within
  the standing hypothesis of 2.7.1: G is a locally compact Hausdorff
  topological group, and `compact [set: G]`. Local compactness follows from
  compactness and the Hausdorff property, but it is kept as part of the
  standing hypothesis. "Countably infinite" is countable and infinite. This
  is an interpretation.
- *"Any Haar measure" (p. 141).* The statement is proved for left and for
  right Haar measures. Federer does not say that they coincide on a compact
  group; [`compact_group_haar_measures_coincide`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L131) derives it from the
  unimodularity of compact groups (2.7.12). This step is supplied.
- *Equal measures and measurability of the sets T s (p. 141).* Federer gives
  no argument. The sets T s are right translates, so a right invariant
  measure is needed; for a left Haar measure the previous item supplies it.
  The invariance phi(A_g^{-1}[B]) = phi(B) holds for every subset B and is
  proved from the uniqueness of the K(X) regular measure associated with an
  integral (2.5.3). The image measure under A_g represents A_g#(mu) = mu
  (2.5.19), and it is K(X) regular because A_g is a homeomorphism. Federer
  does not state that regularity is preserved; this lemma is supplied and is
  stated for arbitrary function lattices and mutually inverse maps.
  Measurability of T s follows from the invariance by splitting the test
  sets.
- *"G would have infinite measure" (p. 141).* The countable additivity of
  2.1.3(3) gives phi(G) = sum over s in S of phi(T). Federer treats only the
  case phi(T) > 0. If phi(T) = 0, then phi(G) = 0, which contradicts the
  definition of a Haar measure as a nonzero measure (2.7.2). The finiteness
  0 < phi(G) < infinity is also implicit in Federer. Compactness of G
  (2.5.14) gives finiteness; the nonzero Haar measure gives positivity.
  These steps are supplied.
- *Existence of T (p. 141).* Federer presupposes T. Its existence is proved
  by the axiom of choice ([`left_coset_transversal_exists`](../../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L87)). This is an
  addition.
- *Vitali's example (p. 141).* Federer gives no details. R/Z is specified by
  the projection onto the coset space and the laws of its operations, with
  existence proved by choosing representatives; this is an interpretation of
  "R/Z". The topological group structure and the compactness of R/Z, and the
  countability and infinitude of Q/Z, are supplied. The conclusion concerns
  Haar measures of R/Z, as in 2.7.17; Vitali's original set in R and
  Lebesgue measure on R are not treated.

<a id="item-2-7-18"></a>

### 2.7.18 — Continuity of translation in L_1 of a covariant measure

Federer, p. 141. If gamma is a chi covariant measure over X, v in L_1(gamma)
and h in G, then int |v(gx) - v(hx)| dgamma x -> 0 as G contains g -> h. The
statement is proved, with the erratum's correction of the proof (p. 141,
line 25). As in 2.7.1, gx is `A g x` and u_g is `u \o A g`. L_1(gamma) is
`total_scalar_lp gamma 1` of 2.5.7, the total-function space of the erratum
for p. 98. The integral int |f| dgamma of f in L_1(gamma) is
`finite_total_scalar_lp_norm gamma 1 f`. The limit is the MathComp limit
along the neighbourhood filter of h in G.

- [`CovariantTranslationContinuity.v`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v). [`covariant_translation_continuity`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L212)
  assumes `is_topological_group mul inv e`, `locally_compact [set: G]`,
  `is_homogeneous_space mul A`, `hausdorff_space X` and
  `locally_compact [set: X]`. Let `is_covariant_measure A chi gamma` and
  `total_scalar_lp gamma 1 v` hold. The theorem then gives three things. First,
  v o A_g belongs to L_1(gamma) for every g. Second, int |v(gx) - v(hx)| dgamma x
  equals the L_1 seminorm of v o A_g - v o A_h, by [`is_measure_integral`](../../theories/MeasureIntegralUP.v#L71).
  Third, this seminorm tends to 0 as g -> h. The corollary
  [`covariant_translation_continuity_lch`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L282) states the same result under the
  standing hypotheses of section 2.7: [`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group`](../../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L56),
  together with a locally compact Hausdorff homogeneous space X. The module
  also contains three supporting lemmas.
  [`regular_vector_lattice_lone_dense`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L61) shows that a vector lattice L is dense
  in L_1(phi) for every L regular measure phi that represents a functional on
  L. [`covariant_measure_positive_character`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L117) shows that every chi covariant
  measure is also c covariant for some continuous character c with
  c(g) > 0 for all g. [`compact_translation_lone_small`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L147) shows that
  int |w_g - w_h| dgamma -> 0 for w in K(X).
- [`CovariantMeasureImage.v`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v). `covariant_character_positive` shows that
  chi(g) > 0 if the monotone chi covariant integral is not 0. Let gamma be a
  K(X) regular measure that represents on K(X) a functional mu with
  mu(u o A_g) = chi(g) mu(u), and let chi(g) > 0. Then
  [`covariant_translation_image`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v#L103) gives the measure identity
  A_g#(gamma) = chi(g) gamma. [`covariant_translation_integral`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v#L131) gives
  int u o A_g dgamma = chi(g) int u dgamma for every gamma integrable u.
  [`covariant_translation_lone`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v#L150) shows that v o A_g belongs to L_1(gamma) and
  that int |v o A_g| dgamma = chi(g) int |v| dgamma. Only the group laws and
  [`is_homogeneous_space`](../../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33) are assumed.
- [`RegularPushforwardBijection.v`](../../theories/RegularPushforwardBijection.v). Let p : X -> Y be a bijection with inverse
  q, and let f o q belong to L_Y for every f in L_X. Then
  [`bijection_pushforward_regular`](../../theories/RegularPushforwardBijection.v#L40) shows that p_#(phi) is L_Y regular
  (2.5.3) whenever phi is L_X regular; it is derived from
  [`lattice_regular_inverse_pushforward`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L94) of 2.7.17. Compare
  [`pushforward_need_not_be_regular`](../../theories/DaniellSpaceImageRegularity.v#L60) (2.5.19) for continuous proper maps.
- *Overlaps with earlier items.* `covariant_character_positive` of
  [`CovariantMeasureImage.v`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v) holds on every homogeneous space and contains
  the lemma of the same name in [`CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v`](../../theories/CovariantCompactRightInvariance.v) (2.7.14),
  which treats the action of G on itself; [`covariant_translation_image`](../../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v#L103)
  contains [`invariant_measure_preimage`](../../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L124) of 2.7.17, the case chi = 1. The
  earlier lemmas are kept, since their items precede 2.7.18.
- [`MeasureScaleIntegral.v`](../../theories/MeasureScaleIntegral.v). `measure_scale c phi` is the measure
  S |-> c phi(S). Let c > 0. Then [`measure_scale_integral`](../../theories/MeasureScaleIntegral.v#L216) gives
  int f d(c phi) = c int f dphi, [`measure_scale_represents`](../../theories/MeasureScaleIntegral.v#L226) shows that c phi
  represents c A whenever phi represents A, and [`measure_scale_regular`](../../theories/MeasureScaleIntegral.v#L253) shows
  that c phi is L regular whenever phi is.

One regression. [`zero_measure_translation_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6635) takes the one-point group
acting on itself. There the zero measure is a K(X) regular measure that is
chi covariant for chi = 0, which is not a homomorphism into R ~ {0}. The
theorem still applies.

Sources: Federer 2.7.18, p. 141, and the erratum entry for p. 141, line 25;
2.7.1, p. 122; 2.5.3, p. 94; 2.5.7, p. 98, with the erratum entry for p. 98;
2.5.13, p. 106; 2.5.19, p. 113; 2.4.18(1), p. 90; 2.4.2, p. 81; 2.1.1(6), p.
52.

#### Divergences

- *Limit of the character (p. 141, line 25).* The book prints
  "chi(h) -> chi(g)". It is corrected to "chi(g) -> chi(h)" as in the
  erratum, and it is used as the continuity of the character at h. This is a
  correction.
- *The covariance identity (p. 141).* Federer uses
  int |v - w|_g dgamma = chi(g) int |v - w| dgamma without proof. Checked
  against the covariance law mu(u o A_g) = chi(g) mu(u) of 2.7.1, the
  printed factor chi(g) is correct, not chi(g)^{-1}. The proof is a
  supporting route. 2.5.19 and 2.4.18 show that A_g#(gamma) represents
  chi(g) mu. Regularity transfers through the homeomorphism A_g. The
  uniqueness in 2.5.3 then gives A_g#(gamma) = chi(g) gamma. This needs
  chi(g) > 0, and also integration with respect to the measure
  chi(g) gamma. Neither is stated in the book. Both are proved here
  (`covariant_character_positive`, [`MeasureScaleIntegral.v`](../../theories/MeasureScaleIntegral.v)).
- *The case mu = 0 (p. 141).* Federer's step chi(g) -> chi(h) relies on the
  continuity of chi from 2.7.1. That continuity holds only if the
  associated integral mu is not 0. If mu = 0, chi may be arbitrary, for
  instance not continuous. In that case gamma is also 1 covariant, and the
  argument is applied to the character 1 ([`covariant_measure_positive_character`](../../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L117)).
  This completes the proof in a case the printed argument does not cover. The
  statement itself is unchanged. [`zero_measure_translation_example`](../../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L6635) checks
  this case.
- *Density of K(X) (p. 141).* "We can choose w in K(X) with
  int |v - w| dgamma < epsilon" is taken from 2.5.3. The library states that
  density for nonnegative functions
  ([`regular_positive_lattice_lone_dense`](../../theories/RegularLatticeLoneDensity.v#L34)). It is applied to max(v,0) and
  max(-v,0). This is a different proof route.
- *Estimate (p. 141).* The pointwise inequality
  |v_g - v_h| <= |w_g - w_h| + |v - w|_g + |v - w|_h, integrated, is replaced
  by the triangle inequality of the L_1 seminorm. The two are equivalent.
- *"spt w is compact and w is uniformly continuous" (p. 141).* No uniform
  structure on X is used. Let N be a compact neighbourhood of h. For g in N,
  w_g - w_h vanishes outside the compact set
  {A(k^{-1}, y) : k in N, y in spt w}. The map (x,g) |-> w(A(g,x)) is
  uniformly continuous in g on that set. The bound of 2.5.13 for Daniell
  integrals of functions vanishing outside a fixed compact set gives
  mu(|w_g - w_h|) -> 0. This follows the proof of the continuity of chi in
  [`CovariantCharacterContinuity.v`](../../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v). It interprets Federer's one-line
  justification.
- *Hypotheses and statement (p. 141).* The main theorem assumes only the
  standing hypotheses it uses. In particular, G need not be Hausdorff; the
  corollary restates the result under all the standing hypotheses. The
  conclusion also states that v o A_g lies in L_1(gamma) and identifies the
  integral with the L_1 seminorm. Federer leaves both implicit. This is an
  interpretation and a mild generalisation.
- *L_1(gamma) (p. 141).* L_1(gamma) is read as the space of total real
  functions of 2.5.7, following the erratum for p. 98. Partially defined or
  extended-real functions are not admitted. This is an interpretation.
